**PART I**

**Chapter 1**

***6:00 AM 14<sup>th</sup> February 2010, Girbin, Scotland***

The streetlights in a faraway old Scottish town were making way to the early morning sunshine, as the sunbeam was forming a tincture passing through the morning haar. The bright dawn sun was camouflaging in the cold wintery brume as if this was the game it had chosen to play. The birds roused from their repose tweeting with verve, and the gentle breeze making the chirping fade into some melodious hum. No matter how serene it appeared, it had that uneasiness, the eerie that was giving signs of an unwelcome morning. This was well endorsed by the people who preferred their shelters than the road.  Amidst such nature’s caper, a Range Rover was slowly rolling down the road. It was black in color with dark glass shades, and it appeared like a beast wanting to tame the wilderness of the street. The car came to a full stop few feet away from the town square. The town square was the only attraction this little hamlet had. Besides the farms where people gathered for merrymaking and fun - the shops, the church, the wooden benches, everything that the town needed was around the town square. The car remained parked with utmost silence, standing there discreetly, blending with the surroundings almost appearing like a time-tested landmark.

The morning coldness couldn’t hold the small-town dwellers from their routine. The shopkeepers opened the store, the ringer pulled the rope of the church bell, and the streets soon got crowded with the Sunday mass goers. The town was slowly waking up from its transient repose. All this while, the Land Rover held to its spot as if it had some long-standing monumental significance.

For Daniel Roberto, it was the special day of the week when he spent time with his family. His family was everything for him, and he ensured to make every minute of the Sunday special for them. For him, every Sunday began by running to the local bakery to get his daughter’s favorite freshly made muffins. He would cook the specials. Brewing black coffee with select beans crushed at home, scrambled eggs, peanut butter sandwiches, and freshly squeezed orange juice. He puts the spread on the table before waking everyone. His daughter always woke along with him but pretended to be asleep, as she loved being roused by her father. Her father hardly disappointed her, and never missed any chance to treat her like a princess. This tradition had continued ever since she remembered, from her childhood days to this day in her early teens. After breakfast, the family visited the local church. Weather permitting, they usually planned some outdoor recreational activities as well. As a family, they cheered for each other, celebrated every little joy, and lived contented. He had a tough job as a deliveryman, often working long hours, and Sunday was his way to thank his family for being there for him.

This Sunday was no different. Besides the cold weather, everything else was aligned with his expectations. Bright sun, resting family, and the dewiness in the air, a perfect Sunday as he wanted it to be. Following his routine, he stepped out of the house and headed to the bakery. His daughter crawled out of the bed and dashed to the window. The droplet of coldness had taken a half ice half water formation on the windowsill, battling the variance in temperature between the heated room and outside coldness. She grabbed her arm sleeves, stretched the cloth further out and used it as a perfect tool for cleaning the haze. As the window gave way to the outside view, she saw her father sprinting out humming his favorite song. A sight no matter how many times she had seen never failed to cease her excitement. She stood there, like always, waiting for her father to dissolve in the morning mist. Soon he disappeared, and she dashed back to her bed pretending to be asleep again. There was a strong rumor about both father and daughter playing this little game, but they pretended unaware of each other’s ruses for reasons best known to them.

While the Roberto family was expecting this to be yet another Sunday of love and bonding little they knew that this Sunday would change their lives forever. Change for the worse.

 

**\**

**Chapter 2**

***7:00 PM 12<sup>th</sup> April 2014, Stockholm, Denmark***

The streets were hustled with people, little more than the usually quiet streets of this small Danish town. It was a cold spring evening, and the burg was engulfed in layers of some uncalled mist. A sudden gush of cold air surged past, as a fast-approaching limousine curbed around the cobbled pavement to reach the entrance of the hotel located at the corner of the street. It was an elegant palace built in the 16th century, now remodeled as a luxury grandiose. The hotel had some historical significance and the best the town offered to the elite class.

The concierge sprinted to action. A valet reached for the door, and another one approached the driver to get the car keys. From behind the dark tinted glasses of the car appeared a visibly tall Caucasian male. He was over six feet tall and was sharply dressed in a cobalt blue Versace suit. His eyes were hidden behind the dark shades and his chiseled shape face refused to give any expression away. Two well-built men surrounded him, and they briskly walked towards the hotel lobby. The entourage moved left from the entrance approaching the executive bank of elevators.  Soon they vanished behind the lift doors like vapor dissolving in air.

Diagonally opposite to the elevators was a coffee shop where a petite looking girl was sipping her rich Danish coffee. She was in her late teens and was looking little out of place in her gorgeous black evening gown. Her back was exposed to the deep and legs covered by the fringes of the dress. The gown was snugging well across her slim waist and her full breasts were tantalizing with the top slightly covered by her turquoise blue scarf.  Her brown long hair was tied in a knot, and her radiant face could make anyone go weak on knees. From time to time she craned her neck towards the lobby, her eyes in constant search for someone's arrival. She was anxious but wouldn't show, hiding her apprehensions behind the gentle sips of her evening coffee. Her wait was soon curtailed.  The man she was waiting for was walking towards her. He swiftly went past her to enter the elevator bank. The day she had waited for long had finally arrived. Everything was planned except for one small glitch - she didn't have a well-thought strategy to get introduced to him.

During this time every year, he visited this place to take part in the annual poker tournament. The only place where she had hoped to initiate any conversation with him. Preoccupied in her quandaries, she sauntered towards the casino. She took a deep breath, shook her anxieties away, and approached the poker room. This side of the casino had a distinct air of richness. Large chandeliers were dangling from the foyer and the golden pale dim lit gave a distinct spark to the exquisite 16th-century paintings glued to the walls. Two tall sturdy sentinels guarded the entrance to the secluded area. With confidence in her stride, she walked towards the entrance. "Hej!" greeted one of the guards in the local Danish way. She glanced at him with smiling eyes and continued walking. One of the guards raised his hand to stop her and asked, "May I see your membership card please."

She still carried her infectious smile and stated she is new to the town and visiting the casino for the first time. "I have the money", were her concluding words. The guard politely refused and declared the place to be a member only arcade. From the corner of her eyes, she saw the man she was waiting for walking towards her and she purposely escalated her voice to get his attention, "I want to see the manager. Is this how you treat a lady?" Before the guards could react, she launched her next assault with an even further escalated tone, "where is it written that only members are allowed?"

By now he was next to her. He was sharply garbed in dress pants and a buttoned-down shirt with a tux aptly fitting his torso. The guards gave way for him to enter the casino. As he was passing through the tall doors he sensed a gentle tap on his shoulders. He turned around and saw a girl greeting him with a radiant smile. She looked straight into his eyes and continued, "You seem to be popular. Can you put these miserable souls in place? They are rude and discriminating."

He looked at her with interest. Her eyes were inviting, and her innocent face revealed frustration and despair. There was something in her that was noble, which urged him to know more about her. And in his most uncanny way, he uttered, "She is with me" and led her to the elite poker room.

The room was circular in appearance. There was one large mahogany table in the center with sitting arrangement for five players. The dealer covered one side of the table facing the dark velvet wall. Three seats were right across him and one on each side of the table. The room was dimly lit, and the walls were humming Mozart's "The Marriage of Figaro". On the far-right side of the table was the bar with an elaborated collection of wine, single malts, and some of the most exotic liquors from around the world, arranged in an exquisitely designed temperature controlled wooden cellar. The elite guests were leisurely arriving and taking their designated seats across the table.

"I deeply appreciate your kind gesture" She took the initiative and extended her hand, and with a lit face continued, "My name is Rita."

He gave her hand a gentle grip and reciprocated introducing himself in a brief syllable "Benedikt", and like any gentleman would do, he continued enunciating his words with a distinct deep voice, "the seats are limited to members only. Please go ahead and take mine for tonight's game."

She was waiting for a moment like this. In split second she turned towards him, with a pair of inviting eyes she stretched to reach for his ears and whispered in a hushed tone, "I appreciate the gesture. For tonight, let me be your lady luck."

Benedikt’s curiosity had increased multi-fold. He was enamored by her charm but wouldn’t let anything reveal. With slightly parted lips his voice trailed "pleasure is mine". He waved his hands to the keeper and instructed him to arrange the seating for the lady.  The keeper sprinted to action, got an additional armchair and placed it next to Benedikt.

The high-stakes poker game had some rules. Each of the five players was required to bring 10M Pounds to the table and no one could leave until one of them would be declared the winner. Benedikt had a decent record. He had won five out of the last seven outings and was clearly the favorite. He was known for bold moves, and in making his opponents shudder with his signature all-in stakes. During the play, he never gave anything away and donned a poker face regardless of wins or losses. The En Welch clock rhymed, welcoming the evening’s 9th hour. It was the official time to commence the annual poker game.

The dealer showed two fresh card decks sealed in cellophane wrap, which he removed in front of the captive audience. The first pack of the card was dealt, and Robinson, who was sitting to the left of the dealer, did the honor of cutting the deck. The players created the initial pot by putting a couple of white chips into the ante. Right after that, the dealer distributed five cards to each player.

The first blind betting was initiated. Robinson started with a hundred thousand bets, which was equally met by the next two players. When Benedikt turn came, he raised the bet to two hundred thousand. Sitting next to Benedikt was Holger. The increased bet amount didn’t go well with him, which was quite evident in his fidgety stature. However, he avoided calling it quits so early in the game, and though hesitant, he went ahead and matched the bet. The dealer waved over the pot indicating that players may look at their cards.  The girl, who introduced herself as Rita, was observing the players with great interest hoping to read their minds, as they peered into their share of cards. None of them gave anything away. No smile, no widening of eyes or a trickle of sweat, not a shade of any slipping hint. Sitting in a live high-stakes poker game for the first time, she understood impassiveness was the unwarranted protocol of the game. However, she kept her hope alive of finding some cue so that she could use it to impress Benedikt.

The second round of betting started. Robinson put forward two hundred thousand to stay in the game. Sitting left to Robinson was Andrea, who after seeing her cards decided to fold. Alberto was next who matched the stake. It was Benedikt’s turn; he pushed forward chips worth million Pounds raising the stake by eight hundred thousand Pounds! He, like before, was inscrutable and it was hard to tell whether he had good cards or simply put, bluffing. Holger was next. Like Andrea, he decided to avoid the agony of losing and dropped out of the game.

Rita noted a slight change in Alberto’s stance, as he moved to a cross leg position and was rotating a chip between his fingers. The waitress was making rounds with the drinks. It seemed she knew her audience well. She got vodka with two olives on the side for Benedikt. The waitress looked at Rita with interest.

"What would you like to have?"

The waitress eyes were infusing an exquisite smile, and before Rita could respond, Benedikt looked through her and with a possessive voice queried, "May I?"

And without waiting for her affirmation he ordered, "Krupnik with vodka for the lady" his gaze still piercing her.

"Coming right way" declared the waitress and sprinted to action.

She sure was impressed, but she reminded herself that she was there for a mission, not to get carried away in some infatuated foolishness. "I am sure this must be the best and I would trust your choice" her eyes flirted as she glanced at him with her mystified smile.

"It is a Lithuania delight and I am sure it will suit you well,” Benedikt stated, his gaze not falling, still looking through her. For once that evening, she knew that the executive floor wasn’t too far from her reach now.

With the pot size nearing two million, it was now Robinson’s turn to fold. There were two players left Alberto and Benedikt. Alberto raised the stake to one million and two hundred thousand Pounds. Rita was keenly observing Alberto as he continued to rotate the chip between his fingers. She got little startled when the waitress broke her concentration "your drink dames!!”

She couldn’t resist anymore. She bent a little to come close to Benedikt’s ears and hushed, "I think he is bluffing". Benedikt hardly showed any reaction. And after a brief pause, he increased the stake by two million Pounds. The game was getting intense, and Alberto hardly disappointed the audience, he matched the stake, and it was time for the final showdown. Alberto opened his first two cards, 7 of spades and 10 of spades. The dealer put the cards in order, unfolding the suspense for the inquisitive audience. Now it was Benedikt’s turn to open his two cards. "Two aces" announced the dealer, as he laid them straight facing the players. The audience was getting desirous to see the remaining cards. Alberto opened the rest of the three cards and took the game away. Benedikt had 8 and 9 of spades and Alberto had a straight flush. Alberto finally grinned showing his yellow tinted teeth and collected the pot.

Rita was embarrassed with her puerile guessing. Though uncomfortable she mustered some courage, and with a muffled tone stated, "I am truly sorry for my infantile behavior, and I am sorry for your loss."

Benedikt for the first time that evening had a thin smile on his lips. He looked through her and gently asserted, "don't be sorry it wasn’t your fault,” he continued with a frivolous tone, "and never cease giving your views. I like when someone put their mind to work, especially like me".

Rita was puzzled. She couldn’t figure out whether it was a compliment or a sarcastic remark. She reciprocated with an edgy smile, and all the confidence she had gained that evening seemed to fade away, leaving her in a nervous and dazed state.

The dealer announced a fifteen-minute break before resuming the next session. "I have to make an important call. I will be back in ten minutes.” Benedikt announced and left her in the room alone. Seeing Benedikt fading away, Alberto made the advances, "never seen Benedikt with a lady before. Guess he never needed one". He was self-amused and blurted a chuckle.

With a smile on her face, she responded, "the game is still on, and who would know better than a pro like you, it takes one good hand to change the tide." She stretched her hand and declared, "name is Rita."

Alberto with some awkwardness took her hand, introduced himself and swiftly skirted away.

As promised, Benedikt came back in less than ten minutes. The players exchanged some pleasantries and settled down for the next round. All players put an initial ante forward. The dealer shuffled a new pair of decks. This time Benedikt cut the deck, and the cards got distributed. The dealer announced the first blind call. Alberto started with five hundred-thousand-pound blind, which was equally honored by everyone. Alberto raised his hand for a second blind and raised the pot to one million Pounds. Though not apparent, one could see slight shades of hesitation on some of the player’s faces. Regardless, everyone obliged, and the pot was standing at 7.5 Million pounds. It was time for everyone to look at the cards. Alberto, after seeing his cards raised the stake to two million.

"Looks like it's Alberto's day today." That’s how Rita felt observing the current proceedings. With dwindled hope, she kept watching Alberto. Unlike last time he hadn’t crossed his legs, but he still took a chip and started rotating between his fingers. Her mind was racing; it must be a cue but couldn’t figure out anything. It was Benedikt’s turn and he surprised everyone by raising the stakes to two and a half million Pounds. Holger was already shaking his head and called it quits. Robinson followed the suit as well. Andrea picked her cards for the second time and after pondering for a while she raised the stake by three million Pounds. Rita knew Andrea had good cards. However, it hardly deterred Alberto from raising the stake by three and a half million. The game was becoming fierce, and it was almost sure most of them would be out of the tournament soon.

Alberto moved his sitting posture, and Rita got the cue she was looking for. Though futile, yet she observed two things, which was different from before. First, he crossed his leg only after seeing his cards, unlike last time when he crossed the leg from the beginning. Also, this time he crossed his left leg over his right. She couldn’t resist herself and succumbed to her intuition. Moreover, her contention was Benedikt wanted her to speak her mind.

"I am sure it is a half bluff. He has the cards, but not high in the order". Benedikt refrained from uttering any word, but his action brought both, a sense of achievement, and a surge of nervousness to her. He went with an all-in stake. She couldn’t believe her eyes; high stake money was rolling in with grit. Andrea couldn’t dare to go any further and saved her remaining chips for the next game. The battle was again between Alberto and Benedikt. Alberto decided to go with an all-in, forcing Benedikt to show his cards. Everyone held their breath as the pile was over whopping thirty-five million Pounds!!

Benedikt opened the first two cards, five of hearts and seven of clubs. Alberto smiled and put forward his two kings. Now it was Benedikt’s turn. All eyes were on him. He revealed his next three cards as slowly as he could, raising the mercury to the height of insanity. He had a six of clubs, eight of diamonds and nine of hearts, it was straight! The entire attention shifted to Alberto. Could he pull the game? He appeared pale and his visage flinched. All he had was one more king and a pair of a queen, not enough to pull the game away from Benedikt.

With other players’ significantly down and Alberto out of the game, Benedikt was heading towards a clear win over others. Like always he gave nothing away. The dealer called the game off for the night only to be reconvened tomorrow. Benedikt left his seat, passed a frail smile to his fellow contestants and dashed out of the room with his prized possession, Rita.

"It was a long night. May I offer you a drink at my place?" there was a clear hint of curiosity in his demeanor as he put forth his suggestion.

Rita couldn’t believe her ears. It was like pieces of a puzzle coming together. Keeping her excitement under control she rejoinders with a distinct smile, "I would love to."

As soon as they left the room the two escorts magically appeared and guided them to the executive elevator bank. They took the elevators to the 15<sup>th</sup> floor. The high-speed elevator was moving with extreme velocity, so were her emotions. The last four years of her meticulous planning and her painful arduous wait was about to take the first step. Her emotions were getting the best of her. She was disconcerted, confounded, and was trying hard to conceal it from the prying eyes of Benedikt.

**Chapter 3**

***11:00 PM 12<sup>th</sup> April 2014, Stockholm, Denmark***

The Elevator opened to a grandeur hallway. The corridor was laced with cameras covering every inch of the hallway. There were men in dark suits guarding the passage like a king's castle. The aura of the place was overwhelming, making Rita visibly nervous. A trickle of sweat appeared on her temple and she felt the thumping of her heart against her chest. Benedikt put a cursory glance at her and observed her visage turning pale. He gently squeezed her hand and with a hushed voice gave his assurance, "Don’t get intimidated. They are for our safety". She smiled sheepishly and appeared perturbed. The bewilderment worked to her advantage, as Benedikt hardly consider her any threat.

The hallway led to a large living room. The place was grand, like a Roman palace with tall ceilings and pointed Arch. The room was parted into two sections separated by raised stairs. The seating place was vast with spread out sofas, decorative sculptures, and paintings, all meticulously arranged in a tasteful setup. From the door, she could also see the bedroom, which was extending from the living room to the furthest corner of the floor. Benedikt called one of his accomplices and whispered something barely audible to her. The black-suited person sprang into action, and soon all guards vanished behind the closed doors.

There she was in Benedikt’s room all by herself. For the past two years, she had visualized being here multiple time. All her wait was finally taking a stride forward. Her brooding was rattled by Benedikt’s grating voice, "do you have a preference, or shall I get you one of my favorites". She smiled and responded with equal vigor "surprise me."

He went to the bar and made the drink with amazing barmanship skills.  "Here is your between-the-sheets drink. Hope you like it." The name itself had sensuousness written all over it. Gently he settled down with his drink. They were intensely looking at each other and before she could comprehend, he slowly leaned forward and caressed her lips. The alluring kiss led to some passionate verve and soon their tongues were exploring each other's mouth. There was hunger, filthiness, passion, and desire. He lifted her off her feet and led her to the bedroom. She was taken by surprise. She knew something similar would have happened, but not at the rate and the pace. She had a plan and she couldn’t afford to ruin it in some silly lovemaking episode. With a lot of reluctance, she pushed him away, first gently then with some force. His emotions were a mix of dejection and surprise, but like a gentleman, he parted. His hair ruffled, and his state disheveled.

She blurted with an ungodly smirk on her face, "I am sorry. I didn’t mean to be rude, but I need to use the bathroom and if possible change."

Benedikt despair soon turned into a harmless chuckle and he led her to the bathroom. He winked, as he pointed to the wardrobe and declared charmingly, "Here is my robe. The best I can offer."

She locked the bathroom door and carefully took off her gown. The dress had her escape material stitched into the long inside sleeves. She wanted to secure the belongings before heading back to the room. With extreme caution, she stashed her gown in the cabinet below the sink, slipped into the robe and came back to him in a jiffy. He was still working on his vodka and was looking away toward the window.  He was taken by surprise as she locked her arm around his neck. His strong arms swung her around and made her fell on his lap. He ogled her for few seconds. Long enough to arouse her. With passion and lust, his tongue started exploring her body inch by inch. The robe and the clothes disappeared, and they turned to making some passionate love. She hadn’t experienced anything like this before. Her trepidations slowly melted away, and she submitted herself to his unfathomable desires. They made love again and again like there was no tomorrow.

It was past 2:00 AM when she roused from her transient repose. Benedikt was sound asleep. The sedative lotion she applied on him worked. She discreetly moved, ensuring there was no rumble to put her in misery, and tiptoed to the bathroom. Hidden in the gown were a body-hugging suit and a pair of gloves. She slipped into the suit, wore the hand gloves to avoid leaving behind her handprints and picked the briefcase lying by the bedside. With the help of a hairpin, she tried to open it. It didn’t buzz. She tried again, but nothing happened. Her time was running out, she had to get out of the place before the break of the dawn. She gave it one final push and with a gentle click the briefcase sprung open.

There it was, the small telescopic cylinder. She opened it and found a piece of paper carefully wrapped inside it. She took her phone out of the purse, ensured the silence mode was on and took multiple pictures of the paper. She gave a quick glance at the pictures. Satisfied with her captures, she carefully inserted the paper into the cylinder and placed it back in the briefcase. There was one more job left before she could escape the room. She searched the room for Benedikt’s wallet. After some combing, she found it in the bedside drawer. She took all the cash, with the hope that missing cash would misguide the future investigation, gave one final look at Benedikt and dashed towards the bathroom.

Her gown was specially designed for her escape. Besides the suit, gloves and the sedative lotion, she also had a glasscutter as part of her accessories. She tore open the rifts between the gown fringes and scooped out the tiny battery-operated cutter. She made sure all the doors were closed to curtail any noise and diligently started executing her job. In about fifteen minutes the glass was off the window. She had earlier checked into a room two floors below from Benedikt's room. She had paid a premium for the corner suite as the room had a downtown facing view. She wrapped all her left-over and put in the drawer underneath the faucets and it was time to evade.

She cramped out of the cut-out window outlet. It was 2:20 AM in the morning and was pitch dark outside. Not a soul was visible. She put her foot on the frame block of the window. Earlier in the day she had put a rope with an anchor a floor above her room. The anchor was hidden behind the wall alongside the drainpipe, which was running from the top of the roof to the ground. She had to climb down one floor to get its access. The hotel had a castle-like appearance with gothic structures, which was working to her advantage. Using the hinges of the building frame she was descending with careful steps. Once she was close to the drainpipe, she moved sideways, resting her foot with extreme caution on the pipe's ring. With utmost care, she took the excruciating journey down, one step at a time.

"Creek, creek!!" Loud cacophony broke the otherwise silent monotony of the night. She lost her left foot grip and slipped, only to get hold of the hinge of a bulging shell. Her heart leaped into her mouth. She never expected in her wildest dream to hear some creepy noise in such ungodly hour. For few seconds, she had no clue what had hit her. And then she heard the flutter of avian species moving away in agony. Unknowingly she had invaded their privacy. She was hanging on the hinge and was in a desperate need for a foothold. The guest in the room right below her switched on the light and peered outside, and to her advantage, the flutter of the birds erased any apprehension he may had and made him go back to sleep. As the light was sipping out of the window, she saw a frill edge connecting the drainpipe to the brick wall towards the right of her dangling foot. She knew if she couldn’t get onto the edge, the hinge would soon give away the resistance. She swung her feet and managed to get a grip on the drainpipe. With caution, she shifted her body weight to the newfound shelter. Her journey was one floor down from there. She found the rope that she had tied earlier in the day and slipped down the cord to her room's window. Once inside she pulled the rope in and closed the window behind her. The "Don’t disturb sign worked like a charm" she mumbled after finding her room untouched by the housekeepers. She was perspiring head to toe. Though she would have loved to take a shower, she refrained. She was running out of time as soon the sedative on Benedikt would start to fade away.

She changed to a pair of jeans and a pullover hood. Picked her bag, which she had packed the night before, and dashed out of the room. The lobby was without a soul.  To her benefit, the receptionist was stealing an early morning nap in the adjacent office room. She found a lone bellman in the lobby, offered him ten Kronor and asked him to get her a cab. She didn't have to wait long for the taxi to arrive. Once inside the taxi, she instructed the driver to take her to the Sofitel hotel. From Sofitel, she took another taxi to the main market, and from there rode the tram to the railway junction.

"When is the next train to Copenhagen?" she almost rattled the man behind the glass wall who was half asleep. He looked at the watch and with a groggier voice announced, “In twenty minutes from platform F.”

She took a business class ticket and boarded the train. The train took about forty minutes to arrive at Copenhagen. From Copenhagen, she bought tickets to go to Basel. The train to Basel was leaving in half hour, she had enough time to make a phone call.

She approached the public telephone booth at the station platform and dialed a number. After few rings, a person with a gruff voice snapped, “Who is it?” she introduced herself and continued her conversation with an embodied tone, “I have the map. Can you call Adriana and confirm my arrival next week? I am calling from a pay booth, will call you back in fifteen minutes.”

After fifteen minutes, she called back. The man responded with an affirmation “She will be waiting for you. Good luck.” She thanked him and took a sigh of relief. After an hour-long wait, the train to Basel approached the platform. It was the first train out in the morning. She took a corner seat and with the bag on her lap and arms around it, she drifted off to sleep.

**Chapter 4**

***12:00 AM 12<sup>th</sup> April 2014, Stockholm, Denmark***

A man dressed in a black tracksuit and a windshield jacket was bravely fighting the biting cold in the open backyard of the hotel. His dark getup camouflaged well with the night's darkness and he was almost invisible unless someone would be within few yards of his proximity. The temperature had stooped to single digits, and he had no respite from the gelid airs, but his clothes. Though he was dressed for the season, he couldn’t withstand the frigid brutality without the occasional shivers.

He was waiting for a girl to appear from the executive floor’s badested ridge. He knew the layout of the floor well and was anticipating her arrival any moment from the windowsill. While he was certain of her breakout route, what he was unsure was the time when it would happen. There was also a thin chance of her being caught. In such situation, he had to execute his alternate plan. But for now, it was a long agonizing wait.

A soft tinkling sound brought his senses to alert. It was a weird soft thrum, hard to notice unless someone was expecting it. He wasn’t sure of its origin but was almost certain who would have caused it.

Then he saw her shadow dangling by the hinge. He was puzzled, not sure what he should do. Before he could conclude anything meaningful, he saw her finding the much-needed support. Soon she was on her way into her room through the open shaft of the French window. He was relieved to see her escaping the mishap unscathed. The moment she disappeared behind the lancet; he scurried to the lobby area. He stood outside, little further away from the entrance. Taking the darkness as the shield, he waited for her to come to sight. His wait wasn’t for long. Soon he saw her sprinting from the front lobby to the concierge area. He witnessed her handing the bellman some Kroners, requesting him to get a taxi. Soon the taxi arrived, and she slipped into the ghostly night with no trace left behind. The man jumped into the taxa he had kept for the night and informed the driver to follow along.

He traced her meandering ways to the train station following her all the way to Copenhagen. He trailed her like a shadow and boarded the same train she took to Basel. He settled himself in the booth next to the door and for the first time that night, he closed his eyes to get some much-needed rest.

**Chapter 5**

***4:00 AM 13<sup>th</sup> April 2014, Zurich, Switzerland***

The train came to a sudden stop, jolting away her transient sleep. She wielded from her repose, struggling to break away from the ordeal she was going through. No matter how much she wanted to avoid the painful nightmares, it kept reappearing with a surged vengeance. The train was pacing slowly through the early morning darkness. The lone bulb in the coach was fighting hard to cast the yellow dim shadows in the ghostly darkness. She rubbed her eyes to get a clearer vision and tried hard to read the dials on her wristwatch. It was 20 minutes past four in the morning. Her destination was just a few minutes away. The train rocked, blew a long whistle and came to a full stop with a shrill cranking noise. She cursed herself for sleeping long. Had it not for the train’s obnoxious thrust she would have missed the station.

Grabbing her bag, she slipped out of the train and headed towards the north exit. She pulled the cellphone out of her jacket and called a number, “I will be out in five minutes” she whispered and ended the call. As she walked past the platform she looked around for any suspicious character but found none. There were few people walking towards the exit, but they all appeared regular commuters, nothing unusual for her to worry, and she continued her walk towards the exit. 

Despite her alertness, she missed noticing the shadow that was following her from Denmark. He blended well with the commuters at large and followed her with extreme caution.

There was a BMW 5 series sedan waiting for her at the exit with its engine running ready to leap. Without wasting any time, she disappeared behind the passenger side front car door. The shadow following her called for a cab and discreetly followed the BMW.

She smiled and looked at the person behind the wheels and announced, “Mission successfully accomplished”. The person driving the car appeared not that pleased. With a concerned look, he blurted, “I have a hunch, we are being followed.”

She looked back and noticed a grey Peugeot SUV steadily following them with a well-maintained distance. Though it appeared harmless, there was something about the car that made her ambivalent. Her mind raced hard to make sense of the situation, but her brain froze, and she failed to comprehend anything meaningful.

**Chapter 6**

***5:00 AM 13<sup>th</sup> April 2014, Stockholm, Denmark***

Benedikt aroused from his disheveled state with a dreadful headache. The room was draped in darkness, and it took him a while to adjust to the gloomed surroundings. He stretched his hands looking for Rita but instead encountered some crumbled bedsheets. His nerves were pulsating with such vigor that he thought his head would blow off. Searching the bedside lamp switch, he moved sideways and after some fumbling managed to turn the lights on. The room was filled with brightness, making him shield his eyes to avoid the bright glare. With reluctant steps, he headed to the bathroom. The splash of cold water pierced like shrapnel but provided him the much-needed relief from his irritant self. He opened the shelf to get the aspirin bottle, took two pills and gulped down with the faucet water. With aspirins in blood, he felt a lot better than before.  As he turned towards the towel rack he saw the cutout window pane neatly placed by the Jacuzzi. There were way too many things for him to comprehend. The thought of the briefcase suddenly crossed his mind. He dashed to the bedroom and to his relief he found the bag at the exact same location as he had left the night before. He put the number combination and the briefcase flung open. By the look, everything appeared untouched. He opened the cylindrical case and pulled the paper out. The paper appeared original without no trace of tampering.  Without wasting any further time, he called his head of security.

The security head appeared perplexed and stood at attention, waiting for Benedikt’s instructions.

“Have you seen the girl?”

The man looked bewildered and with a timid voice stated, “Not that I am aware of. Let me check.” He pulled out his transmitter and started talking to someone.

Benedikt was furious. How could a girl make such a big mockery of him? Despite the security, the stealth access, and all the measures, she wandered into his bedroom, accomplished what she intended to, and vanished without any trace. His migraine was creeping back, adding fuel to his anger.

“How could she disappear with dozens of you around here? What do I pay you all for? I want her whereabouts in next thirty minutes? Damian, I want you to trace her and bring to me. If you fail, don't bother to come back. Am I clear?”

Damian was sweating profusely. He nodded and dashed out of the room in haste.

Benedikt was puzzled by the girl’s motive. He looked around the room, checked the briefcase, living room and the closet, nothing was missing. Besides the wallet, he had checked everything else he could think of. He pulled it out from the drawer and found all cash missing. The amount was meager, about 500 Kronor and few 100 US dollars. He was baffled and flustered. “Which sane girl would risk her life to get some insignificant sum of money, jump out of the 15<sup>th</sup> floor, and take the challenge to invade a decent security web?”

It made no sense to him unless she took pleasure stealing from affluent people. The more his mind tried reasoning, the more frustrated he became. The only respite was the aspirins. Demurred by the chaos he tried calming his mind but failed. Frustrated, he called Damian and instructed him to get a doctor. He wanted to find whether he was drugged. Soon Damian came along with the doctor. The doctor took some blood sample for the test, checked his vitals, and advised him to repeat the aspirins after every four hours.  After the doctor left, Benedikt asked Damian about what he had found so far. The only person who had seen the girl since she disappeared was the Bellman. And according to the bellman, the girl left around 2:30 AM in a taxi. He told Benedikt, his men are on the job to find the driver. 

Benedikt was a tad calmer than before. With a gravelly voice, he said. “My money is missing from the wallet. I have a hunch she didn’t come for the money. She had a bigger motive. This girl is smart. Let us not underestimate her intent.”

He sat on the reclining chair with his fingers moving over his forehead, making a futile attempt to get some temporary relief from the nagging pain, and continued his monologue “This is a disguise, a trap to make us believe she was here for the money, but I am not a fool to get winded in her deception. We need to be pragmatic in our approach if we are serious about nabbing her. I am still not sure of her motive and the only way we can find anything about her plan is by finding her. We need to be quick. The longer we take the harder it will be to get our hands on her.”

He took few sips of water and continued, “Here is what you need to do. First, find everything about her. When she checked in, when was the last time she was here, anyone she met during her stay, the room she stayed in. I want every minute detail that concerns her. Is that clear.”

“Yes”, was all Damian managed to speak.

Benedikt continued, “Next I want to talk to the taxi driver. I don’t care where he is. Go and find him. I also want all hallway, lobby and the poker room camera recordings. Get a picture from the recording and do a scan to find any information about her. Report back in twenty minutes. Will you?”

"Alex is on the job. I have tasked him to collect the tapes. I will ask him to run a scan on the girl as well." Damian blurted and rushed out of the room to work on the remaining tasks.

**Chapter 7**

***6:00 AM 13<sup>th</sup> April 2014, Stockholm, Denmark***

Damian called his second in command Russ and tasked him to get Rita’s registration details from the hotel manager. He also instructed him to inform Alex to get all camera recordings for the last 72 hours. After putting his team on the job, he rushed to the concierge area to trace the cab driver.

The hotel security team was active and vigilant covering every exit from the place. Benedikt was an important customer and an influential person. They knew he possessed enough power to ruin the hotel reputation. And if he decides to do so, it would take a long time to mend the damages.  Seeing the head of Benedikt's security, the manager on duty dashed towards him and assured all cooperation. He wasn’t sure what the girl had done to upset Benedikt, and despite his curiosity, he couldn’t muster enough courage to impose the question. Damian instructed the hotel manager to get the details of the taxi that took Rita out of the hotel. It was an easy task, as at that time of the morning there would be hardly any other guest looking for a ride. Besides, as part of the guest security protocol, the details of all taxis transporting guest would be noted by the bellman.

The name of the cab driver was Curz. Damian scanned the record book, and without looking away from the register imposed his question "where can I find him?"

"He usually drives at night and must be sleeping now. I tried his number, but he isn’t answering."

"Do you know where he lives?" Damian gravelly tone showed clear frustration.

"I don’t, but the other drivers may know. Let me ask them." He left and soon returned with a person who knew where Cruz lived. Without wasting any time Damian commanded one of his men to get him.

In twenty minutes, he was back in Benedikt's living room. Benedikt was on the phone talking to someone and was pacing across the room. He gestured Damian to set up the camera recordings and he continued with the call. Damian pulled out the CD, put it in the player, changed the television to AV mode, and waited for Benedikt to finish his call.

"So, what is the status?"

Damian almost jumped out of his skin. He didn’t expect him to come out of his call that soon. He recovered from his lost stature and continued.

"Rita checked into the hotel three days back. The address she left in the records was not real, as we couldn’t find any street in Copenhagen for the name she provided. She was always seen alone, no friends, no meetings with anyone. What baffled me the most was her request for a room two floor below this executive floor. According to the manager on duty, the request was made on the pretext of having the downtown view. Also, she came to this place twice before, both times during the annual poker tournament, and she always stayed in the same room."

Benedikt was listening to every minute detail with great interest. The slight benefit of the doubt he was shedding on Rita had now vanished. He was certain it was a well thought out intrusion, planned with extreme measures and executed with brilliance. What he was unable to fathom was her motive. Except for the money, not a single object from his suite was out of place. The fingerprint report was also negative. While Benedikt was still deep into his brooding, Damian continued to finish his findings "we should also have the cab driver any moment. Would you like to see the recordings in the meantime?"

Benedikt agreed and Damian played the video. It was over three days of recording covering the hallway, lobby, and the poker room area. Benedikt was keen to see the poker room recording first, around the time when he was out for a call. He fast-forwarded the video to play the poker room portion of the video. There was nothing out of the ordinary that looked devious. He saw how Alberto approached her and the way she dour him. He carried a wry smile and admired the lady for her witty response. Though he was irritated with the chaos around him, he couldn’t abstain from not praising her for the courage she had shown. He commanded Damian to change the video to show the lobby area. Before he could change the CD, a gentle tap on the door forced him to leap to the door. He saw Alex standing with Cruz, the cab driver.

The cab driver was in his pajamas, looked like he was dragged out of his sleep. He appeared scared, and his eyes were searching for answers unknown.

Benedikt launched his questions “Did you give a ride to a lady around 2:30 AM this morning?”

“Yes” came the response. His voice had a shade of anxiousness.

“Where did you drop her?” Benedikt continued with his barrage of questions.

“I dropped her at Sofitel hotel by the Kruns Street.”

“Was she with someone? Did anyone join her on the way?”

By now he was convinced that his life wasn’t in danger anymore. “None. All along the ride, she was quiet. I tried striking a conversation, but her response was stale, so I left her alone.”

Knowing nothing more he could find from the driver he let him go. Damian informed they found nothing in Sofitel hotel, and no guest stepped out of the hotel around that time. He also mentioned checking the Sofitel hotel register, and his failure to find anything meaningful.

The smirk on Benedikt's face confused Damian. "She is a genius, several shades higher in intelligence than all of you put together. Check all nearby hotel lobbies. At that time of the hour, hotel lobbies are the only place to get cabs. Sofitel wasn’t her destination, I am sure she took another cab from there to some other place. Find that cab and find her next destination."

Damian had never taken such an embarrassing jolt before. Without further delay, he disappeared behind the closing doors.

Benedikt continued with the lobby-recording playback. He was fast-forwarding, rewinding at times and zooming in, but he noticed nothing of significance. As he was sweeping through the tapes at one point he saw himself moving to the executive bank of elevators, and then he saw her in the tape. Her picture was appearing grained as the video captured her on the extreme end of the frame. He paused the video and zoomed in. Though the image was blurring he could see Rita in a black gown, appearing out of place in that small coffee shop.

There was a knock at the door.  With a raised voice he enquired, "Who is it?"

"This is Russ from the security team."

He was let in. Russ informed that the doctor report came, and he indeed was drugged. “Looks like the substance was applied to your lips.”

Benedikt wasn’t surprised, as a piercing headache and the bad taste in his mouth was a good testimonial of her doings. He put a cursory glance at the report and continued watching the tape.

This time he played the video in slow motion to observe her reaction when he went past her. He saw her attention going towards the direction of the elevators. He could tell she was waiting for him all this time while pretended to have her coffee. He played the video forward and saw her approaching the elevators to the first floor where the poker tournament was held. The more he was learning about her, the more his intuitions were taking a definitive shape. It was evident she was there for a purpose. She was there for him.

He couldn’t believe how he fell into her trap, in such foolish and naïve manner. It was uncomforting, and his mind had morphed into a confetto, smeared with conflicting thoughts. The girl who had put his entire morning in disarray was not frustrating him for her misdeed, but for her disappearance. Deep somewhere he knew he wanted to find her, more for him than for anything else.

Benedikt glanced through the rest of the tape with dwindled hope. Beside being evident that she was in the hotel for him, he couldn’t find anything meaningful. He was about thirty minutes into it when his phone rang. He saw it was Damian calling and picked the phone in a jiffy, “so what did you find?”

“You were right.  She went across the street to the Carson Hotel and got a taxi from there. The concierge person identified her from the picture I showed him. She had to wait for good fifteen minutes before she got her next ride. We also located the other taxi driver. According to him, he dropped her by the market street. Like in the previous ride, she was alone, didn’t speak and remained to herself. Market Street is a business place and no business were opened around that time of the morning. I am certain Market Street was again a transient stop for her. Right, where she got down there was a tram station. I am quite certain she took the tram to the central train station.”

Benedikt lips graced a vicious smile as if he was enjoying the cat and the mouse game. It was like a puzzle with pieces scattered all over the town and he was tasked to put them together to solve the mystery called Rita.

“Head to the station and find out where she went. At that time of the morning, I am sure there will be very few travelers.” Benedikt commanded.

“I have sent Alex. I am expecting a call from him any moment." This time his voice had a spark, a fervor that was hard to conceal.

“I am impressed. Now find that girl for me.” Benedikt couldn’t avoid carrying a cynical simper as he concluded the conversation.

Damian for the first time since morning appeared relieved. He knew it would be a hard day, but he was enthused with renewed hope.

Benedikt knew sooner or later he had to inform the higher authorities. He wanted to ensure he knew enough before calling them. It was never going to be an easy conversation explaining how a girl landed in his room, had vanished in an unceremonious manner, and worse he had no clue of her motives. No one will buy the money-stealing story either. He was never a philanderer, and once when he tried, he had put himself in trouble. And now after the entire hullabaloo, he wasn’t sure if he wanted to catch her for her misdemeanors or for a purpose he was avoiding finding answers for.

In about thirty minutes Damian was back. His glowing face was suggesting that he got hold of something important. Before Benedikt could put forward any question he blurted with excitement, “Her name isn’t Rita. At least her photo ID suggests Sanchez. She boarded the tram to the train station and from there took a train to Copenhagen. There was another person with the name Trevor who took the same train. Not sure if they are related by any means. From Copenhagen, she took another train to Basel. Trevor also too took the same train. Couldn’t find any more information on Trevor. He didn’t stay in this hotel or in any of the nearby hotels.”

Benedikt had found three crucial pieces of information, the real name of the girl, the place where she went, and another person with the name Trevor. Basel wasn’t a big town; if she stays there for a day, he could find her. Time wasn’t his best friend and he knew he had to move quickly. He instructed Damian to put the Basel circuit on the job.

Keeping the news away from his higher-ups was never a good idea. Now that he had some details of her whereabouts he decided to call his seniors. With some hesitation, he dialed a number and was in the conversation for good fifteen minutes. At the end of the talk, though he appeared worn out, a sense of relief consumed his countenance. Despite all the pain, ordeal and nightmarish experience he was nothing but in complete admiration of the girl. It was evident he was getting drawn to her, not well for him by any measure, but for now, he carried the discomfort. The eagerness to meet her had increased many folds. All he wanted now was a good sight of her.

While he assumed she was in Basel, little he knew about the detour she took at Zurich.**\**

**Chapter 8**

***4:30 AM 13<sup>th</sup> April 2014, Zurich, Switzerland***

While Sanchez was trying hard to comprehend where she went wrong, her companion drove the car in random paths to descry the Peugeot. Part of his plan was to confirm if the Peugeot was for real behind them or was it just a false alarm. After taking few arbitrary turns it was evident that the Peugeot was indeed following them. It never disappeared no matter which way they went.

The roads were little busier than usual. This was the last weekend before Sechseläuten festival, the traditional spring holiday in Switzerland.  Around the streets in open spaces Böögg, the winter of Effigy were decorated for the grand Monday celebration. Though the Peugeot was dissolving at times on the busy streets of Zurich, it kept resurfacing back, confirming their apprehensions of being trailed. He kept a close eye on the rear-view mirror as he maneuvered his car with great caution, thinking of ways to bolt away.

Sanchez was warily observing everything that was happening around her. Their planned day proceedings were in complete disarray. Belted in the car all she could do was to go through the commotion. She decided not to intervene and left it to Stephen to resurrect them from the situation. Though she was muffled, her mind was muddled with several competing contemplations. Could this be a miscreant of Benedikt’s horde? Or was it someone that she wasn’t aware of? If this was Benedikt’s creation it was beyond her comprehension, for he was too powerful to chase them in such discreet manner. His men could stop them anytime and do what they needed to get done with. She knew even the local politicians, the police, and the government officials all would support Benedikt without any reasoning or questioning.

She replayed in her mind everything that happened during her final dash from the hotel. She distinctly remembered no one was in her sight, neither in the lobby nor at the concierge, the only exception being the bellman and the cab person. On her way to the station, she had checked multiple times to see if anyone was following her and couldn't recall seeing any suspicious character. No one except Stephen knew about the plan. But despite the prudent planning, there were people chasing them, who were aware of their whereabouts. She was clueless, and her mind couldn't conclude anything meaningful. She got booted out of her brooding when Stephen applied the brakes with urgency. She looked at him with inquisitive eyes questioning about the sudden stop. And then the car made a sharp turn into an apartment parking lot.  She realized it was an attempt made to evade the followers.

Sure, he succeeded, as he saw on the rear window the Peugeot passing by the complex without stopping. The parking lot was quiet; given it was a weekend, people were still in repose. Stephen wanted to be certain about his conjecture. He waited for good twenty minutes before stepping out of the car. He told Sanchez to stay in the car, as he went to check the streets for the rogues.

The cold wind of the early spring weather welcomed him, forcing him to close the buttons of his coat, and to push his hands into the warmth of his pockets. He tried his best to operate in stealth mode. He stepped on the sideways of the parking lot and walked towards the street with extreme swiftness. Once at the edge of the apartment entrance, he put his back to the building wall, protruded his head out and without exposing his torso glanced at both sides of the streets. Besides the moving traffic and the occasional pedestrian passing by, there was nothing that caught his attention. He looked for the Peugeot and couldn’t trace it either. Realizing his ruse worked, a sign of relief graced his visage.

Once he was sure no one was watching him, he exposed himself to the street and walked towards his left. He stopped at the intersection to check for anything out of the ordinary, convinced of no unconventional sighting he turned around and approached the other end of the street. He couldn’t find anything concerning there as well. Satisfied with his perusal he sprinted back to his car.

Sanchez perturbations were testing her patience. From time to time she craned her neck sideways to check on Stephen. After about ten minutes of angst, she saw him pacing towards the car. Stephen opened the door and settled down with urgency.

“Looks like our little delusion worked. Seems the Peugeot went pass us.” He rubbed his hands to get some warmth before starting the onward journey.

"Good riddance.” She dropped her shoulders as if she had heaved away a long-carried burden, passed a frail smile to Stephen, and closed her eyes.

“We need to leave Zurich soon. Let us get back to our apartment, rest for the night and leave first thing tomorrow morning. Sooner or later we have to reach Portugal and nothing like having few extra days to our advantage.” His voice was edgy and his mind still clouded thinking about the Peugeot.

Sanchez wanted to get a good night rest. After the last four long rumpus nights, to state she was tired was an understatement. She knew Stephen was anticipating a detailed report of her expedition, as they hadn’t spoken for the last four days. They had set a protocol not to call while she was on her mission, no matter how dire the situation may be. Now was the time for them to catch on all the measures. But she was sleep deprived, and she had no vigor left to put her brother’s perturbation to rest.

Stephen could read her mind and told her to get some sleep while he would drive them to home. Before Stephen could finish his statement, Sanchez was already in deep slumber. He looked at her and saw a sense of tranquility covering her stature. He smiled and got back to business. He pulled his car out of the parking with extreme care, looked for any warnings with prying eyes, and put the vehicle in motion. He knew once he leaves the city it would take a good couple of hours to reach the apartment, and he wanted to finish it in the shortest possible time without attracting any more trouble.

As Stephen was driving through the busy streets of Zurich his mind went on comprehending everything that happened since Sanchez’s arrival. He knew what Sanchez had accomplished was the first small step towards their goal. He was also aware that no matter how strenuous the journey might seem they were ready to face it without worrying about the consequences. It wasn’t easy for him to let her go in the devil’s world, but they had no choice. While he was glad to see her back from the dangerous liaison she undertook, he was aware of the peril that loomed ahead.

The unexplained following by the Peugeot was still hovering his mind. There were many unanswered questions, but for now, he was glad to put the immediate danger behind. His prime focus was to reach the apartment without getting into any further trouble. Driving alone, with the car-chasing nuisance out of sight, his mind apprehended the mountainous task that stood in front of them. He knew their path was rough, with many uncertainties and unknown, but that didn’t deter his enthusiasm. The glitter in his eyes was shining bright, even when his mind was crowded with some unsolved quandaries.

**Chapter 9**

***8:00 AM 13<sup>th</sup> April 2014, Stockholm, Denmark***

Benedikt was flabbergasted. A few hours back he thought he had cornered her, but now that believe was rapidly diminishing. Despite his stronghold in the region, his team had no clue about her whereabouts. The detailed reports had come from his Basel team and it was disappointing, to say the least. There was no scintilla of her existence. His infamous posse was disheveled, and hair tousled. No matter how much he tried, he couldn't keep his agitation curbed. His team had checked closed circuit camera of the train station, interviewed taxi drivers, went through town and council reports, but Sanchez was simply put, blatantly missing. When he first started the search, his assumption was the hunt wouldn’t exceed few hours. It was two days now, and the progress made was a mere collection of few scattered clues. His time was running out and he barely had any concrete leads to trace her down. He knew next few hours would be decisive otherwise his worst fear of a long-haul chase would come true. The threat of his higher up intervention was looming over his head as well. They were equally concerned and demanded him to control the situation with urgency. With every passing moment, his fear of becoming a sheer mockery amongst his peers was taking a more formative shape.

He pulled out her report that his team had put together, glanced through all the checklist items and found nothing missing. The only thing that had worked for him so far was his connection with the local passport office. He was now aware that before coming to Denmark she spent a considerable time at Ayr, the port City of Scotland. His team carried a complete search of her apartment in Ayr. Besides the fact that she was living there for the past two years, nothing concrete was found. The apartment owner rarely saw her during her two years of stay. She paid the rent on time, kept the apartment clean with no note of any untoward incidents. She lived an isolated life. No neighbors knew her, and she had no friend or acquaintance for confrontation and questioning.

Benedikt mind was spinning. The way Sanchez character had evolved was a shade higher than a professional. The discrete life she lived, the meticulously designed plan to get to him; the immaculate execution of the same and vanishing from the scene were all disturbing signs. He was outclassed. If at all his accomplices could exhibit a dash of what she had demonstrated, he could have been at a different level within his clan. It was absurd, but there was also a sense of bereavement that bothered him, it was as if he had lost a loved one. He was scared to admit, but deep in his heart, he knew he was fatally attracted to her. He still remembered the passionate indulgence they had, and her eyes, he couldn’t forget them, as if they were narrating a story untold.

He shook his head, and attempted to tarnish the untimely reverie and mumbled, “It has to be that map."

It was a cryptic map depicting the pathways of a dangerous dealing and was classified information. Anyone having access to it was a huge undertaking. At first, he was ignorant, as he assumed even if she had access to the map, she could hardly do anything with it, but now that believe was slowly giving way to a more formative trepidation. He was aware that it would be a mammoth undertaking for anyone to defeat the plan. Too many affluent people were involved and too much was at stake. “No one can corrupt it. No one can do anything about it”, he spoke to himself and no matter how firm he wanted to sound the voice had deep frail of vagueness.

This time he diverted his attention to Trevor. He had a vague notion that both Sanchez and Trevor had something do with him. Trevor was another mystery. Unlike Sanchez, his team could find nothing about Trevor. As disturbing as it sounds, it led him to the conclusion that Trevor wasn’t his real name, and he was successful in hiding his identity. What was more puzzling to him was, if Trevor and Sanchez were together then why only Sanchez identity was exposed. It might be possible they weren't together, or her identity was intentionally exposed to misguide him.  And then he thought about the other possibility, the probability of Trevor discreetly following Sanchez. However, he couldn’t find any reason strong enough to support the hypothesis.

As his mind was going through several such quandaries, one thing he was certain was about her presence on that train to Basel. He knew she bought tickets for Basel but chose not to get off the train. As the facts were unfolding he was undoubtedly convinced that he was up against a seasoned professional. She was intercepting his moves, which was dangerous by any magnitude, and it warranted him to treat her like an expert, not like a naïve teenager he considered before. His mind had become a mud puddle, the more he was stirring, the muddier it became. Deep in his heart, he knew that to get to her he had to think like her.

He went back to the drawing board and replayed the events that had unfolded so far. He tried distilling facts from assumptions. He was certain that she must have disembarked in one of the ten stations between Copenhagen and Zurich. He pulled out a sheet and plotted all the ten stations and started reasoning about the place that she might have chosen to disembark. He bit the end of the pencil and started mumbling, almost lost in his contemplations.

“She will not take a chance to get off the train too quickly as that would put her in imminent danger of getting spotted.”

He put an X on the first four stations as they were in the first 4 hours of distance from Copenhagen. He also crossed out Basel. That left him with three places Hamburg, Freiburg, and Zurich.

He pulled out his phone, tapped the speed dial number of Damian and instructed him to come to his room. Damian appeared in a flash, and stood in an attentive position, waiting for instructions.

Benedikt was strolling in the living room with hands crossed to his back and eyes on the floor. He was aware of Damian presence but didn’t bother to pay any attention. With no verbal exchange, the place morphed to a ghostly eerier, making Damian visibly uncomfortable. It was long two minutes before Benedikt blurted giving perspiring Damian some respite.

“It is clear from the findings so far that she isn’t in Basel. I strongly believe...,” He paused and continued “in fact, I am positive that she got down somewhere in between. I have given it a lot of consideration; the train stops at 10 places between Copenhagen and Basel. We don’t have enough resources to deploy on all the ten places. I had to pick few, and I have picked these three places”.

He passed the paper he scribbled on to Damian. Damian looked at it with great interest. He saw the name of the stations and lot of pencil marks across the same. Before he could conclude his findings, Benedikt continued with his monotonous tone, “Hamburg, Freiburg and Zurich, she is in one of these three places. We need to find her in next four hours, as the morning will dawn, I have a hunch she will change her location again. Put all your resources behind it, choose the best three men to lead each place and I need the team to report back every thirty minutes. As soon as you learn anything about her inform me, I would like to be in that place personally to oversee matters. Also, for the remaining stations, get all the train station closed circuit camera recordings. In case the odds are against us we should know quickly. Any questions?”

“No, I am good for now,” and he swiftly left the room.

After Damian departed, Benedikt had one final job left for the night. He grabbed his phone and called someone. The person picked the phone right after the first ring. Benedikt spoke in a low tone, “Listen I am calling you because I trust you. I have a job for you. But you need to make sure you operate in an absolute discreet manner. I don’t want anyone to know, almost anyone you can imagine.”

The person confirmed with a firm yes.

Benedikt continued further, “I am going to send you details of a girl named Sanchez and a man called Trevor, although the latter name I doubt is his real name. You need to find out whether they have any association with TransPacific in any form or shape. Once you find anything you call me on my personal number.”

The person agreed. Benedikt tried getting some rest. He lay on the sofa, but his mind was delirious, struggling to keep pace with several competing deliberations and predicaments.

**Chapter 10**

***4:00 AM 14<sup>th</sup> April 2014, Zurich, Switzerland***

The alarm clock ruffled the quietness of the otherwise serene morning. The night had still not given way to the dawn, as the gray clouds held the darkness with all its might.  Stephen's hands searched for the clock and after few failed attempts found the snooze button, which reclaimed the calmness instantly. On a regular day, Stephen would have extended his sleep to the last possible minute, but not today. He wanted to bail out of Zurich last night itself however decided otherwise, as he wanted Sanchez to recuperate from the last four days’ ordeal before they embarked on their next mission. While she slept like a log, he struggled to get any rest. His mind was too clouded with doubts and apprehensions. He tried hard, but as it always happens, the things you run away from haunt you back more fiercely. The previous night’s impression kept revisiting him until the wee hours. He was mentally exhausted and finally when he managed to sleep he was unceremoniously stirred by the dreading alarm sound.

After an initial scuffle, he dreadfully dragged himself out of the bed. He decided to use the bathroom first, giving Sanchez a little extra time to rest. In twenty minutes, he was all done. Wrapped in a towel he sauntered towards the only bedroom the apartment had. Sanchez was still sleeping. The wall clock showed 4:00 AM, and it was time for Sanchez to wake up. He gave her shoulders a gentle nudge and whispered, “Wake up San! We need to catch the 9:00 AM flight.”

Sanchez looked at him with blurry eyes, agape and lost.  She quickly regained her lost stature, stretched her arms, and pushed her back against the wall adjusting a pillow behind her and said with a gravelly tone "Good morning."

“Good morning. Sorry for being hard on you. We need to hurry. I want to be on the road in thirty minutes. Why don’t you get dressed? In the meantime, I will fix breakfast.”

Sanchez responded to his request with some reluctance and had to traipse in her attempt to find the bathroom. Meanwhile, Stephen got to his business hurriedly. It wasn’t long before Sanchez was back, all ready to conquer the world. She was dressed in petite dark blue jeans with a pullover hood, her waist-long natural brown hair was left without any confinement, giving her demeanor an alluring appearance. She was giving herself a good look in the mirror when Stephen announced that the breakfast was ready. There was coffee, scrambled eggs, and few toasts spread. They sat by the kitchen table and hurled on to devour.

By 5:00 AM they were ready to hit the road. They had two carry-ons with them. Stephen wanted to keep the luggage light to acquire more agility. They put their bags in the car, locked the apartment door and headed south to St. Gallen-Altenrhein Airport.

Stephen knew Zurich was the most popular airport in the region and could draw undue attention from his pursuers, hence chose to go to St. Gallen Airport instead. It would take them about an hour and a half to the airport, and he was well within time to catch the 9:00 AM flight to Lisbon.

Despite the early morning ordeal, Sanchez looked well rested. The drive to the airport was uneventful.  Soon they were in the boarding area ready to leave Zurich.

The flight departed on time and it was sparsely filled. There was nothing alarming so far and slowly they were convinced of their success in dodging the miscreants from the last night. It was a long flight, enough time for them to rest and rethink their go forward plan.

**Part II**

**Chapter 11**

***15<sup>th</sup> March 2012, Ayr, Scotland***

Tracing John Patterson in a large city like Ayr was like trying to find a needle in a haystack. Sanchez was in the city for over four months now, still clueless where to start or whom to approach. Besides the name and a vague assumption that he was in Ayr, she had nothing concrete to substantiate.

Four months back, her life confined in a small town where she knew everything that surrounded her. The known faces, the rumpus town, the choir group. She was familiar with all of them. But now she was in a city, much bigger than her past abode, exposed and vulnerable. Besides Aunt Naiena she knew no one in Ayr. The school, the city, and her home everything appeared new, everything was poignantly altered. She came to Ayr with boatloads of hope, but with the passing time, the reality showed its ugly side, morphing her into a disgruntled person. She tried every possible way to do away from the misery, but every attempt she put ended defeating her.

The only person, who could bring that occasional frail smile on her face, despite the hullabaloo in her life, was Aunt Naiena. She gave her the much-needed space and never questioned her disconcerted behavior. It was not that Sanchez didn’t try to curtail her emotions, but the past was too harsh, and her melancholy state often surged without any forewarnings.

She didn’t want to trouble her Aunt anymore and moving out to her own place was the only answer she could reason with. She managed to get some odd jobs after school hours and earned enough to rent a place. This was her escape from the guilt of showing a glum demeanor to her aunt.

She had several unanswered questions; many unresolved mysteries, and a mind crowded with anxieties, all screaming loud, making a perfect cacophony, with no visible escape path.

**Chapter 12**

***10:00 AM 16<sup>th</sup> March 2012, Ayr, Scotland***

The apartment was a small sixteen by twelve feet studio with an attached bathroom and a small kitchen area. The room had just enough space to fit a small mattress. All her belongings were scattered around the cramped space, disarrayed and unkempt, and no effort was spent to put the place in order. It was no more than a refuge for her, a temporary home until she could devise a well thought out plan to find John Patterson.

It was a beautiful Friday morning. The summer was around the corner, well endorsed by the warm weather that consumed the surroundings. People were out on the streets enjoying the climate, taking a break from the dreadful winter they witnessed not too long ago. She had a day off from school. With nothing to do, she pulled out a worn-out notebook from the closet and started flipping through the pages. It was the same diary, which had John’s name scribbled on it, the writings that gave her the idea to leave her hometown and come looking for him in Ayr. It was not that she had not gone through the journal before, but she kept revisiting it with dwindled hope to find one clue that could help her find John.

And then she was on the page that had the map of Ayr. It was a busy print with odd scrawls made by pencils in a haphazard pattern. Over time it had discolored, giving it a faded greyish look. She had seen it many times before, and every time it appeared more puzzling than before. The letter TP scribbled within parenthesis next to John's name was always a mystery to her. She had tried decrypting it many times but never succeeded. Today was no different. She kept staring at the page for a while and when couldn’t conclude anything meaningful, frustrated and despaired she put the notebook away.

The time was little over 11:00 in the morning. She opened the windowsill and dangled her head out to feel the gentle breeze. There was something in the air, an uncalled freshness that convinced her to leave the confinement of her room. Seaport Park was always her favorite destination, a place where in the past she had succeeded in breaking away from the shackles of her gloominess.

She boarded the bus, took a window seat and put her earpiece on with Adele “Set Fire to the Rain” playing at a high decibel. She was in trance, floating in her own world when the signage on a passing building almost made her jump from her seat. The tall structure had TransPacific written on it, but what caught her attention was the word TP carved like a logo shining distinctly in the mid-day morning sun. She hurriedly got off at the next stop and traced her way back to the building. It was a wild guess, and her instinct suggested some connection between the TP in the notebook and Transpacific.

The building was a tall glass structure and the architecture suggested a modern design. With some hesitation, she stepped into the lobby, and the place blew her mind away. It was nothing less than what she had seen in movies. The place was bigger than life, it had a gigantic water fountain positioned in the center to almost 100 feet high ceiling, an array of modern paintings were graciously covering the walls, she could see at a distance elevator guarded by biometric access and an elegantly designed reception desk armed with gorgeous damsels. The place also had sturdy and serious looking security guards, monitoring the areas discreetly through their prying eyes. The whole setup was intimidating, making her nervous and fidgety.

She mustered some courage and approached the receptionist. “How can I help you?” The receptionist queried with a warm smile.

With a sotto voice, she enquired if she could meet John Patterson.

“Do you know which department he works for?” the receptionist quizzed her with a subtle tone.

She had no clue what to respond and shook her head in negation. She was perspiring. Her face looked pale as if she was caught pilfering.

The receptionist smiled, mostly to comfort her, and with an engaging tone continued, “Let me see” and got busy working on her computer. After few minutes, she looked back at her, still carried the congruent smile and declared that there were four John Patterson, and unless she had some more details she wouldn’t be able to help.

Sanchez had some mixed reaction. While the company had four John’s it hardly meant anything. John Patterson was a common name. Besides, even if all four of them were called out, there was no way she could have recognized the one she was looking for. Going on a quizzing spree with them was never an option anyways. She politely thanked the receptionist and promised to come next time with more specifics.

**Chapter 13**

***2:00 PM 16<sup>th</sup> March 2012, Ayr, Scotland***

Despondent, she casually sauntered tracing her way towards the exit. Her mind suggested ways to converse with all four Johns but none of the alternatives were holding any water.
