She almost exited the building when a large kiosk by the guest waiting area caught her attention. Few visitors were playing with it. While unsure what the kiosk was about, out of curiosity she walked towards it. There was nothing on her mind, besides a faint hope of finding answers to her competing quandaries. She spotted an empty space and tapped on the large interactive display.  The screen came to life. It had various zones providing information about the company, its business, credo, and other related facts.  She was randomly playing with the infographics when a section called “New Employment Opportunities” caught her attention. She tapped the area with interest. The screen displayed information about TransPacific’s recruiting program for summer interns. There was an email ID at the bottom of the display and instructions on how to apply for the job. She took a quick note of it before leaving the building. 

On her way back home, she convinced herself to do whatever it takes to get the job. She had a strong hunch that the person she was looking for was in TransPacific. And her hope was to reach to him through this job. She was laced with newfound determination to succeed, and the grit in her believe enthused her with renewed hope. After reaching home she soaked herself in a bathtub. The deluge of hot water helped her immensely to cultivate her thoughts. She felt fresh and rejuvenated ready to conquer the world. First time since she came to Ayr, she had something to cheer about; a purpose to chase, and she wanted to give it her best.  

Her first strategy was to write an email to them. She knew if she failed to get their attention the first time, it would be her last, as she presumed there would be some software to filter and accept only one mail per candidate. She had to get it right on the first go, as there wouldn’t be another chance, and failure was not an option. She wanted the letter to be crisp, to the point yet interesting. She turned on her computer, and after a lot of deliberation, corrections, writing and rewriting she crafted her final script, which she believed was one of her best pieces she ever wrote. It took her the rest of the evening to bring it to a final closure.

She was extremely good in language arts. During her school days, she used to write stories and poetry. Despite having a stronghold in English literature, this email was the toughest letter she ever wrote. Writing poetry was never a do or die situation, but finding words to ensure selection for a job, which she badly wanted, was overwhelming, to say the least.  Once satisfied with her work she read it aloud to ensure it sounded right.

“Respected Madam/Sir,

As a child, my most exciting moments were always the Christmas time when Santa magically delivered my wish list toys. It was sheer magic, and I used to thank the reindeer that carried Santa to my home despite the dreadful winter.  I also grew up thanking the postman who gave me the joy of receiving my pen friends' letters from distant places, or the delivery of surprise gifts from my grandparents. It always brought a smile to my face.

Soon I was introduced to reality and I learned there are no magic reindeers. Nor does the postman have a magic wand that could bring surprises on a whim. And I learned that all the complex crisscrossing of parcels across the globe is immaculately handled and accurately delivered by logistics companies such as yours. It is because of enterprises like yours that makes this otherwise ginormous world truly within our reach. Logistics companies truly help bring myriad cultures into one-fold and help us appreciate the world better and make us more compassionate and informed.

My father was a delivery man, who traveled places to make sure he delivered things that mattered most to people on time, and in the process brought happiness in their lives. Since the beginning, I had always dreamt of working for a logistics company to continue what my father started, bringing happiness to countless people through my job. 

TransPacific is an esteemed enterprise and I am aware that to earn a seat as an intern besides the intent, there needs to be an excellent education track record one should demonstrate. I have made a conscious decision in electing the right subjects to be qualified for the job. I have taken advanced courses in Geography and Political Science. I am not only good in demographics but also have great knowledge about the political landscape of Scotland, England and the east European region. Besides the core subjects, I have technology as one of my electives, and I possess significant knowledge in getting work done using computers.

With this background, I submit my candidature for your kind consideration.

In Anticipation and with best Regards,

Sanchez Roberto.”

Once convinced she emailed it to the ID she had noted from the kiosk along with her resume. It was a Monday when she wrote to them and it wasn’t until Friday when she received a response back.

She decided to spend that weekend with aunt Naiena. It was a lazy afternoon and she came directly to her aunt’s place from school. Like always, she headed straight to upstairs where the desktop computer was stationed.

She logged in to her email account in anticipation of a response from TransPacific, and there it was! Her heart was pulsating so hard for once she cogitated it would explode on her. With sheer nervousness, she clicked on the mail and waited impatiently for the mail to open. She tried hard to control her excitement as she read,

“Dear Ms. Sanchez,

We have received your candidature for the job and we take great pleasure in inviting you to the next round of evaluation. You are required to come in-person for your next interview. The details of the location and time are given below.

Please ensure you bring an ID, all certificates and any recommendations that you consider will be relevant to the meeting.

We look forward to meeting you. 

Regards,

June Wield

Recruitment Coordinator”

She was on top of the world. She couldn’t believe what she read. No matter how many times she read, still it was hard to sink in. She could have stayed in that room reading it for next few hours, but her elated being got ruffled when Aunt Naiena called over for evening supper. Before scurrying away, she wrote a thank note to the recruitment officer, acknowledged the receipt of the mail and confirmed her in-person presence for the interview. She was beaming with elation after a long time and it was visibly noticeable to all, and Aunt Naiena didn’t shy away from complimenting.

She had planned ahead in anticipation of the interview call. She had requested her literature and Geography professors for recommendation letters. She was a straight ‘A’ student. Much to the envy of her classmates, her literature professor was accused of favoring her over others. Both the professors were pleased to learn about her summer intern job, and they gladly provided their respective recommendation notes. She already had a distinction certificate on computers and fortunately that year she had also won the interschool debate competition.

Her next two days went overthinking about everything that could go wrong in the interview and what mitigation strategies she would deploy.

After a long excruciating wait, finally, it was Monday. She was at the office thirty minutes before time. She put on the best attire possible. A dress pants she got for her concert the previous year and a white shirt that she bought with her savings over the weekend. After about fifteen minutes of her arrival, she saw two more candidates joining her. They were three of them – clearly, she was the youngest, at least by the look of it. She was a first-year college student – usually, people come for intern jobs in their final year of college and hence she visibly was littlest amongst the three. In fact, that was her biggest apprehension, she was scared to be rejected for being too young for the job.

At about 8:50 AM the receptionist called her name and she was walked into a conference room. The room appeared inviting. It was a well-lit space, large enough to fit four people. There were two people in the room waiting for her. She greeted her audience and she was reciprocated with some grim smiles. The room that appeared inviting suddenly metaphor to a suffocating zone. She tried her best to retain her composure as she settled on a chair across them.

The first person introduced himself as David Sussex, customer relationship manager. He was sitting right across her, must be in his late thirties, wearing a plaid coat with a checked shirt. Right next to him was a young lady named Amanda Pearl; she was looking graceful in her black dress suit with a white blouse.

She forwarded the copies of her resume and recommendation letters to them. As they were flipping through the pages, David launched his first question.

“Tell me more about yourself?”

She told him about the village she belonged to, her family, academics achievement, her interest in gymnastics, coming to Ayr for higher studies, awards she had won and her hobbies. She mentioned everything except few things, which she concealed for reasons best known to her. 

David continued with few more questions about gymnastics and the awards she had won. He was particularly interested in her recent debate win. Sanchez provided him with all necessary details. After answering the first few questions she was at ease much to her relief. All her fears had disappeared, and she was in complete control of the conversation. 

David continued, “What do you know about TransPacific?”

That was an easy question for her – she had done an exhaustive research on TransPacific. She was sure that she knew more about the company than her immediate audience. She provided them a nice summary – right from how it was founded, its value and credo, the acquisitions it had made over time, recent news, geographical spread, financial performance, key leader’s insights and their future growth strategy. Though they both held an impassive posture, she could clearly see through them on how impressed they were.

Amanda, the HR person broke her silence. Her question was more about how choosing this job would help in her career progression. A prelude to this question was in her initial email to them, and she went ahead and elaborated it to their satisfaction.

David took the rein back from Amanda and put forward a dangerously leading question. “Let us assume you are in a customer call and the person is in extreme frustration about an undelivered package. You couldn’t trace the package either, and you need to reach out to the shipping team for help. Meanwhile, the customer is furious and making all kind of threats. How will you handle the situation?”

It was a tough question to answer, particularly when she hardly had any time for rationalizing her options. But, that was the prime intent behind the question, as he wanted her to respond impromptu. 

She took a pause, recalibrated her thoughts and continued. 

“If the situation is that bad and I can’t resolve the problem, the chances of calming the customer is highly unlikely unless one approaches it little differently. I will first empathize with his situation. To ensure that he believes in my empathetic demeanor, I will use an apologetic tone in my conversation with him. I will also let him know that in the system I am unable to see his order and I need to put him on hold briefly as I need to talk to the shipping department. When a person is frustrated, the last thing you want to do is put the person on hold. As I may not be sure how long it may take, I will ensure that I will come back on the line in regular frequency to update him on the proceedings. I will be transparent with him about what I am trying to do and if the investigation is going to take a longer time, I will keep him informed about the same. No matter how many hops I need to go I will ensure I will not transfer the call. Also, I will get a convenient number I can call him back in case the line gets dropped. In situations like this giving reassurance is key. I will ensure that if I am unable to resolve his problem, I will have my supervisor available for him to interact with. Meanwhile, I will send an escalation note to my immediate supervisor apprising him of the situation in hand.”

She was in complete control of the conversation and she knew after that response, she had secured the job. After some casual probing, they finally came to the penultimate question. “Any questions you have for us?”

While she was tempted to inquire how she faired, she controlled her anxiousness. She passed a tight-lipped smile and queried about the next steps.

She got some general answer on someone from human resources would get in touch with her should they decide to go forward. With that, she thanked them and stepped out of the room.

She looked at her watch and was amazed to learn that she spent a good forty-five minutes with them, and time surprisingly flew. She was delighted and headed straight to college to continue with her schedule.

She didn’t hear anything from them until the following Monday. There was a mail congratulating her on being chosen as an intern. There was some online paperwork that she had to get done, some drug test and a contract that she was required to sign.

She had no clue how to react to the news. First, she was numb and then a sudden surge of emotions overtook her sanity. She cried, she laughed, and she screamed. It was a huge milestone and she dearly hoped all the answers she was looking for years were hidden somewhere in TransPacific, and she was raring to unearth it. 

**Chapter 14**

***16<sup>th</sup> April 2012, Ayr, Scotland***

Her job as a summer intern was starting in two weeks, and her eagerness to begin was testing her patience. After a long arduous wait, the day finally arrived. She was aligned with the customer service department, reporting to David Sussex, the person who interviewed her.

He was one of the nicest people she met at TransPacific. He was kind, gave her lot of space and helped her learn the business. She developed an instant liking for him. The first few days were tough. She was overwhelmed with the size and the scale of the work. But David support in the initial days helped her immensely to overcome her unwarranted apprehensions.

She was on the customer service operator floor.  She had no job assigned. Only instruction she received was to help call agents whenever they needed any. First two weeks went in observing the work, learning how they operated and taking count of processes they followed. After her induction, she was at the total discretion of the team leads on the ground, most of the time she was running between agents to pass information or generating some arbitrary reports. While working for a large corporation was exciting, she ensured not to drift away from her goal to find John Patterson.

It took a month before she was assigned a desktop computer, something she was eagerly waiting for. That day she decided to stay back in office to initiate her initial research.

At 6:00 PM the day shift rolled off. The night shift people were slowly coming in and the floor was sparsely filled. She looked around and found no one paying any attention to her. The time looked right for her to initiate her search. With extreme caution, she opened the global address list on her computer and searched John Patterson. As the receptionist had mentioned before, she got four people with the same name. She looked through the details of each one of them to see if she could draw any meaningful insights.

The first John was a vice president of international operations, she ruled him out, as he didn’t seem to be the person who will carry freight around. The second John had a field job. He was from the Far East side of Scotland, a place called Fraserburgh. Though his job profile was akin to what she was looking for, the distance between her village and Fraserburgh wasn’t convincing enough. It was too far for anyone to make delivery on a regular basis. She put aside this John in her fallback plan and moved forward in examining the remaining two names. The third John was in South Africa, putting that one aside she opened the last John details.

And as fate would have it, this John was a perfect match. He was from Ayr and sure he was a freight delivery person.  She hurriedly noted his number and office stop. Her feet were trembling with fear, her stature perspiring and her heartfelt desirous. All she wanted was to leave the office as fast as she could before anyone notices her fidgety being.

**Chapter 15**

***30<sup>th</sup> April 2012, Ayr, Scotland***

Though not confirmed, she had a strong intuition of being very close to the truth she was seeking. The first four months in Ayr were testing times; often she was deplored and clueless.  But, with the job she had found a purpose, a resurrection from her shambled life, and she was enthralled fancying the possibilities that had surged back with urgency and purpose.

On her way back home sitting alone on the bus, a thought crossed her mind leaving her scared and petrified – when a neophyte like her can find John Patterson why the cops failed? Or did they consciously stay away for reasons unknown? Something was not right, and it disturbed her, there was more to this episode, a conspiracy, a subterfuge she wasn't aware of. More her mind indulged in the quandary increasingly she became uncomfortable and restless. She forced her mind away from the shackles of the past incidences and brought her focus back on John Patterson. After a lot of deliberations, she concocted a plan and now she was raring to execute on it.

Her next day went by quickly, mostly in running tasks for the team. She was now well versed with the customer service process. She also gave a couple of significant data curating ideas. Dave was extremely happy with her contributions and gave her the go-ahead to pilot one of the concepts she had proposed. Incidentally, there was an organization-wide idea innovation quest undergoing, which was run by the corporate office. Dave encouraged her to put forward her idea for the contest.

Soon it was evening, she had planned beforehand to stay back late. She patiently waited for the office people to leave. Once convinced of no one being around, she picked one of the agent’s phone and called John’s number. After few rings, she heard a deep embodied voice on the other end.

Sanchez cleared her throat and in a disheveled voice blurted, “Am I speaking to John Patterson."

“Yes, you are."

“I am calling from Mr. Cussak’s office and there is an important packet he wants me to deliver to you. You can either come to office or I can deliver it to your home. Sorry for the inconvenience, but the package needs immediate attention.”

Mr. Cussak was John’s reporting manager’s superior, and her hope was he wouldn’t take his chances to say no. Also, as he wasn’t his immediate reporting manager, her belief was he wouldn’t call him either to confirm for the same.

“Do you know what I need to do with the packet?” came a puzzled response from John.

The conversation was progressing as she anticipated.

"I wouldn't know. But there is a sealed letter that has all the instructions. Would you prefer to come to the office or do you want me to deliver it to you?”

He took a long pause, and with clear averseness in his voice stated, “It would be great if you could deliver. The address is 254 Brendstad Street Ayr.”

She thanked him and promised to deliver it in the next hour. She couldn’t believe she had John’s address in her possession. She had a strong intuition of this John to be the one she was looking for.  Though she knew it was a huge risk it hardly deterred her from going ahead with her plan.

She ditched her usual daily commute bus ride and took a cab instead. Throughout the travel, she kept rehearsing the script she planned to narrate to him. She was delirious and tried her best to keep her anxieties under control. The traffic was denser than usual, and it took her about forty-five minutes to reach John’s residence, longer than what she expected. The taxi driver dropped her on the street right across his home. It was an old block of row houses. With her mind clouded with numerous apprehensions, she gently tapped the door. After a couple of knocks, a disheveled looking person appeared at the door. He was in his late fifties, appearing more than his age because of his worn-out look. He was wearing a pair of dilapidated jeans and a tee shirt, which had the official emblem of the Bohemian Football club.

She quickly introduced herself as the person from Mr. Cussak’s office. He looked at her empty hands, and with a confused look blurted “You said you have a packet for me.”

She acknowledged his observation and immediately launched into what she had practiced relentlessly all the while on her way to his home.

“Would you be kind enough to allow me inside your house? I want few minutes of yours and I will explain you everything”, Sanchez blurted the words in a most uncanny way, not a shade close to what she had rehearsed.

He appeared irresolute and his countenance displayed shades of bewilderment. With a penetrating voice, he cracked, “What is it about?”

He sounded displeased, and Sanchez was losing her plot at a rapid pace. In a visibly unconventional way, she further blurted, “I promise it wouldn’t take much time. I need few minutes to explain.”

The old man took a pause and it appeared the longest minute of the evening for Sanchez. With dwindled hope and pleading eyes, she kept looking at him. After pondering for a while, and with a reluctant look he gave in to her request and instructed her to wait outside. He was back in a minute with his shoes on. “Let us take a walk instead.”

The by-lanes in the neighborhood were quiet with few sporadic evening commuters gracing the streets. As they went past his home he snapped, “Tell me what is on your mind. Do you even work for TransPacific?”

Without raising any further alarm, she clarified that she did work for TransPacific, but the entire packet delivery episode was a farce. She told him that she had to meet him, and she couldn’t find any other resolute. Though she apologized with profound genuineness it failed to make any change in his agitated outlook. He looked wild and with an irritated voice he remarked, “You better have a good explanation for your misdeed or else you are heading for a big trouble.”

It was time for her to execute her script. Perplexed, she pulled her out of her incoherent conjuncture and hesitantly continued, “Mr. Patterson let me straightway come to the point. Has your business taken you to Gribin in the past? Do you know anyone from there?”

He stopped abruptly and stared at her with wide-open eyes. His disheveled hair looked further tousled. His face turned pale and eyes appeared agape. A thousand emotions were crossing his face and from being confused he soon transformed into a scared person. Observing his flustered demeanor, she was certain she found the John she was looking for. Without giving him much time to react she proceeded further, “Mr. Patterson I have no intention to upset you or harm you. All I want is answers to some questions. Questions that I am seeking answers for the past two years…”

Her voice trailed reflecting a shade of brittleness. "I need your help. Please say something” by now she couldn’t concede her emotions anymore and looked asphyxiated and shaky.

He stood there dumbstruck for a while. And when he realized what had struck him he blurted with an irritant tone, “I don’t know what you are talking about. I need to go now” and hurried back towards his home. Seeing him fleeting away she dashed to narrow the distance between them. She grabbed his hand from the rear making him slow down, “Mr. Patterson trust me what you will tell me will remain with me. Please… you are my only hope."

He shrugged his hand off and without saying anything he surged ahead leaving her disheartened behind. She stood there for a moment or two before leaping behind him. He was around the street and she dashed with all her might to be in front of him. By now he was irritated. He snapped and warned her to stay away from him. He also threatened her for consequences if she comes after him. She had no option but to let him go. One thing was certain he knew something, and he was clearly avoiding any confrontation. For the next several weeks she tried talking to him, but without success. She even stalked him, followed him discreetly, confronted him, tried hard to strike a conversation, but nothing seemed to work. All her plans were losing steam and it appeared as if she was at a dead-end, with no-where to go.

**Chapter 16**

***18<sup>th</sup> June 2012, Ayr, Scotland***

Two months had gone by since her last confrontation with John, and she had barely made any progress. Her over indulgent mind was delirious to find some headway but was miserably failing. On a Sunday afternoon lost in her chaotic quandary, she pulled her father's notebook from the closet.  While she was languidly flipping through the pages something caught her attention. She noticed John’s name invariably scribbled at many places. It was marked against addresses, on footnotes and as part of some To-Do list items. Right at that moment, she had an epiphany moment. She was almost certain that this time John would give in to her demand.

She took her chances and headed straight to his house. It was a cold afternoon with few people on the streets. She reached the door without anyone noticing her presence. The house appeared deserted with no shade of any living soul around. She tried peeking through the glass door, but the curtains were shielding her vision. With some hesitation, she gently knocked on the door and patiently waited for some response. The gentle knocks turned to some hard bolts, and after some repeated attempts John responded. She must have woken him from afternoon sleep as his long hair was ruffled and scattered all over his wrinkled face. His somnolence vanished as soon as he saw her. He almost closed the door on her, but she used all her might to hold the flap, and in an orotund voice she exclaimed, “If you don’t listen to me, soon you would need to answer the police.”

That worked. All his guards vanished, and with dropped shoulders, he stated in a rough dejected tone, “why don’t you leave me alone. You don’t know what you are looking for. It will ruin us.”

He finally spoke. She pushed herself into his house as if she had an unstated decree to be in his living room. This time the old man hardly resisted and slouched on the sofa. He appeared defeated and by his looks he sure had submitted himself to the situation.

Sanchez swiftly followed him and sat on the chair right across him. Without wasting any time, she continued, “Mr. Patterson, as I said before I have no intention to harm you. I want to know what happened and trust me once I have the truth you are out of it. No one would know anything.”

He was edging to escape. Hesitantly with a low tone, he blazoned, “You got to trust me. I hardly know anything to tell you.”

She was hesitant to interrupt his flow of thoughts and kept her anxiousness under control. He was gazing at the ceiling as if trying to recollect the old times and continued, “it was something to do with that package...” his voice trailed, and he became silent.

Her mouth was dry, and her cheek had the rough edges of the morning brittle cold. She couldn’t hold her nerves any longer and blurted, “What Package? Who sent the package?”

“I have no idea. All I can tell you is that the package instruction came from the head office – it came from Evan Slender, who heads sales operations for Scotland and England. Please keep me out of it. You need to, believe me, I don’t know anything beyond this” his eyes were pleading. There was sincerity in his request and he clearly wanted to escape from her trap.

She had another name to chase now. Before letting him go she had one final question for him – why was he so scared about the situation? But he didn’t have any answer for her. He kept gazing the floor, as he couldn’t face her anymore.

It was a revelation, a confirmation that the answer she was seeking for was in TransPacific. She thanked him and assured him that whatever he stated would stay with her. It appeared as if he got himself out of a guilt he was carrying for a while, and a sense of calmness regained him.

The hunt was becoming complicated for her. Though she had a new lead, yet there were more questions than answers. There was a distinct sense of accomplishment; a gratuitous excitement that engulfed her, yet there was an uncertainty that invariably snatched all her exhilaration away.**\**

**Chapter 17**

***1<sup>st</sup> May 2012, Ayr, Scotland***

It wasn't an arduous job to trace Evan Slender. A quick search on the global address list was all she had to do to locate him.  He was the executive chairman of sales operations, and in his profile picture, he appeared to be in his early fifties, depending on when the photo was taken. She did some further research and learned he was one of the three people who founded TransPacific. He was a self-made person, ranked in the top 300 in the Forbes list of billionaires. To her astonishment, besides this fact, not much was written about him in the public domain. He had not spoken at any conferences or was interviewed by any media. Compared to the other founders of TransPacific, he was the least mentioned about. She continued her research for several days but failed to locate anything meaningful. Tired of her failures, she concluded either he was an introvert or extremely particular about his privacy. She thought of visiting him but the access to the executive floor was restricted.  Nothing was working, and she was splitting hair thinking to find a wedge to break away from the snare she found herself trapped in.

Months went by without any progress. Though she was left with no options she resisted denouncing her defeat. She was bereft of recourse but not hope. She knew her day would come and convinced her fidgety mind not to lose hope. With no lead in hand, her job at TransPacific kept her going. Dave was pleased with the impact she had created in a short period of her association with TransPacific. The improvement ideas she gave to the team on process quality got shortlisted as one of the top three innovative ideas across the organization and she was invited to present it to an evaluation committee.

She was delighted to learn that the chief guest was none other than Evan Slender. The forces of natures were favoring her to help come closer to her goals. She knew her intent was noble and considered the event as a medium to reach to Evan Slender. She prepared well for the event – she revised her idea, tested it with the team, created a running report from the pilot she executed before and quantified the outcomes. She was ready for the day.

On the day of the presentation, all the participants were called upon to the executive floor. There were three finalists including her, and they were required to present their idea to a panel of judges. The executive conference room was one of the swankiest places she had ever seen. The room was octagonal in shape, spacious enough to fit about thirty people.  The big glass top table in the center was also octagonal in form, appearing like a miniature version of the room, well fitted within it. There were ergonomically designed Aeron chairs sparsely arranged around the table, each sitting area had a leather-bound notepad and a Waterman ballpoint pen placed with impeccable precision. Intensity controlled recess lights dropped from the wall were illuminating the room with its dim yellow glow. Across the table, there was an eighty-inch television set with state-of-the-art meeting room gadgets. Two sides of the wall were covered with mahogany wood finished bookshelf with classic hard bond books arranged across the sections in an immaculate manner.  The room was on the edge of the 15th floor and the transparent glasses on the sidewall were giving the picturesque view of the port city. Despite the room being intimidating, to her surprise she was at ease, ready to embrace the challenge. All the participants were in the room about thirty minutes before. The conference room assistant helped the participants’ set up their presentation and they were ready for the showdown. 

Five minutes before the event commencement she saw the executives walking in. The panel of judges comprised of five people, representing the five division of TransPacific. They took the seats close to the display. One of the Judges was Evan Slender. His appearance was exactly like his profile picture. He was wearing a dark suit, looking stark apposite, and his face was beaming a radiant smile. Besides the judges, they had the three-division business manager whose ideas were selected for the competition, Dave was one amongst them. There were also people whom she couldn’t identify, but by the looks, they appeared to be from the executive office. Unlike what she assumed, the executives were extremely friendly, they put the participants at ease and congratulated them for making it to the finals. After the initial introductions, it was show time. Each one of them got about fifteen minutes to present their idea.

Sanchez was the last one to go. She was confident for few reasons. First, her idea had a direct impact on savings; her pilot proved that she could save upwards of 5M pounds over 5 years for the company, highest amongst her competition. Second, she had a working prototype while others had a concept on paper. And she was good in public presentation.

The presentations were done on time and the participants were requested to wait in an adjacent room. After about twenty minutes of wait, they were called back in, and she was announced the winner.

Besides getting some bonus money and certificate of acknowledgment she got what she was expecting the least, a chance to work with Evan Slender directly. She couldn’t believe, and her emotion got the best of her. Much against her wish trickle of tears appeared at the corner of her eyes. She wasn’t sure whether the sniffing was out of joy or because she managed to break away from her chaotic quandary.

Evan congratulated her and stated he was looking forward to seeing her in his office. After Evan was gone, a person walked up to her and introduced himself as Bradley Faucet. He stated that he was from Evan’s executive office and instructed her to come and meet him in his office tomorrow. He gave her his card and swiftly paced out of the room.

**Chapter 18**

***15<sup>th</sup> May 2012, Ayr, Scotland***

Bradley was the program manager responsible for executing multiple mission-critical programs for Evan’s office. He became Sanchez's new official reporting manager. Her job was to ensure that her idea gets implemented and the benefits get delivered. One thing she indisputably understood; TransPacific recognized talent, otherwise providing an intern with such a big responsibility was a rare sight in any corporate setup.

Life in TransPacific became extremely busy for her. Over the period of her stay, she found Bradley to be astute and a manager with amazing leadership skills. She learned a lot about managing project timeline, delivering quality work and getting milestones accomplished. First few months went by swiftly. Besides being close to Evan’s office she made no progress on her personal agenda. Partly it was impeded because Bradley was omnipresent. He ensured she always had company. He was playing the role of a responsible leader helping his team member to succeed. Besides, he was the first one to come and last one to leave the office. His protective demeanor wasn’t helping her to continue with her investigations.

She realized, the only way she could be on her own was by coming to office earlier than Bradley.  Going forward she decided to be in the office by 6:00 AM, much before Bradley’s arrival time. While alone in the office she looked through files, searched office drawers and peeked in Evan’s workplace, but she couldn’t find anything meaningful. Coming early didn’t help her by any score either.

It was two months in her new role when one day she noticed some strange behavior in the office. A high-profile delegate was visiting Evan, and the entire floor got cordoned off. All employees were confined to a small zone with strict instruction not to trespass. She saw the incidence repeat few times in the next few months. No one knew the reason behind such unreasonable outlandish demand. And to her disbelief, people followed the routine and never questioned.

Evan’s office assistant, a young lady in her mid-twenties, became a good friend of Sanchez. One day over the lunch table Sanchez casually enquired her about the fuss created during such visits. While she reserved her comments, the only part she revealed was a name, Benedikt, and how the office becomes a secured zone whenever he was around.

It must be a week of her early morning ventures when Bradley tersely appeared right at the time she entered the office building. With a stern voice, he commanded her to see him in his office.

With an insipid look, she entered his cabin. She was petrified and no matter how much she tried, she couldn’t shrug away her startled mien. He didn’t greet her, displayed a stern look, and with a demanding tone, he probed why she was frisking around. The earth caved on her, and her mind went numb. She tried her best to regain her composure and responded softly that she had no clue what he was talking about.

His eyes were searching her, making her lose any remaining nerve. He declared he was aware of all her proceedings and had got the videos to prove it. He further went ahead and announced that so far only he knew about it, but if she refused, to tell the truth, he would be forced to involve the security team.

She was caught red-handed, her mind was whirling hard to spin a story and out of nervousness she blurted the wrong words, “It is sheer out of my inquisitiveness. I was eager to find out why we are put in isolation when Benedikt arrives.”

She was weaving herself to a point of no return. His immediate next question was how she knew about Benedikt.  She didn’t want to put Evan’s office assistant in any trouble and put forward a vague answer stating she herself couldn’t recall how she knew his name.

Bradley instructed her to take a seat and offered her some water, which she graciously accepted. All her guards were dropped, and she was starkly exposed. His gazing eyes were disturbing her the most. After about couple of long minutes he stated, “Look I know you are not telling me the truth, but honestly, I don’t care. I have no interest in why you are sniffing around. However, I am interested in you.”

He took a long pause. She was puzzled by the sudden change in his stance. She kept quiet and waited patiently for him to continue further. “I can help you with what you are looking for.  Let us head out for a cup of coffee. Shall we?”

She didn’t react and lamely followed him. Right outside the office, there was an Italian coffee shop. They both settled with a cup of black coffee. Without mincing any words, he continued, “Evan is running a large scam. I am not sure about what they do, but I am certain they are delivering goods across a route that starts from Europe and goes all the way to Russia. The plan of the route is decided every three months and Benedikt is the custodian of the same. Once the route is defined they all meet to discuss things. There is no one fix place for the meeting, this time the meeting was in Ayr. Lot of senior executives of TransPacific, high ranked government officials and politicians are part of this. The reason I am telling you all this is because I want you to join me, and help me collect evidence against them. You are intelligent, beautiful and you are different. We can form a good team." He took a pause and continued, "Remember this discussion remains between us. If you try to act smart and tell anyone about it I will expose you. No one will believe your story once I show the videos.”

She had no option but to say yes. However, she was curious to know more about him, though she was hesitant she still went ahead, “Who are you? Why are you after Evan?”

He brusquely responded, “I will never bother to know about you and you shouldn’t about me.”

She agreed, that way she could keep her secrets. Confused about what he was expecting from her, she innocently enquired what he wanted from her.

“For now, I want you to join a gym, a kickboxing class, professional karate and rock climbing. You are already enrolled – make sure you learn the skills with utmost sincerity. Trust me, you will need it in your quest to find more about Evan.” There was a shade of evilness in his smirk that made her obnoxious and uncomfortable.

Though surprised, she timidly accepted what he offered. For the next one year, all she did was training her body. She was frustrated. At times she desired to escape, but despise the thoughts knowing what Bradley was capable of doing. After about a year they had their first real conversation, the discussion about getting details of the map from Benedikt. 

She left her job at TransPacific and dissolved into oblivion. Surprisingly, she never saw Bradley, nor did he approach her. He was like a mystique character from some gothic settings. She was thankful to him not only for the lead but also for the training, which had come so handy in getting her hands on the map. She was unsure where the map would lead her to, but she had a strong hunch as this to be the medium to reveal the mysteries she had been hunting for the past few years.

**Part III**

**\**

**Chapter 19**

**4:00 AM 15th April 2014, Zurich, Switzerland**

The train halted abruptly, pulling Maxwell out of his slumber. For the first few seconds, he was in a state of oblivion, trying hard to resolve his whereabouts. It was a traumatic experience to give away his sleep. The last few days were nothing but living life on the edge. And as reality sunk in, his first reflex was to ensure Sanchez was still on the train. He craned his neck sideways to peep into the compartment but couldn’t find her anywhere.

He transformed from a lethargic self to a sprinter with a purpose. He dashed to the train compartment to reconfirm her absence and once convinced, he leaped out of the train with his eyes searching for her in all directions. It was wee hours, and to his advantage, there were few people on the platform. He scanned each one of them with prying eyes, and then he saw her not too far away from him, scurrying towards the exit. She demonstrated a sense of urgency as she approached the main hallway of the station. He scooted back on the train, grabbed his bag and went behind her. He moved with watchful steps to ensure not to make the chase look too obvious. From the distance, he saw her disappearing behind the doors of a BMW, which was parked right by the curb of the inner driveway of the train station exit.

This wasn’t a part of his plan. Sanchez had taken him by complete surprise. He had a well laid out scheme to trail her upon her arrival in Basel. And here he was, in the middle of Zurich without any support system, disheveled and frustrated. He saw a taxi stand next to the exit, and he dashed towards it with all his might. He got into the first one standing by the edge of the station entrance, flashed the driver his identity card and commanded him to follow the BMW.

The taxi driver was startled. Though reluctant he followed the instructions, perplexed and confused unsure what struck him. Maxwell’s mind was racing. He tried to comprehend the sudden change in plan introduced by Sanchez but failed to conclude anything meaningful. The streets were busy because of the spring festival weekend and he didn't mind it at all, for it helped him to camouflage. After about fifteen minutes of following her, he observed the car making some random turns as if she was aware of being followed. He instructed the taxi driver to slow down and directed him to be about three to four cars behind the BMW.

His doubt was confirmed when he observed the BMW continued taking some indiscriminate paths. He had no option but to follow, for losing her sight wasn’t an option he could afford. As he was contemplating his next move, he saw the car taking a sharp turn on a side lane. It took him a couple of minutes to make that turn and when he finally got to the cross street, the BMW was nowhere to be seen. He forced the taxi driver to accelerate the speed. There was a big apartment complex on the left, he peeked at the entrance as he went past it and saw a slight glimpse of the car.  He informed the driver to take the next right and stop by the curb. Using a rideshare app on his phone he called another taxi. The cab was about five minutes away, and he hurried to look for a temporary shelter. He had a strong intuition that someone would come looking for him. He saw a coffee shop at the street intersection. He bolted in, ordered a cappuccino and took the corner seat by the door. From where he was sitting he had a clear sight of all the four corners of the street.  The cab arrived on time. He instructed him to turn on the ride meter and wait for him.

His guess was right, in about five minutes he saw a young man, in his early twenties prying the streets. The man spent few minutes observing the street with cautious eyes. Once satisfied with his investigation he turned back towards the apartment building. The moment his back faced the street, Maxwell leaped out of the coffee shop and waited at the curb to see where he was heading. Sure enough, he was going to the opposite intersection to continue his search. Maxwell rushed to the parked taxi and instructed him to continue waiting for a few more minutes. The taxi driver appeared upset and before he could complain, Maxwell announced his identity to the driver. He abstained himself from taking any chance this time and instructed the driver to take the passenger seat. The driver had mixed emotions, torn between being concerned and nervous, but Maxwell had bigger problems to solve than worrying about his fellow companion.

After ten minutes into the wait, he saw the BMW rolling out of the apartment complex. It made a left on the street and headed north. This time Maxwell was extra prudent. He kept the taxi at a distance and traced the path with added stealth and alertness. He was cognizant of the fact that the extended distance between the two cars might result in losing Sanchez’s sight. With no superior alternatives, he took the conscious decision of taking the calculated risk. After few turns, he saw the BMW heading towards motorway A3. He was relieved, as trailing a car on a highway is much easier than the lattice of Zurich streets. After about two hours of driving on A3, he saw the BMW taking the highway exit. To make his chase easier, he found few more cars exiting the highway. It helped him, as he blended his car with other vehicles into a perfect disguise. The BMW drove for another ten minutes before it turned towards a small lane.

Maxwell avoided the turn to ensure he didn’t give any undue exposure, especially when the chase was almost coming to an end. As he passed the lane where Sanchez's car turned, he made a fleeting glance to see which way it traveled. He couldn’t see the BMW but managed to see a yellow sign stating in German “Kein Ausgang” – an evil smirk crossed his lips as he knew the street was leading to a dead end with no further outlet. He was certain they would be in one of the houses on that lane.

On the next intersection, he disembarked the taxi. The taxi fled with a screeching noise, as the driver tried to get away from the ordeal as fast as he could. He gave himself few minutes before starting to walk towards the lane where the BMW turned not too long ago. He paced his walk, which was little shy of a dash. His ferreting eyes were looking for the car. After about 600 feet wandering into the lane, he saw another lane cutting to the left. He had a strong intuition of finding the car in that lane and went ahead with his instincts. As he walked through the narrow pathway, he saw a row of two-storied apartments. There were about 7-10 blocks, spread on each side of the lane, and each block had 8 apartments. The parking lot was by the individual blocks, and the cars were neatly parked in 2 by 2 lanes. Maxwell was scrutinizing the cars, looking for the BMW. After passing the first two blocks he saw the car neatly parked in the front row facing the street. He saw only one apartment in the building with the lights on. Despite the sheer darkness, it would be hard for anyone to miss the tremulous smile his face graced.

He knew he had only a few hours to execute his plan, for he was certain they would soon leave the place. He had some chores to do before leaving the apartment. Now that all the hoopla and the commotion were behind, he was famished and had a sudden urge for black coffee. He traced his path back to the main street. There was a gas station right across the street; the convenience store by the gas station was the only possible place to get anything in the early morning hours. He ordered a coffee and picked a cold sandwich from the refrigerator. There was a small sitting area by the café and he settled with his early morning nibbles. After devouring his sandwich, he pulled out his phone and called someone, and was in a discussion for about ten minutes. He got a call after thirty minutes of wait. He stepped out of the café and walked towards an Audi R8, parked outside the store. A man in his mid-thirties stepped out and handed him a packet. He shook his hand and slipped into the R8 while the other person disappeared into the coldness of the morning mist.

Maxwell Parked the Audi at the entrance of the lane where Sanchez was sheltered. He carefully trod the path with extra vigilance.  He was by the block where Sanchez’s dwelling was located. And his inquisitive eyes searched for the apartment lights. Sure, enough the lights were off, and his conjecture was accurate, they were reposing.

It was early hours of a lazy Tuesday; besides, the coldness had put all souls in their shelters. Taking the morning mist as his shield he quickly sprinted into action. He had a transmitter, which he installed under the boot of the BMW, little further away from the exhaust. He turned the transmitter on and checked the signal on his GPS. The GPS came to life and displayed the coordinates of the BMW in its small display. It was a long chase, and now that he took care of what was important he could look forward to getting some rest. He walked back to his Audi and drove it to the next street, parked it by an empty spot, reclined the seats and dozed off. This time he trusted the transmitter’s receiver beep to keep a check on his sleep. Neither he had the verve, nor any energy left to be paranoid and stay awake.

**Chapter 20**

**4:00 AM 17th April 2014, Stockholm, Denmark**

The loud chime of the phone ruffled the quietness of the night. This was the first time Benedikt slept with some peace since Sanchez disappeared.  But the phone refused to give up and was making an absolute dissonance. Benedikt jolted to consciousness and vaguely looked around for the phone. He found it between the sofa cushions he was resting on. He fumbled, and after few attempts succeeded in hitting the answer button and responded with a gravelly voice.

“Chief, it is me. I have some important information to give you.” There was a spark of excitement in Damian’s voice.

“Go on” Benedikt senses were alert, and he craned his neck to switch on the lights.

“The CCTV footage at Zurich train station clearly captured Sanchez getting off the train early in the morning. Trevor was on that train too. The way he approached the exit makes me believe that he was following her."

Benedikt looked at his watch it was 4:00 AM in the morning. If he leaves now he can be in Zurich by 6:30. “I will be there by 6:30. Is there anything else, you found about them?”

“I also checked the security cameras of the main entrance. She left in a BMW, though the image wasn’t clear, I still managed to get the plate number. Trevor left in a cab. The cab waiting area was at far left, away from the camera. I tried all possible ways but couldn’t trace the plate number of the taxi.”

Benedikt was wide-awake by now and his eagerness to know more was at the extreme.  “Whom does the BMW belong to? Did you find out about the taxi driver?”

Damian continued, “We may have to wait until morning to get the BMW details. Marcus couldn’t trace the transport registration person yet. Also, I couldn't locate the taxi person. The taxi area by Zurich station is open for all drivers. I tried checking with the drivers at the stand, but unfortunately, no one noticed him getting into a cab. I am on it and I am hopeful of getting some lead soon.”

Benedikt started getting dressed and made his final point, “I am leaving now and will see you in a couple of hours”.

He ended the call, picked his travel bag and tapped a number on his phone and blurted “Get the jet ready, I will be there in fifteen minutes. Also, get the route path and clearance for Zurich." 

As Benedikt walked outside the lobby a cold wave of wind gave him shivers through his spine, taking him by surprise, and the residuals of his tiredness faded away in a flash. His mind was in constant battle with his predicaments. This was his chance to salvage the damage, to confront Sanchez and to conquer some unstated desires.

 

 

**Chapter 21**

**7:00 AM 17th April 2014, Zurich, Switzerland**

It was little shy of 7:00 AM when Benedikt landed in Zurich. The private transfer at Zurich allowed Damian to take his car straight to the tarmac where Benedikt’s jet was cooling down. In few minutes the retractable air stairs flung open from the clamshell-shaped plane door and Benedikt started descending.

No matter what the situation or occasion was Benedikt never got his sense of dressing wrong. He was wearing a dark blue woolen double-breast Armani suit. Underneath the suit was a neatly fitted waistcoat, gracing his shirt was a single knot sky blue tie. An unbuttoned London-Fog trench coat was covering his stretched arms and torso. His dark brown shoulder length hair was back brushed, and eyes were covered with dark blue shades. The wind coupled with the gust of air from the jet wings was making his trench coat make a surging clamor noise, aptly complimenting his demeanor.

As soon as he was on the final step of the jet bridge, Damian lunged forward and opened the rear door of the car. Benedikt ignored the invite and opened the passenger side door instead. A helper brought Benedikt’s luggage and put it in the opened boot. Damian looped around and took the driving seat and within minutes he was out of the airport on to the service lane.

“What is the update?”

Damian without waiting any further started speaking “We were able to get the car's details. It is a Hertz rental car. Someone rented the car by the name Stephen Roberto.”

“Isn’t Sanchez last name also Roberto?” Benedikt’s voice reflected inquisitiveness.

“Yes. Both have the same last name. There must be some relationship between them. Also, the car is rented until tomorrow. And...”

Benedikt interrupted Damian and barged in with his next question “Where did they rent the car from?”

“They rented it from the Zurich airport, but it is interesting to note that they had requested to return the car at St. Gallen-Altenrhein airport. I have booked you in the Ritz close to the St. Gallen-Altenrhein airport. After dropping you I will go to the rental office and join Russ, who is on his way to the airport.”

Benedikt’s face turned red and from his look, anyone could guess he was on the verge of losing his cool. He tried his best to control his nerves and with a passive voice blurted, “I had my share of sleep, let us head straight to the airport.” He looked at his watch it was 7:15 AM. “How long will it take to the airport?”

“Once we are on A1 motorway it will be less than an hour. We should be there by 8:30 AM.”

Benedikt knew he was running out of time. He had no option but to hope he could get hold of Sanchez. “Drive as fast as you can” he instructed Damian. He had few more questions for him, but he decided to wait. His prime motive forthwith was to reach the airport at the earliest possible time. He pulled out his phone, called someone and instructed the person on the other side to find about Sanchez's flight details.

The A1 motorway was lean in traffic and Damian was doing a fine job in maneuvering at the highest speed one could possibly drive without getting into trouble. Benedikt pondered hard to find some way to get hold of Sanchez. His superiors had given him only the next three days to bring it to a closure. Regardless, he had to bring this untoward nuisance to end as starting Monday he had to be in Tallinn to meet the Russian delegates. Wednesday was when project “sub rosa” would commence, which couldn't be compromised at any cost.

He was bolted out of his brooding as he got pulled abruptly by the car seat belts, caused by the sudden brakes applied by Damian. The cars were congregating, and from 160 KM/HR, the speed got reduced to 30 KM/HR. Benedikt lowered the window, stretched his neck out to see what caused the holdup, couldn’t find anything and ridden with frustration he flinched. He was about ten minutes into the stall now and his annoyance was at the extreme. He sure wasn’t a happy campaigner, giving Damian jitters although none of the proceedings was his fault. After dragging for about fifteen minutes he witnessed the reason. The left lane was closed, causing the cars to divert on to the remaining two operating lanes. The exasperation on Benedikt’s face was blatantly visible. He couldn’t hold himself and blurted out, “the damn municipality workers – not sure what are they constructing in this cold morning, wasted fifteen minutes for nothing.”

He looked at his watch and time was 8:35 AM. Every minute was diminishing his hope exponentially, and the delay wasn’t helping to keep his stature calmed. To add to the misery, Russ was also badly stuck in some accident traffic and was delayed even further than them.

They arrived at the rental car area without any further delays. Damian took the car straight to the rental office area, parked the car, and both rushed to the rental office. There was a person shouting from the back for parking the car at the main driving area, but they cared less to lend their ears to him. Damian enquired about the BMW in haste. And to add to their insult the car was returned a while back.

Benedikt pulled out his phone called someone and blurted, “Did you find anything?”

“Not too long back two tickets to Lisbon were bought. It took me a while as the tickets were bought only this morning.” The person on the other side responded with some hesitation.

“What time is the flight?” His aggravation was at a rise.

“Sorry chief. The flight departed” came the timid response from the other side.

Benedikt snapped the phone and screamed in frustration “Damn!! I was this close from nabbing her!!”

He looked at Damian and with a grating tone screamed “Get me to the jet. We are flying to Lisbon.”

As Benedikt settled himself in the car for the hour-long ride to the plane, his mind drifted off to a different world crowded with many passing thoughts. He murmured “Why Lisbon? What is her motive?” Benedikt was certain that the whole Sanchez episode wasn’t a trivial matter. There was a game plan Sanchez had, but no matter how hard he tried to solve the mystery, he failed. He queried himself, “his operation ‘Sub Rosa’ had nothing to do with Lisbon then why is she traveling to Lisbon?” He went into a deep subornation, “Am I oversubscribing to a fear that might not even exist. She had nothing to do with operation ‘Sub Rosa’. Maybe she is a kleptomaniac and robbed me for fun.”   He couldn’t reason it, and the urge of getting to the depth of it was almost unbearable.

He attempted to divert his attention by using the time to impose few questions to Damian. Without looking at him he continued in a low hushed tone, “Did you find anything about the person who was chasing Sanchez?”

“Nothing so far.” came the reply from Damian.

“Do you have a good capture of his picture?”

“I am afraid not.”

“Well then put someone on the job. Will you?”

“Sure” Damian responded sheepishly and called and instructed someone to work on the picture.

Benedikt dialed another number and patiently waited for the person to respond. The phone kept ringing without any response. He tried the number three more times, but no one picked the phone. He put the phone aside and enquired, “How long more?”

“We should be there in next 20 minutes”, Damian declared. His voice reflected clear signs of hesitation. Benedikt noted that the traffic was cruising along; ironically that wasn’t the case while he wanted it the most. He could have caught Sanchez before she boarded the plane. The phone rang and pulled him out of his brooding. He picked the phone and snapped, “What took you so long to respond?"

“It was a mistake, didn’t notice my phone was in silent mode.  Since last night I…” Benedikt stopped him in between and blurted with a grating voice “I am not interested in your story. I want you to head to Lisbon Airport now. I want you to trace and captivate two people. They are traveling by TAP Portugal and will be landing in next hour. You need to ensure you grab them and meet me at our usual place. I am on my way to Lisbon and should be there in next few hours. Details of the passengers will be sent to you in the next fifteen minutes. Any questions?”

“Nothing for now, but I may have few after I see the information.” the person on the other side of the phone announced.

Benedikt continued further “Also, one more thing. I might send you a picture of another person. I strongly believe he is on the same flight. If you find him, get him as well.”

“Alright chief, point noted."

Soon Damian pulled into the Zurich airport private transfer Tarmac. The jet was waiting for Benedikt.

As Damian opened the door, Benedikt announced unceremoniously, “You are coming with me.”

Damian wasn’t surprised as he was aware of Benedikt’s unpredictable behavior. He followed the motion and entered the jet. Soon the plane was waiting for the clearance to fly.

Benedikt was all set to retire from the early morning ordeal he had gone through, but his phone decided otherwise. With a dejected look, he glanced at the phone.  But his face lit with an unprecedented glitter when he saw the name. He picked the phone with urgency and whispered in a soft tone “Hold for a second”. He instructed Damian to leave him alone. After Damian left, with a gravelly voice he continued, “So tell me what you have found?”

Damian could see from a distance Benedikt animated conversation. He patiently waited and wondered what could be more important for Benedikt than to be in Lisbon, particularly after knowing Sanchez was already an hour ahead of them. After fifteen minutes Benedikt was finally done with his conversation, he was oozing a sense of relief, a mystery that Damian would never be able to solve.

**\**

**Chapter 22**

**5:00 AM 16th April 2014, Zurich, Switzerland**

The constant beeping of the GPS shook Maxwell out of his slumber. In the apprehension of Sanchez escaping unnoticed, he slept miserably.  And now when he tried ditching his conjured repose, he experienced as if he hadn’t slept at all. The monotonous hum of the transmitter was an indication that the car was on the move. This time Maxwell wasn’t in a hurry, for the transmitter he implanted the day before would help him find them without any more speeding car chases. He prudently gauged his options and decided to give five minutes’ lead before beginning the chase.  While trailing, he ensured to manage a safe distance but within an attainable reach, to help recover from any gratuitous situation. 

After about an hour of driving, he saw the signs to St. Gallen-Altenrhein Airport. He couldn’t resist the smile and admired their intelligence on picking this airport instead of Zurich, “the best way to avoid any intruders,” he murmured. It was about time to accelerate and to be at the airport before them. He knew he had the advantage of leaving the car by the curb, unlike them, as they had to go to the rental office first before heading to the airport.

Soon he was by the ticketing area. His vigilant eyes were fixated on the main door screening each person with observant eyes. Ten minutes into the wait, he saw Sanchez arriving with her companion. He put the hood on to ensure minimum exposure of his countenance and started following them. After walking for five minutes he saw them entering the TAP Portugal ticketing queue. This time he wasn’t surprised. He knew they would be heading to Lisbon but wanted to ensure his credence was accurate. He followed them to the queue. There was one passenger between him and Sanchez. The closest he came to her in years, a state of affair, which he accepted with great interest.

He saw them purchasing tickets from the counter. As soon as they disappeared from his sight, he approached the ticketing lady, showed his ID, looked at her badge and with an urgent, but firmed tone blurted, “Miss June, I need you to answer few questions about the two individuals you were attending to a few minutes ago.” 

Maxwell took the lady with utmost incredulity. She looked frightened. With a nervous look and with a stuttering voice she blurted, “I have to get my supervisor.”

“Sure, but you need to hurry.”

Within a minute the supervisor came rushing to the scene. Maxwell showed his identity and continued, “I need few quick answers.”

“Sure sir, how can I help you?” The supervisor was listening with absolute sincerity.

Without wasting any time Maxwell blurted, “There was a young couple who bought tickets from this counter few minutes back. I want to know which flight are they on? Can you also confirm their names?”

The supervisor was nonplussed, and he sure was not amused. From his appearance, it was evident that he had never faced a situation like this before. Maxwell was losing patience and with an agitated tone he stated, “Look you are wasting time.”

The supervisor regained his lost stature. He looked at the lady at the counter and with a taut voice commanded, “Get him whatever he needs."

The lady swung to action, her hands were moving fast on the keyboard and staring at the screen she spoke, “Their names are Sanchez and Stephen Roberto. They are on flight TAP020 to Lisbon. The flight leaves in about 45 minutes”

Maxwell pulled out his credit card and gave it to the lady “Put me on the same plane. What seat number are they in?”

“24 A and B.”

“Give me a seat few rows before them.”

As she was working on to get the tickets, Maxwell turned towards the supervisor, “I know you need to report the incidence. Make sure no one should come looking for them. If anyone has any question send them to me." He handed his visiting card to him and continued, "They aren’t criminals, I am chasing them for a different reason. Is that clear?”

Supervisor though perplexed, showed his agreement with reluctance. Maxwell swiftly moved towards the security gate. He had enough time to get a good breakfast before boarding the plane, something he was looking forward to since the time he woke up.

He cleared the security in ten minutes. As soon as he was out of the security check, he put his hood back, covering a good part of his exposed face, to avoid any direct contact with Sanchez. And with watchful steps, he sauntered towards the gate. His pulsating eyes looked around the seating area but couldn’t locate them. He glanced sideways and found a coffee shop. His famished being won the battle against his inquisitive mind, and he decided to grab a quick bite before looking for them. At the entrance of the coffee shop, he saw both sitting and enjoying their coffee. They were in some deep conversation and missed seeing Maxwell entering the store.

Maxwell immediately retracted his steps, turned around as fast as he could and without looking back he approached the seating area. The waiting area was sparsely filled, suggesting that there were few people going to Lisbon that morning. He found a good seat from where he could easily observe them. Though undesirable, he ditched the idea of satisfying his hunger, at least for the time being. He pulled out a book from his bag and pretended to read, as he closely watched them dwelled in some intense dialogue.

Sanchez’s intricate composed character deeply fascinated Maxwell. It was love at first sight for him when he saw her for the first time about two years back. Sanchez was always a mystery to him, an enigma that his soul always yearned for. She was reserved, quiet, always exuded a sense of calmness. Many times, he was on the edge, ready to express his love for her, but circumstances were beyond his control impeding him from executing his plan. Every time he met her, he felt like meeting her for the first time. Every time she had a new hue, exuded desirousness and had a trace of abstruseness. And every time he was left with the desire to know her more than all the previous time.  She was an alluring dream, a hazy occult, an impulse that was enigmatic and arduous to behold.  Today wasn’t different, and he was drawn to her the same way, as he was when he saw her for the first time.

The boarding announcements screamed through the speakers, alerting Maxwell. His hunger crawled back as he slowly came out of his reverie. The time was about right for him to get some breakfast as Stephen and Sanchez were getting on the line to board the plane. He dashed to the coffee shop, ordered an egg muffin and an espresso to go. He stood by the coffee store and patiently waited till all the passengers boarded the plane. Soon the boarding area was empty and it was time for him to get on the plane. He put the hood over his head and swiftly moved to the boarding gate. Once inside the plane, he took his seat as fast as he could, taking extreme measures to avoid coming to their notice.

It was time to relish his breakfast. He was content and murmured gently, “When you are hungry even a simple egg muffin becomes the most sumptuous meal.”  Once done with his chores he pressed the flight attendant call button. A pretty looking female in her early twenties arrived. She enquired with a wide smile on her face “How can I help you?”

“I am going to sleep the entire flight. Make sure you don’t wake me unless we are on the ground and I am still sleeping.” Maxwell tone intended the pun, and he passed a gentle smile.

“Don’t worry, our slogan says, ‘with arms wide open’ and this is true for any comfort for our esteemed guests like you. Have a pleasant restful flight."

Maxwell found a reason to smile. He thanked her and drifted off to sleep.

**Chapter 23**

**9:00 AM 17th April 2014, Zurich, Switzerland**

The jet was about to leave Zurich when Benedikt's phone created an unperturbed dissonance.  From the sound of the ringtone, he knew it was a call from the higher-ups. He waved Damian to leave the cabin. This was the second time in the last fifteen minutes he was asked to leave. Unsure how he would avoid overhearing the conversation, he landed himself in the bathroom.

Benedikt face turned insipid as the person on the other side continued with a monologue. After listening for a while, he barged in, and with an assertive tone he blurted, “I will get her this time. My people are waiting at the Lisbon airport. I should have an update for you in the next couple of hours."

The person on the other side continued with a tight voice, “Benedikt, your time is running out. You have taken way too long to arrest the situation. I am asking you again, do you need help?”

“I have it in my control.” It was hard not to notice the frustration in his gruff tone.

“This is your last chance, otherwise I will be forced to put my men on the job, no exception. I hope it’s clear.” The firmed tone meant business and Benedikt was aware of it.

“Yes," he sounded asphyxiated as he forced those words with a great deal of reluctance.

“Good. Also, any information on the other person?” the barrage of questions never seemed to end.

“If he is chasing her, he has to be on the plane. We will put a close eye on every person that gets off the plane. If he is on the plane we will get him too. I will call you as soon as I have any further update.”

“Ok. I will be waiting” and the line was snapped.

Benedikt took a sigh of relief as he put the annoying phone away. He was irritated for sure, but was unable to discern the reason behind it? Was it because of his repeated failure to get hold of Sanchez or his errant seniors frustrating him?  Whatever the reason he was sure of one thing. Sanchez was driving him wild. His inability to get hold of her before the ‘sub rosa’ was a huge concern and was testing all his remaining forbearance. But despite all the commotion and exhaustion, his craving for Sanchez had only increased by the moment, “Gosh Am I in love? It can’t be”, he murmured with some hesitation.

She had made his life despondent. He had to leave the poker game in the middle, something he waited for the entire year. She made him look bad in front of his seniors, from an otherwise spotless career and had forced him to run around like a rookie. All these were good enough reasons for him to loathe her, but instead, his heart was aching for her even more. He hadn’t experienced such restlessness before. Every time their brief innuendo crossed his mind, his urge to be with her increased multi-fold. He had so many questions, and the wait to accost her was almost unbearable. He knew confronting her was the only way left to put his anxieties to rest.

He came out of his brooding and noticed Damian was not around. Then he remembered how he waved him to disappear. There were two places he could have gone to, the cockpit or the bathroom. The cockpit was out of the question as the jet was in motion. The only other place was the bathroom. He headed to the rear of the plane and knocked at the bathroom door, and Damian came out embarrassed and rueful.

Before the takeoff, he dialed one final number and enquired. "Are you at the airport?”

“Yes, I am. The flight is on time. I should have them by the time you land.”

“Good, see you soon. Don’t disappoint me.”

The jet left for the skies and Benedikt went into a reverie, Sanchez was laced all over his heart, thoughts, and soul.

**Chapter 24**

**12:00 PM 17th April 2014, Lisbon, Portugal**

The Lisbon air traffic was busy welcoming the spring tourists to Portugal. The plane was encircling the airspace for a long time struggling to find clearance to land.

Sanchez was looking down from the window and was enjoying the beautiful panoramic sight. It was clear skies and the mid-morning sun was shining with brilliance. She could see the stunning row houses arranged like pearls on a string, appearing exquisite and serene from up above.  The plane after circling for a while started descending. The landing was uneventful and as it was taxing on the tarmac, the flight attendant voice rattled the quiet setup. Although Stephen was awake he kept his eyes closed, trying to get whatever respite he could before their next tribulation, but the blasting speakers were not helping the cause. The plane came to a halt and the passengers started disembarking. Stephen and Sanchez got off the plane taking extreme precaution. Their vigilant eyes were looking at people with suspicion, attempting to spot the goons, if any exists, before they would succeed in tracing them.

The lines at the immigration were long and it took them a while to clear immigration check. So far, they hadn’t encountered anything alarming.  They paced out of the check gates and started executing their preconceived plan. The Lisbon airport map was engrained in their head. They knew where they were headed with fine precision. There were restrooms right outside the check gates, before the airport terminal receiving area. They took a left and headed straight to the washroom. They remained there for good twenty minutes, and when they came out, they dawned a different look. Sanchez's head was covered with a khimar, a headscarf that concealed her face half way down from her nose. A black veil masked her forehead and her body graced a full-length black Abaya. She appeared a little stout, mostly created by the extra padding of clothes she had worn. By her looks, anyone could mistake her to be a middle-aged lady from an Arab nation. Stephen sported a mustache; his head was covered with a Bonnie hat and he donned a military dress. He had the stripes of a cadete aluno, a cadet officer in Portugal army. Stephen was a well-built young man, with the military clothes on him; he looked like a recent recruit in the Portuguese army.

The entire car-chasing episode in Zurich had made Sanchez over cautious and paranoid. The plan to appear concealed was Sanchez’s idea. She worked on the plan the night before in Zurich, to dodge anyone who might be after them upon their arrival in Lisbon. The Arab ethnic wear was already with her, which was part of her alternate plan to escape from Copenhagen.  Besides, to appear in disguised looks were always part of the evade strategy. They always carried few dresses and adequate makeup with them in case the circumstance enforced them to don a different look.

They both took different paths and headed straight to the underground train station, for a ride to the city hall. As they passed the waiting area Sanchez incisive eyes noticed a corpulent person at the receiving area, who with few of his companions were guarding the exit path. By their sheer appearance and the manner, they were scrutinizing each passerby, made Sanchez believe they were there for a purpose. Her paranoia led her to wear a disguised look, which came to her rescue. She went past them without raising any alarm. Stephen was ahead of her and he also succeeded in concealing his identity from the prying eyes of the ruffians.

Their plan was to meet by the entrance of the City Hall Building. They chose not to be in the same train compartment and got on the train without any untoward mishap. Sanchez disembarked at the Santa Apolónia station. There were two stations in City Hall; Stephen’s plan was to disembark at the Gare do Oriente station, which was little further away from the Apolónia station. Once off the train, she headed straight to the ladies’ room. She took off her Arabic attire and changed into a nice floral printed off-whitish skirt. The skirt was broad, spreading till the ankle length. It was a renewed version of the traditional seven skirts that local Portuguese wear. The seven skirts represented the digit seven which had ethnic significance in Portuguese culture – mostly calling out the seven days, seven colors of the rainbow or the biblical seven waves. Matching her skirt was a black headscarf, which resembled well with the garb she wore. The white-laced blouse embellished her bosom, as she donned the look of traditional Portuguese lass.

She left the ladies room and walked towards the city building. It was around noontime in Lisbon. The morning sunshine was warm enough for people to enjoy the weather.
