Although there was a hint of humidity, the breeze traveling from the nearby ocean brought a sense of freshness. The tourists were flocking around the monumental city building, taking every chance to capture the moments using their cameras and smartphones. The street food stalls across the city hall monument were buzzing with people who were trying out the local food delicacies. There was a sense of happiness and an epitome of peace that consumed the region.

Throughout the walk, she was superfluous and vigilant. Her peering eyes were looking for any slightest of the hint that could suggest any danger. So far, she didn't find anything out of the ordinary, but her mind was fretful thinking about the person she saw at the airport.  She knew to avoid any further tribulations they must leave Lisbon at the earliest possible time. Contemplating with her choices, she slowly sauntered across the street to a coffee shop. She sipped her coffee, kept a good eye on the surroundings and waited for her brother to arrive.

Stephen’s station was little further away from the main City Hall Building. He disembarked about ten minutes after the station Sanchez got off and paced towards the City Hall building. The edifice had a monumental significance and was a must-see sight for tourists’ visiting Lisbon. Though the building was rebuilt in the late 18th century, it still held the architectural and artistic significance of the 17th-century gothic looks. The crowd around the monument helped Stephen to camouflage his appearance, as his eyes looked for Sanchez. A tap on his shoulder shocked him, he turned around and saw Sanchez standing behind.

Sanchez greeted him with glee as she hugged Stephen. To passersby’s it appeared as if two lovers were meeting after a long time, the man returned from his duties and meeting the love of his life. The charade was an important part of their plan, for if the goons were still hiding in stealth, they would not consider them as a threat. After few minutes of pretense, it was time for them to leave the place. Sanchez looked towards Stephen and blurted in a subtle voice “Did you notice the stout person wearing a checked shirt at the Airport?”

Stephen was trying hard to match to Sanchez wayward careen, “Yeah! I noticed, but then I wasn’t alarmed as he was looking away from us. The disguise worked. Great job, after all, it was your idea to wear these fancy clothes” He almost chuckled as he blurted those words.

“That isn’t the point – what I am worried about is despite all our efforts, we still got traced. We have been lucky to escape them so far, but I am afraid if we carry on like this soon we will run out of luck. We must escape this gridlock. It is a big distraction and a nuisance." She was dyspneic, and she spoke with an oscillating tone while hurtling through the cobbled streets of Lisbon.

“I agree. We need to give this a good consideration. We are making it way too easy for them to locate us. We are liberal in using our identities for buying airlines and train tickets, and I am sure it is working against us. These criminals have access to all information and tracing us is an easy job for them. My opinion, we need to find a way to travel without disclosing our identity” blurted Stephen

Sanchez looked around as she crossed the street and continued, “You have a point, but more on this later. Let us plan to be in Nazaré at the earliest, hopefully without any more trouble. Also, now that you made your point, we should ditch the train."

Stephen was famished. His desperation to have a good meal was beyond comprehension. Past few days were nothing but feet on the streets and grabbing whatever came their way. While he was eager to suggest, he knew Sanchez was right.  With clear hesitation in his tone he tried to put his point forward, "Should we eat lunch before we head towards Nazaré?”

Sanchez rolled her eyes, she stopped walking and her hands naturally came and rested on her hip, with an appealing tone she blurted “Stephen! any moment Benedikt’s accomplice could be here. Soon they will know we dodged them. The best possible escape path was the train, which from the airport only comes to the City Hall – eventually, they would come looking for us here. I know you are starved, but you got to wait for few more hours. I am sorry.”

Stephen gave it a final try “What if we go to the waterfront. There are so many restaurants there and they could hardly find us?”

This time Sanchez had a smile on her face “You are an ingenuous soul. I know they will not hunt for us in the eateries but would surely guard all the city exit point. Let us not waste time. We need to be out of here soon.”

Stephen lost the battle and with dropped shoulders followed Sanchez, disgruntled and hungry. Leaving Lisbon was the utmost priority for her, and while she knew Stephen was famished she put a deaf ear to his request.

It took them another five minutes to reach the bus stand. Despite being popular with the tourist, the place had all signs in the Portuguese language. Fortunately for her, Portuguese was her third language in school. The next bus to Nazaré was in thirty minutes – something she dreaded not to see. Sanchez was nervous. She had a wild hunch that the ruffians would come looking for them any moment. Thirty minutes was long enough time for anyone to make up for the lost time.

Her preoccupied stature was in deep perturbation, weighing between options to circumvent the situation when a young boy interrupted her contemplations. With a sharp accent, he blurted in Portuguese “Você quer um autocarro privado para Nazaré.” (“Do you want a private transport to Nazaré.”)

It appeared as if the boy was God sent, the situation presented Sanchez a way to come out of her consternation. She tried not to look over excited as that might result in getting overcharged. With a calm stature, she responded to the boy in a small tone “Depende. Quanto é que você carrega: (“It depends. How much would you charge?”)

The boy sensed that he might have gotten a customer and led Sanchez to the other side of the stand. There were a dozen private cars parked in that narrow-crowded corridor, and a person in his early thirties guarded a small booth by the entrance. The boy rushed into the booth, provided a brief commentary and directed the person to Sanchez.

The person grinned showing his stained tobacco teeth “200 euros. Muito barato” (“200 euros. It is cheap.”)

Knowing the danger that was looming over their head it was a sweet deal, but she gave a gentle push to see if she could bring the price lower “muito caro - eu só posso pagar 150 euros (“too costly – I can only pay 150 euros.”)

The man pondered for a while and finally stated: “então você tem que compartilhar a van.” (“you have to share the van with others.”)

Sanchez was in no mood to take any risk – she made a counter offer of 170 euros and the deal was sealed.

Within five minutes they were on their way to Nazaré. Stephen was hungry, exhausted but still didn't fail to acknowledge Sanchez’s charisma and her ability to think on the feet. He knew if it weren’t for her, their fate would have been sealed unfavorably long time back.

**Chapter 25**

**12:00 PM 17th April 2014, Lisbon, Portugal**

Maxwell deplaned with extreme urgency. The morning rush of incoming planes was pouring in and the immigration lines were getting extended to long serpentine queue. He approached the official row, which was reserved for diplomats and government officials. There were only two people ahead of him and he went past the immigration in no time. Before leaving the secured zone, he gave a cursory glance at people waiting to clear immigration. He found both Sanchez and Stephen waiting in one of the regular long lines. There were about 15-20 people ahead of them and according to his estimate, it would easily take them another thirty minutes to clear immigration. He headed straight to the main terminal. The Lisbon airport was bustling with travelers, mostly tourist. April was a great time for visitors as the weather was ideal for vacationers and warm weather seekers. Also, since Lisbon was the main port of entry to Portugal most of the travelers initiated and concluded their journey here.

He looked around for any dubious person but found none.  As he walked across the waiting area his wary eyes caught some unusual activity. He noticed a visibly obese person scanning the arriving passengers in a discrete and surreptitious manner. Though he was some distance away he could tell the person resembled someone he knew from the past. To avoid any direct eye contact, he moved to the left and walked sideways so that he could be diagonally opposite to him. There was a coffee shop with a long queue of people waiting to get their mid-morning dose of caffeine. He joined the line and slowly inched forward. As he came close to the counter, he managed to get a good close sight of the suspect. His apprehensions were accurate. He knew the person from the past. His name was Francis and he was a close accomplice of Benedikt. He also noticed few of his men dispersed around, looking ever passerby with vigilant eyes. Each of them had a picture in their hand, which he was certain was of Sanchez.

Maxwell knew he had little time at his disposal to stop Sanchez from getting past the immigration line. He moved away from the line and started pacing towards the immigration area. It was grueling to refrain from running, but he managed to maintain his sauntered walk to avoid attracting any undue attention. The entrance to the immigration was at the left of the waiting area. As soon as he was out of the direct sight of the goons he dashed with all his might. He was by the restricted zone under a minute. So far, he hadn’t seen Sanchez. He was hoping either she was in the immigration area or waiting by the baggage area for the luggage. Regardless, the only thing that concerned him now was to stop them from going out to the arrivals. He knew it might defeat his plan, for he had to declare his presence to Sanchez. But that was least of his worry as for now keeping her out of Francis's trap was his utmost priority.

There was a young lady guarding the entrance to the immigration. She saw a person running with urgency towards her. She sprung to her feet and took her gun out of her waist holster. There was enough commotion for passersby to take notice of, as they observed with disbelieve and consternation to witness any untoward incidence. Maxwell was quick enough to flash his ID, had a quick word with her and went past the checkpoint. People were curious but soon faded away. 

He swiveled his way through baggage area to the immigration area.  The lines at the immigration counter were longer than before. His eyes scanned the lines and got fixated on the queue where he saw Sanchez and Stephen waiting not too long ago. His heart sank when he couldn’t see them anywhere. He scanned the remainder of the lines but couldn’t spot them there either. He ran his eyes over the place few more times, but there was no trace of them. Partially convinced that they might have cleared the immigration, he turned back to check the baggage area. He gave a quick glance at the display board to find the carousel number for the flight they were on and noted the belt number. He pushed his way through to the carousel. The bags were still pouring on to the belt and people were crowding the area looking for their belongings. He scanned the area few times but failed to locate them. He was perplexed, and his stature reflected uneasiness. He took few long strides to reach the waiting area again and was relieved to see Francis still waiting for Sanchez to arrive.

“How is this possible? Have I missed seeing them?” he mumbled. He searched all outlet but failed to find their trace. He knew he couldn’t have missed seeing them unless they were called upon for secondary checking. He decided to do a scan a second time. He paced back to the baggage area, and checked every avenue, but failed to spot them again.

He was bewildered. “How can they vanish? I was guarding the area like a hawk”, he mumbled. Confirmed they weren’t in the baggage area he dashed to the immigration zone. He performed a final thorough scan but failed to locate them again. His options were running out and he decided to do a formal inquiry.

He approached one of the immigration officers, showed his badge and stated, “The life of two people who arrived from Lisbon on TAP airlines this morning is in danger. I need your urgent help. Their names are Sanchez and Stephen Roberto. Can you please find out whether they went past the immigration?”

The immigration officer wasn’t amused, “You have to talk to the chief officer. I don’t have the authority to provide what you need.”

Maxwell was exasperated. Unfortunately, he had no other option but to meet the chief officer, with a shade of hesitation he blurted, “Can you please call upon him promptly. It is a matter of someone’s life and death.”

“This way”, the officer showed the path and sauntered with no urgency towards a bank of elevators. Maxwell almost dashed but the officer refused to speed up. The elevator took them to the third floor. The officer instructed him to wait outside, as he went past the door to get the chief officer. It was an excruciating five-minute long wait before the chief appeared. Maxwell wanted to confront on their incessant behavior but couldn’t do much besides repressing his anger.

“Someone’s life is in danger. I want your help in getting Sanchez and Stephen Roberto’s information at the earliest.” He managed to control his rage, but his taut voice couldn’t hide his infuriation.

The chief officer blurted, “We have some rules here. Can I see your ID? I need to verify your identity first?”

Maxwell annoyance was crossing all limits. “You can do all your checks later. If something happens to these people, it will not be good for anyone.” His voice showed signs of stridency.

The chief officer looked at him in an indignant way, and with a tight voice declared, “This area is under my jurisdiction and I define the rules. Follow them or you can leave.”

Maxwell knew arguing with this man wouldn’t help. He pulled out his identification and gave it to him. Though he was stifled, he had no option but to wait. The officer returned after five minutes and this time spoke in a soft tone, “I apologize, but as you would appreciate, we need to follow the rules. Now, to your question, they have cleared immigration about 20 minutes back. Hope this helps.”

Maxwell was flabbergasted. He couldn’t believe what he heard. He thanked them and rushed back to the waiting area. Francis and his accomplice were still looking for them. A sign of relief appeared on his face and he knew Sanchez had outsmarted not only Benedikt’s goons but him as well. It was an eventful morning and before he could decide what he wanted to do next he needed some respite from the mad rush. He grabbed a coffee, found a table and relished his drink. His plan was to see what Francis would do next. Besides he wouldn’t have missed the chance of seeing Francis’s expression, after he would learn the girl he was waiting for outwitted him on the land where he considered himself an undisputed king, at least until now.

**Chapter 26**

**2:30 PM 17th April 2014, Lisbon, Portugal**

The drive to Nazaré was uneventful.  Sanchez remained attentive throughout the ride, doubting every vehicle that trailed their car and observing every passing vehicle with irresolute eyes. Stephen was too famished to worry about any worldly distractions. Besides, he trusted his companion's vigilance, and in a most nonchalant way went for a short siesta. They were in Nazaré late afternoon. The driver dropped them at the town square.

“I know you are hungry, but you have to wait for some more time. I am sorry, but I cannot take any chances. Let us walk out of this place and get a taxi from the street.” Sanchez avoided any eye contact while making her point. Stephen was too tired to rejoinder and followed her out to the street. The late afternoon breeze was making the sunrays soothing and calm. The vast white sand beaches expanding the horizons were outlining a breathtaking view. Tourists swarmed the streets, and everyone seemed to be in a blithe mood, but not the Roberto’s. They were in Nazaré for a purpose. The scintillating views and the bohemian atmosphere couldn’t distract them.

Nazaré being a tourist destination, taxis were in abundance. They hailed one and instructed the driver to head towards Alcobaça, which was in the northeastern part of Nazaré, and with deep anxiousness waited to exit the place.

It was a short trip. They got off near an upscale restaurant. The evening sun was still glowing, scattering its hue across the wide horizon. Stephen was famished, but not angry. He wanted to make sure Sanchez was aware of that. He forced a smile and with a sotto voice whispered, “If it wasn’t for you we would have been hungry and tortured."

Sanchez couldn’t help from having a good laugh and approached the restaurant with a buoyant spirit. Despite the early evening hour, the restaurant was buzzing with people, giving a strong endorsement of a healthy spring tourist onslaught in this small town of Portugal. The hostess was a charming young girl, dressed in short jeans and a white shirt, which had Cabeco embossed.

Her eyes crinkled and with a beaming smile she spoke “Madam, Onde você prefere sentar-se, dentro ou for a.” (“Where would you prefer to sit, outside or inside.”)

Sanchez smiled and spoke in Portuguese “Dentro seria ótimo. De preferência, um estande com um pouco de privacidade.” (“Inside would be great. Preferably a booth with some privacy.”)

“Certo. Por favor, siga -me desta maneira.” (“Sure. Please follow me this way.”) and she led them inside.

They settled in a booth, which was diagonally opposite to where the guest entrance was located. This gave Sanchez a good view of the people entering the restaurant, allowing her to watch for any intruder without inviting any undue attention. The ambiance of the restaurant was pleasant, well supported by its modern décor. Located on top of a hill, the restaurant had a picturesque view of the region. Nature was kind to this part of the world. With lush green hilltop covered with wildflowers and the vastness of the Atlantic Ocean, it was an elegant sight. The occasional goats and horses grazing the land down below was a scintillating view. Inside the restaurant, there was a live band playing the traditional Portuguese 15th-century Fado music. Keeping the tradition of the Fado alive, there was a solo singer well supported by two people with a wire-strung acoustic guitar. Fado express sadness, but the version performed by the band hardly reflected any glumness. Indisputably, it was a modified version to meet the audience need but was done tastefully preserving the local heritage.

They ordered a bottle of Douro Red, one of the best Portuguese dry wines. Stephen settled for an Acorda shrimp and Sanchez ordered cod in olive oil, the place was famous for seafood, like any good restaurant in this ocean-facing village. It was quarter past eight when they concluded their dinner, looking contempt and regaled. Sanchez was relieved, for no untoward event occurred since they left the City Hall. She was convinced this time she had successfully evaded her pursuers. But despite the confidence, she didn't put her vigilance guards off. Her eyes still searched the area with inquisitiveness and vigor.

It was time to ditch their fancy clothes. Both headed to the restroom and came back appearing like tourists. Sanchez was wearing a white Capri, a flaxen colored shirt, and a bandana. Stephen wore a jean and a polo T-Shirt. Both were wearing beach sandals. Soon they walked past the restaurant towards the main marketplace. The evening breeze made the environment much cooler and pleasant. The streets were still crowded with tourist, mostly shopping or heading out for a good nightlife.

One of Stephen’s close friend Jules was in Nazaré. As part of an exchange program, Jules was in Ayr for about six months. In one of his countryside exploration trip, he was in Stephen’s village where both met and became good friends. He kept visiting Stephen during his stay at Ayr. Jules specialized in cartography. It was Stephen’s idea to take Jules help to decipher the map. Sanchez was skeptical in the beginning, but Stephen succeeded in coercing her to believe him. For he was certain Jules could keep secrets.

Stephen hadn’t got a chance to let Jules know about his arrival.  Jules was expecting them but was unsure when they would arrive. Sanchez had strict instructions to keep the phones off. If Jules had tried calling them he wouldn’t have succeeded. Stephen didn't want to appear unannounced and decided to give him a call. He spotted a public phone booth and dialed the number. Soon Jules was on the line.  After exchanging pleasantries Jules offered to pick them up. Stephen resisted but resigned on Jules’s consistent insistence. Jules needed about thirty minutes and asked them to meet him at a close by pub.

For the first time that evening Sanchez appeared a little comforted. While Stephen was on the phone she strolled across the street to the wooden benches by the beach corridor. She sat on an empty bench and gazed blankly at the wide-open ocean. The crashing sound of the waves was an integral part of nature’s calmness, and she was lost in nature's captivity.

Stephen walked out of the booth, and to his horror, he couldn’t locate Sanchez anywhere. He was perplexed, agitated and scared, all at the same time. Many untoward perturbations crossed his mind, and with every passing minute, he started to lose his plot. He frantically started to look around and after about five minutes of incredulous search, he found her sitting on a bench right across the street. He was relieved to see her, but his anger refused to subside. “I have been looking for you all over the place. You could have at least told me before coming here.” His tone was a shade below screaming.

Sanchez didn’t react and kept gazing the vast openness. After a brief pause she broke the silence, “Stephen, come sit beside me. This place is so pristine, so beautiful. You need to experience this.”

He looked away to observe what his sister was trying to explain. He was instantly drawn to the quietude that resided in the emptiness of the night. Unlike the streets, which, were swarming with tourists, there was no visible soul near the water. The only noticeable sound was the crashing waves and the occasional creeks of the seagulls. He sat beside her and with a soft tone mentioned about his conversation with Jules. The talks receded, and they kept to themselves as if agreeing to an unstated protocol to stay by the ocean for as long as time would permit.

**\**

**Chapter 27**

**1:30 PM 17th April 2014, Lisbon, Portugal**

Maxwell was on his second cup of coffee when he saw Francis huddling with his team. From his delirious appearance, it was evident he was aware of Sanchez’s disappearance. Soon Francis's men spread out in different directions. A couple of them went towards the metro station, few rushed to the arrival gate and Francis accompanied with two of his accomplices rushed to the baggage area. It took him a while to convince the security officer before he got access to the restricted zone. After about twenty minutes he came back to the waiting area. He looked flummoxed and wandered like a lost soul, unsure and clueless. Maxwell couldn’t resist from smiling and enjoyed every moment of Francis’s despondency. Francis spent few more minutes in the waiting area running helter-skelter in his last ditched attempt to locate Sanchez. After some time of scurrying around, he realized his worst fear was a reality now. Without further ado, he dashed to the departure gate. A black Mercedes van was waiting for him. Soon he disappeared behind the sliding door, and the van zoomed past with a screeching noise.

Maxwell sprinted behind Francis but with caution. He had pre-arranged a car to follow Sanchez, but ironically his plan had altered. He tapped a number on his phone and soon his car appeared at the departure gate. He took the front seat next to the driver and instructed him to go after the van. The man behind the wheels was a police officer, he was a local and he traced the van's path with utmost discretion.

The traffic at the airport was thinning down as most of the morning flights had landed. It was past noon and the sun was shining brightly in this westernmost city of continental Europe. The wagon was crisscrossing the traffic, driving hastily, trying to find its way out from the airport terminal in the shortest possible timeframe. Their car was behind a couple of cars from the van and trod its path without much urgency, for the van was big and was clearly visible from distance. After about few minutes of maneuvering, the wagon took the A12 highway ramp. Their car followed through and got on to the motorway. Once on the highway, the van took the extreme left lane and drove at a high speed. The motorway was densely populated with vehicles, enough for Maxwell to take the shield. The van kept cruising for twenty minutes before taking the exit towards highway N4 and soon entered the parking lot of Montijo private terminal. The traffic at the entrance of the airport was sparse, Maxwell instructed the officer to head straight on N4 to avoid any direct confrontation with Francis.

He was confident of Francis’s location and had a luxury of taking a loop before entering the airport. They drove ahead for some time before turning around to head towards the private airport terminal.  He was aware of Benedikt’s flying only private jets and was certain that Francis was there to receive him. The officer took the car to the nearest gas station and got off the car.

Maxwell gave himself ten minutes before heading to the terminal. It was a small airport and the parking area could easily accommodate few dozen cars. The airport had only one runway, which was surrounded by a barbed wire fence. As he turned on to the terminal he saw the van on the tarmac. He parked the car in the furthest lane, switched off the engine and waited. 

**\**

**Chapter 28**

**1:30 PM 17th April 2014, Lisbon, Portugal**

Francis was enraged. He couldn't fathom Sanchez had evaded and remained in a state of disbelieve. His earlier hunch led him to presume she hadn’t boarded the plane. But when the immigration officer told him about her departure, his residual dwindling thoughts vanished. 

 “How is this possible? I was right there and had checked every passing person!!” His voice reflected deep frustration and his demeanor implausible, refusing to accept the situation. He knew they couldn’t have gone too far and the only way to salvage the damage was by demonstrating agility.  He called upon his men and spread them out to every outlet. Benedikt was about to arrive in forty minutes at Montijo airport. Though he was not required to receive him, he decided otherwise. He wanted to be the first person giving the bad news. It was a tough decision, he dreaded to face Benedikt's wrath, but he knew it was the right thing to do.

It was a short drive to Montijo airport. He took the van straight to the tarmac, parked at the edge of the runway and stormed out with urgency. The executive receiving area was a small glass building. He went past the swinging doors and approached the main reception area.  The place looked deserted. There was a lady guarding the reception desk. He offered her the plane tail number and enquired about the arrival time of Benedikt's plane. The plane was in the vicinity and was about to land.  Before the receptionist could finish her sentence, Francis was gone. He marched out of the door back to the tarmac area.

With every passing minute, he was morphing into a disgruntled being. “What would I tell him?” he mumbled. He knew upsetting Benedikt wasn’t good for his business. Benedikt had the power, the authority to dethrone him and make him insignificant. He was frightened thinking about the consequences.  The only way he could resurrect was by finding the girl, provided she was still in Lisbon. He had put his entire workforce on the job. They were guarding the city exits; scanning information of all travel tickets bought in the past hour and checking all nearby hotels. So far there was no success. How he wished he could get hold of her before Benedikt's arrival.

The jet landed with a screeching reverberation making Francis jump out of his skin. The plane landed few hundred meters away from him. He made some last-minute frantic calls with the hope of finding some answers, but it was nothing but disappointment. Knowing he cannot better the situation, he got back into the van and instructed the driver to move the van closer to the area where the jet would be parked soon.

The jet came to a halt at its designated apron. Slowly the engine noise receded back to normalcy. The jet ladder unfolded and within a minute Benedikt appeared on the jet bridge. Following him was Damian carrying a small bag. Benedikt was sporting a dark pair of glasses, which concealed any trickle of emotions from any puerile guessing. The strong demeanor of Benedikt was making Francis squirm, and beads of sweat rolled down his temple. He had no clue how to announce the news. How would he disclose that the girl he was instructed to nab was on the loose, and worse, he had no clue about her whereabouts?

Benedikt came straight to the point, “Didn’t we plan to meet at our usual place? I hope you don’t have any bad news to announce.”

Francis' throat went dry and for some miserable long seconds, he failed to speak. With great difficulty, he somehow found his lost voice and announced, “Chief, we lost her. I was personally at the airport and looked for every passing passenger. I don’t know how we missed catching her sight. I ….”

Benedikt cut him in between, and his voice trailed to oblivion. Francis was gazing the ground, he had no courage left to face his boss.

Benedikt rage was visible in his screeching voice, “I gave you one job Francis and you screwed it. I told you how important this was for me. A twenty-something girl snoops from right under your nose and all you can offer is news of her disappearance. Shame on you and you know what, I am better off without you useless baboons. You all are a bunch of inept people I have as a part of my defunct team."

Though Francis speaking rights were curtailed, he still mustered some courage and spoke in a low tone, “I have my men by all the city exits and if they are in Lisbon they will be caught."

Benedikt lost his temper; he threw his hand in the air and with a grating sound blurted, “I don’t want anything from you. Do me a favor, stay out of it, Will you?”

Benedikt left Francis in complete shackles. He pulled out his phone and dialed a number. His phone was answered promptly. And he walked away conversing. Soon he was far away, and Francis could barely hear a word.

He was left with Damian alone. With great effort, he managed to glance at Damian. Damian greeted him with a tight-lipped smile and broke the long impeding strenuous silence between the two, “I know what happened was terrible, but if it may console you a bit, this girl had deceived all of us, including the chief. We are on the run for the last four days and she had dodged us multiple times. I must tell you, she is no ordinary girl. She is well trained and knows what she is doing. I know chief is angry now, but he will come around." Damian squeezed Francis’s shoulder as he finished his monologue draped with an emphatic outlook. Francis didn’t say anything, but passed a benign smile, appreciating his friend’s solicitude.

It took Benedikt some time to finish the conversation. He paced back to where Francis and Damian were standing. This time he had a more composed outlook than he was a few moments back. “Did your men find anything?” The voice was flat and there was no reflection of the frustration he showed before.

Francis responded in haste “Nothing so far, the exits are guarded. They will call me as soon as they find something."

Benedikt continued, “They must be in disguise. I am positive that’s how they escaped you. Tell your men to look under the skin. They wouldn’t appear like the way they appear in the photo”. Francis realized Damian was right on both the counts; Benedikt had calmed down, which was such a big relief, and this girl was no ordinary. He stepped aside as he called his men instructing them in Portuguese about what his chief had told him.

Benedikt continued upon Francis return, “I have laid out a plan, if she takes the bait, we may have a long and adventurous night. In the meantime, let us get some rest.”

They all jumped into the van and started their onward journey. Benedikt reclined his seat and tried to get some sleep. The creeping eerie in the van was rattled by the ringing tone of Benedikt’s phone. Benedikt came to complete attention. He was keenly listening without uttering a single word. The call went on for five minutes and finally, he spoke, “Give him some time. I am sure soon you will have some good news."

He snapped the phone and laid back on the seat with the hope of getting some respite from the proceedings of the chaotic day.

**\**

**Chapter 29**

**8:30 PM 17th April 2014, Nazaré, Portugal**

Sanchez was lost in her thoughts, so was Stephen and draped in the shroud of silence they searched for their own solicitude. It was about time and Stephen took the initiative to break the long-standing lull, “Jules should be here any moment. Let us walk towards the pub.”

Sanchez smiled through her crinkled eyes and followed Stephen’s lead. It was a short walk to the pub. Sanchez was amazed to note the stark difference between the two locations. The ocean was so calm and placid, whereas the pub was bustling with the raucous crowd.  The occasional loud music was pouring out of the pub as people were opening and closing the door, nothing unusual for a tourist location. On a normal day, she would have loved the ambiance, but the recent tribulations she had undergone demanded her to be more imperturbable.

The wait wasn’t for long. Sanchez saw a 6 feet tall structure approaching them from across the street. His unkempt dark hair was flowing as he walked, and his sun-kissed skin was glowing even in the night. He had a well-toned physique, evident from the bulging biceps and chest through the body-hugging t-shirt he wore. He shouted in joy from the distance with a full-bodied tone, which added to his endowed Greek like appearance.

He hugged Stephen tightly, exhibiting genuine warmth. Jules was meeting Sanchez for the first time, but that hardly constrained him from greeting her with the same enthusiasm as he showed to Stephen. His loud cordial demeanor took Sanchez by surprise, exuding an authentic comportment as if she was meeting someone she knew for long. 

"Why is your phone not working? I tried it so many times without any success.” Jules put forward an innocent question as they sauntered towards the car.

Without giving anything away Stephen declared, “Well our phones don’t have roaming and we wanted to keep our cost low." They all grinned. Jules continued “I can get you a local number for real cheap, twenty pesos for seven days, that way at least you can receive my calls.”

Sanchez finally spoke “That seems like a great idea. Why didn’t it come to my mind before?”

Jules couldn’t resist from blurting “Irmã that is because of Stephen's influence. When you meet, smart people like me ideas start to flow” They laughed their heart out and Sanchez knew for certain Jules could be trusted.

Throughout the way Jules kept them entertained. He chatted without stopping, sometimes blurting out silly jokes and at times becoming a tourist guide, giving details of the places they were passing by. With him, there was no dull moment. Jules house was about five KM off from the Nazaré main street, in a village called Valado dos Frades. Sanchez could see the vast paddy fields in the basking light of the clear moon sky. Driving through the single lane narrow road she could see the occasional farmhouses appearing far off from the road confirmed by the distinct dim light, which stood out in the openness. The weather welcomed them to roll down the windows, and the warm wind was gently soothing their being. The place looked deserted, rightfully so as it was past nine pm and most of the families would have gone to bed. The monotonous hum of Jules car’s engine was occasionally given company by some passing vehicle. Towards the end of their journey, Sanchez shut her mind off, undeterred by the constant chatting of her companions. She enjoyed the pristine calmness the village offered, drifting her away from her rueful trepidations. Besides, the two friends were meeting after two years and had a lot to share, something Sanchez could easily give a pass.

Jules took a left on a muddy outlet and veered off the main road towards his house. The road was bumpy, to say the least. Jules warned them to hold tight to whatever they could find. He drove for another five minutes, with their body shaking with every jerk it appeared the longest five minutes of their journey thus far. The house welcomed them with an open gate. It had two distinct pillars, but no gate to guard the house. The corridor was several hundred feet long. They drove through it for a while before they could get a clear sight of the house.

The bright moonlight coupled with the glare of the lonely bulb at the porch gave a clear view of the small yet beautiful farmhouse. The tall walls were draped in layers of white stucco complimented well with the contrasted terracotta tiled red roofing. A four feet structure made of local stones retained the vernacular essence of the place, which surrounded quarter of the house. Sanchez was awestruck by the petite beauty as they picked their bags and traversed through the front door into the house.

The interiors of the house were kept simple, less crowded with furniture and spotlessly clean. The house had a refreshing aura and was welcoming. The door opened to a living area, which, was well lit.  There were few windows in the room opening to the exterior. The edifices were designed using bricks in a crisscross diamond shaped fashion and were painted in blue color. The weather permitted them to be open all season, though it had a plastic drape mostly to avoid rain from coming inside. The living room opened to a kitchen to the far right. In the center of the room was a wooden sitting area, arranged in a two by two row with table neatly fitting between the rows of chairs. There was a single light casting from the center of the roof and the walls were graced with few Portuguese local decors.

Jules lived with his mother. As they entered the house he announced, “Mãe must be asleep, you will meet her in the morning. Are you guys hungry?”

Stephen smiled and told him that they had dinner at Main Street. Jules continued, “Care for a local glass of wine?”

“Thank you. We will retire for the night. We have a lot to accomplish tomorrow.” Sanchez tried to conclude the conversation and passed a close-mouthed smile.

Jules refrained from offering any further small talk and showed them their room. It was a small, but a well-kept room with two cots and a cupboard. The room also had an attached bathroom.

“We rent this place during tourist season. Fortunately, the couple living here left this morning. Have a good night and I will see you in the morning.”

It was a long day and soon they were fast asleep. For a welcome change, they weren’t disturbed by any nightmares, they were too tired to accommodate any dreams.

**Chapter 30**

**4:30 PM 17th April 2014, Lisbon, Portugal**

The Jet landed with a roaring sound. Maxwell had a clear sight of the plane from his car and he sat holding onto his curious mind. The jet ladder unfolded and Benedikt climbed down to the open tarmac. Benedikt’s longtime security head, Damian also walked out of the plane. " Looks like the entire team is here to find a girl”, he mumbled, and a vicious smile crossed his lips.

He saw them breaking into a conversation and soon Benedikt was throwing his hands in the air. "Perhaps Benedikt was made aware of Sanchez disappearance”, he thought and couldn't refrain from smiling imagining the unpleasant chinwag they may be undergoing. The discussion soon turned into an animated dialogue. Though he could hear the raised voice, it was not loud enough to get any sense out of it. Benedikt's anger was at the extreme. After few minutes of heated dialogue followed by a phone call, they got into the van and headed out of the airport. It was about time for Maxwell to commence another car chase. He knew he had to be careful chasing these seasoned criminals. In the event, he gets caught the consequences could be grave, for Francis could commit any crime in Portugal and could walk away unscathed.

The road connecting the airport and the main highway was a narrow two-way traffic lane. There were no other vehicles except the van on the road. Maxwell had no choice, but to wait, and soon the van drifted off his sight.  After few minutes of strenuous wait, Maxwell saw another car going in the same direction.  He drove right behind the other car using it as a shield. The car in front was moving at a decent speed, helping him to gain some of the lost ground. The road was a long stretch in the middle of an open field with no man-made or natural obstructions around. He was able to get a clear sight of the van even when he was behind a few kilometers. After about ten minutes into the drive, he saw the van making a right turn. The sign on the side of the road indicated the turn would take them to highway A33. After the pivot, the two-way traffic road became a four-lane road. Maxwell moved his car to the middle lane and sped-up slightly to make on the lost time. As the highway was approaching the road was getting busier, which he used it to his advantage. He blended with the traffic and followed the van taking extreme caution.

Benedikt wasn’t in a hurry. There wasn’t even a shade of urgency he demonstrated while driving away from the airport. Soon the van took the ramp to enter A33 south. Maxwell followed them to the highway. He kept a comfortable distance from the van and followed his target with caution. After about 20 minutes the van took the exit towards Vale da Amoreria. Soon they turned on an unnamed street or at least there was no visibly appearing name to the street, and shortly after that, it stopped in front of a dilapidated dwelling. With tall grasses and unruly weeds around, the house looked abandoned.

Maxwell avoided going into the lane, instead, he took his car few hundred meters ahead and took a turn on to the next street. It was a one-way narrow lane squeezing between two tall buildings. As he drove few meters forward he saw a parking lot on the left, stretching out from where the building ended to further north. There was a man guarding the gate. He took a parking ticket and paced his way back to where the van was stationed.

He found the van parked outside the house. The place appeared deserted. There wasn’t a single soul visible around the property. He stood by the corner of the street hiding behind the walls of a three-storied building. He had a plan to execute but wanted to take some time before working on it. He monitored the house for another five minutes and couldn’t find anything alarming. While the evening was slowly crawling in, there was still about an hour left for dusk to arrive. Maxwell was contemplating whether he should wait for the darkness to prevail or should he go ahead and get done with the job. Undoubtedly approaching the van in open light was a risky affair, but the fear of Benedikt leaving the house was making him unsure. He weighed his options and decided to wait for some more time.

While waiting Maxwell played the whole event back on his mind. It was puzzling for him to see Benedikt reposing when Sanchez was still on the loose. From what he knew about Benedikt he wouldn’t relax until he had his hand on Sanchez. Either they had found her or there exists a scheme that he wasn’t aware of. It was crucial for him to execute his plan, the only way he would have any chance to learn about Benedikt’s motive. Maxwell waited for another thirty minutes. There was no change in the outlook. The van stood in an unceremonious way and no one came out of the house either. The sun was spreading its hue across the horizon, indicating its plan to retire for the day. The shades of the tall building were slowly covering the streets, spreading its grayness across the van and the house. It was dark enough and he decided to move forward with his plan.

He came out of his hiding and marched towards the house.  The street by large was quiet, besides few people walking by returning home from work. Soon he joined the sparse pedestrian traffic and reached the house in few hundred steps. He gave a good look at the worn-out dwelling as he went past it to the next street intersection.  The house was at the end of the street, which gave him a good advantage to curve around the street and he waited for the right time to launch his plan. He checked his bag to ensure he had the transmitter with him. He turned it on and made sure it was functioning.  The transmitter also had a built-in microphone, which could intercept the voices and relay it back.  For receiving the voices from the car, it needed to be installed inside the car. It wasn’t an easy job, as it would involve getting access to the car. Though he had a set of keys, he wasn’t sure how long would it take to open the van door. He put the gadgets back into his small backpack, took a final view of the street and started walking towards the van.

The van was parked on the left side of the house. The porch was away from the house entrance. Maxwell knew if he were on the passenger door side of the van, people from the house wouldn’t be able to see him unless they come really close to the van. The height of the van was tall enough to hide him from any prying eyes. He looked on both sides of the street and found a person walking towards the van from the other side of the lane. He wasn’t worried as he was facing away from the street, hiding from the direct view of the pathway. He grabbed the passenger side door handle and worked on opening it. He tried the first key, but the door stood like a piece of steel. He tried the second one, but it refused to buzz. The person on the other side was fast approaching. He had time to check one more key before the person would go pass the house. He was about to try the third key when he heard a creaking sound as if someone was opening a door. Before he could react, he heard footsteps approaching towards the van. He had a split-second reaction time and the only thing he could manage to do was to slip under the van. The van had a good ground clearance, and he clung to the exhaust pipe holding his breath. The van engine was shut for some time and the exhaust pipe was at a bearable temperature. As he wrapped around the exhaust he noticed his bag dangling low from his back. Anyone with a good pair of eyes could easily spot the bag from distance. Before he could do anything, he saw two people approaching the driver side door. He could see their feet few meters away from him. He wanted to avoid any circumstantial misfortune and decided to get the bag off his back. While keeping the leg looped around the exhaust he let go one of his hand as he slowly tried to get one of the straps of backpack slid down. The strap on the loose hand came out with few tries. Half of the bag was touching the ground and the other half dangling on the other shoulder. With extreme quietness, he repeated it for the other hand, the bag slowly came to the ground. He flicked it and kept it on top of him, above the exhaust with his hands wrapped around it.

The men were talking in Portuguese. Maxwell had no clue what they were discussing, but he could make out that they were having a serious conversation. Soon he heard the unlocking beep sound of the van. They opened the front door. It appeared they were looking for something. One of them was still standing outside the van. Maxwell was relieved to note they weren’t taking the van away. After few minutes they locked the car and started to walk back to the house.

A large crashing sound made Maxwell's heart skip few beats. He saw a bunch of documents falling around the car. Not what Maxwell wanted, but at the same time he was glad that he got his bag out of the way. They got to the ground and started collecting the scattered documents. Fortunately for Maxwell, they didn’t spot him. They collected the papers with haste and hurried back inside.

Maxwell was relieved to see them going. He was dripping in sweat from head to toe. He wasn’t sure whether he was perspiring because of the hot weather or for the commotion he went through.  He hung in his position for another few minutes and when he found everything back to normalcy, he slipped down the pipe and found his way back to the passenger side door.

He was shocked to see the key bunch hanging from the van door key knob. His frustration was at the extreme, “How could I do this?” he spoke softly. Without getting too distracted with his mishaps, he went back to his job. The evening shadow crowded the space and there was hardly any movement on the street either. After about seven tries he finally heard the snapping sound, the door gave in to his persistence.  He put the transmitter underneath the glove box in the lowermost corner. He checked it twice and ensured it was out of sight for anyone to notice. Satisfied with his work he swiftly came out of the van, locked the door and paced out of the area as fast as humanly possible without raising any alarms. 

The evening was closing in fast and Maxwell had an intuition that it will be a long night. He was content with his achievement and was eager to have Benedikt and his accomplice back in the van so that he could eavesdrop on their plan and accordingly decide on his next course of action. Once he was back in his car he put the transmitter-receiver on. The receiver was giving away a blank tone and a constant GPS position of the house. He was happy to see the gadget working.

He had one more chore to take care before he could finally get some respite. He dialed the number of the police officer who met him at the airport and helped him with the car. After few rings, the officer answered the phone. The two men spoke for about five minutes. They concluded their conversation with an agreement to talk again in about an hour. Maxwell decided to get dinner. There was an Asian joint right by the street where he was parked. He entered the restaurant with the hope that the transmitter doesn’t come to life at least for the next one hour.

**Chapter 31**

***8:00 AM 18<sup>th</sup> April 2014, Valado dos Frades, Portugal***

The village church bell rang with a loud peal ruffling the quietness of the morning. The bell chimed with persistence, waking Sanchez from her deep slumber. It took her few minutes to comprehend her location. The curtains on the windows were drawn, hiding every outlet, making the room pitch dark. She took her time adjusting to the darkness. Once she got the lay of the room she reached for the table light, and with some resistance, she turned it on. The ringing of the bells had no effect on Stephen, and he remained in repose. She regretted losing time sleeping, something she couldn’t afford knowing their life was in a constant peril. Deserting her morning laziness, she lowered the curtains and the room was filled with bright sunlight. She was about to turn around when the view from the window caught her attention. She had never seen such a panoramic sight before. The house was on top of a hill and from the window, all she could see was grape vines in vast openness. The thin line of the shores far below appeared as if it met the horizon. There was some occasional chirping of the birds, adding life to the serene abode. On the left side of the valley were the sporadic spread of old Portuguese style houses in no order. All of them draped in some distinct shade of white with red brick roofs. Down below the valley, she saw the church bell tower standing tall in peace and calmness.

The vast exquisite spectacle froze Sanchez, and she forgot the promise she made to herself not too long ago of not wasting time. She was nudged away from her emptiness by Stephen’s gentle tap on her shoulders. It seemed the morning glare piercing through the window eventually stirred him from his sleep. Before he could say anything, he saw the welcoming sight of nature’s ingenuity, and like Sanchez, he too was spellbound and stood there awestruck.

“Let us get freshen up. We have a lot to do” Sanchez announced as she took her eyes away from the view and sauntered towards the bathroom.

Soon they were in the living room. Jules was sitting by the small dinette in the kitchen area chit-chatting with his mother in Portuguese. Jules mother appeared to be in her late-fifties. She was a short woman, stoically built, draping a traditional bright colored bouffant long skirt with a shining red kerchief embracing her bosoms.

“Come to the kitchen”, Jules shouted as he saw them in the living room. Jules mother had a broad grin on her face, “você slepp bem.” *(“Did you sleep well?”) *she spoke to her guest in Portuguese. Sanchez felt the warmth in Jules mother’s voice. She responded in Portuguese that they had a good night sleep. Jules’s mother was delighted to learn Sanchez could converse in Portuguese and invited them to sit on the small dinette. Jules mother put a lavish spread on the table. She had coffee, bread toasts, fruits, boiled eggs and the traditional “sopas de cavalo cansado”, red wine with old breadcrumbs. According to the lady, it provides great strength to face the day. They devoured the sumptuous meal. It was a while since they had a home cooked meal and they enjoyed it wholeheartedly.  It was time to get to execute on their plan. 

Jules study looked like the den of a historian. The walls were covered with maps from all spheres. The room had one desk which had a small display screen connected to a desktop computer. On one side of the table, a Mac was running some CAD software with a European political 3D map. Right next to the desk was a couch, occupied with various instruments a professional cartographer uses – plotters, beams, large rulers, magnifying glasses and other drawing and engraving instruments. Jules was known for his work in Portugal. He also worked in the capacity of a historiographer for the local Discovery channel.

They had to move things around to find space to sit. Sanchez put her phone to life and showed Jules the map pictures.  Jules looked at the images with keen interest. After spending some time observing the maps, he connected the phone to the computer. The enlarged images of the map appeared on the large screen. He searched for a pen and a paper from the pile of books and other materials scattered around the room. After some determined hunt, he found what he was looking for. And soon he was in business, lost in his own world. He scribbled some notes as he observed the map with great interest.

For the next one hour, he remained engrossed in his work. He switched between the map image that Sanchez had provided, and some of his own maps on the computer, all this while scribbling in a relentless manner on the notepad. They watched the genius at work with awe and hope. After a while of gazing, referring maps and deliberations he spoke, “This map is cryptic. I believe I got most of it, but there are things you cannot figure out only by looking at the map” He turned around, smiled and for the first time since they entered the room looked away from the map. Both Sanchez and Stephen were anxious to find what Jules might have learned from the map.

Jules waved at them, asking them to come closer. He spread his notebook sheet he had scribbled on the table and continued, “This is a secret pathway map, designed to work on a strict schedule and timeline” Sanchez and Stephen had no clue what he was talking about. They were giving him a patient hearing hoping to get accustomed to the lingo of a cartographer.

Jules was too involved with his findings to notice the puzzled looks on his friends’ faces. He continued, “Do you see the small dots across the map – these are the places that someone will travel to. Now there is an interesting color pattern on the dots.” There was excitement in his voice as he pointed to the map. They could see the colorful dots and they equivocally expressed agreement on what he was pointing at.

“I believe this indicates the day of the week. There are only 7 colors and they are repeating in a synchronized pattern. Whoever is starting the voyage, starts in three days from now or might be three months from now when the days of the week match with what is coded in the map. My hunch is more inclining towards three days from now.”

“How do you know that?” Stephen curiosity had no limits and his voice echoed the sentiments.

“I knew this was coming.”

He passed a mischievous smile and momentarily took his gaze away from the computer to look at Stephen.

“When you zoom the small dots to some enormous size, like this, you can see a number. The first dot had a number 7, which is exactly three days from now. If you follow the pattern you will see the number either staying the same or changing, I took the average travel time by both sea and road and its kind of sets well with the pattern.”

Sanchez was glad she listened to her brother and made the trip. Jules was on a roll, “Most likely the first part of the journey is by sea. You must be wondering how I can tell that with confidence. Well, if you carefully see the first few places in the journey map, they are all major seaports.” He pointed to the map and continued, “The journey starts at Tallinn in Estonia. From Tallinn heading to Ventspils in Latvia and then to Gdynia in Poland.”

By now Sanchez was captivated, deeply engrossed listening to every piece of information Jules was stating.

“From there, likely the journey will continue by road. As the next stop, Warsaw is in the center of Poland surrounded by land. The trip is ending in Belarus in a city called Viciebsk. If you know a little about the Eastern Europe geography you will notice that Viciebsk borders Russia and the border between Belarus and Russia is porous. Traffic can easily travel in and out without any border security check. So, my guess is Russia has an important part to play. That’s all I have.”

Both Sanchez and Stephen were astounded. They had looked at the map multiple times but couldn’t figure a thing. And it was implausible to notice how Jules had read the map. It appeared as if he was reading a script, a book detailing all the facts.

“We need to destroy all the evidence except for the phone images. It will be a good idea to memorize the information. Let us take some time to go over it and memorize by heart” Sanchez looked at Stephen to get his agreement.

Jules took a brief test on their knowledge and it was time to destroy the evidence. The photos on Jules machine were deleted and the paper he had scribbled were shredded. They thanked Jules, hugged him and decided to leave. Jules was keen to stop them for lunch, but he knew they had little time and a lot to accomplish. He gave them a ride to the main street and bid them goodbye.

Sanchez had one more job left before leaving Portugal. They grabbed a quick lunch from a street vendor before heading to the other side of Nazaré.

**Chapter 32**

**7:30 PM 17th April 2014, Lisbon, Portugal**

It was half-past seven when Maxwell walked out of the restaurant. The GPS had no movement confirming Benedikt hadn't left the house yet. With the passing time, his perturbations were at a gradual rise. He looked at the receiver irresolute and torn. Laced with doubts he decided to check the house to ensure he wasn’t reeling on false hope and drove through the street where Benedikt was sheltered. He was relieved to find the van still parked on the porch.  The house lights were on and he was certain Benedikt had no immediate plan to leave the place.

Even though Sanchez evaded him at the airport he wasn’t worried, for he knew where she was headed. The only reason why he decided to go after Francis was to ensure he could ahead of Benedikt's game plan.  A while back he had sent his police officer friend to guard the place where he was expecting her. However, until that moment she was nowhere near that place. With Benedikt insouciant behavior and no confirmed news on Sanchez's location, Maxwell concerns were growing with every passing minute. He dreaded the thought of Sanchez trapped by Francis's men, and his only hope was Benedikt, for sooner or later when Benedikt would decide to leave, he could follow his lead. But Benedikt was still there, blasé, resting unruffled in the obscured dilapidated house.

He had no option but to wait. Taking some rest was the best way forward for him, and he decided to get a hotel for the night. He found a small motel couple of miles north of where Benedikt house was located.  Keeping the transmitter's receiver next to him he retired for the night with the hope it would wake him the moment the van inches forward.

 

**Chapter 33**

**12:30 PM 18th April 2014, Nazaré, Portugal**

Sanchez was looking for a public phone. She now had a local cell number but refrained from using it.  She had enough problems to deal with and had no desire to add any more to her list of worries. After few minutes of wayward walking, she spotted a telephone booth right across a desolated bus stop.  She dialed a number and waited for a response.

A lady’s disembodied dead tone came to life “Quem é esse?” (“Who is it?”)

“Am I speaking with Adriana?"

"Sim."("Yes")

I am Sanchez Roberto. I come with the reference of John Patterson. I am in Nazaré. Can I come and meet you?” She chose her words carefully in Portuguese.

The lady paused for a while and stated in a flat tone “Está bem. Venha aqui” (“Ok. Come over.”)

Sanchez was a little disconcerted, for the lady on the phone sounded a tad indifferent. The way John had portrayed Adriana, she was expecting a much warmer response. With a hesitant mind, she sauntered out of the booth perplexed. Stephen sensed her anxiety and with a feeble tone enquired, “Everything ok?”

She put her dangling lock behind her ears and with a vain smile continued, “Nothing serious. She sounded impassive. I hope she doesn’t change her mind."

Though Stephen was worried he never wanted Sanchez to be aware of it, so he put his famous grin and blurted, “Everything will be fine. Let me get a taxi”. and soon they were on their way to Adriana's house.

The taxi applied sudden brakes, dodging some pothole, bringing Sanchez out of her brooding. Stephen enquired the taxi driver about the remaining travel distance and the driver announced they were only a few minutes away. The midafternoon sun's coruscating light was seeping through the scattered clouds making the tall casted shadows of tree disappear in patches. The place was sparsely populated with houses. All the homes she had seen so far had the outside walls made of woods, well supported by brick-built structures, covered with pale stuccoes. The uniform row of houses, the broad grape vines farms, and the pristine weather - all made the village appear like a sketch out of a picture book.

Adriana’s house was no different from the ones she saw in the settlement. The ligneous house looked like weather-beaten dwelling, standing alone at the farthest corner of the street. The house was surrounded by a time-tested picket fence, part of which was broken, clearly unable to stand some preceding weathering storm. There was a half-broken wooden gate, which was standing tall like a time-tested check post, guarding the entrance of the house. The gate was leading to a mud lane surrounded by unkempt briers, awkwardly growing giving the façade a dilapidated appearance. The shrubs and the weeds covered the lawn and it appeared no one had bothered trimming the yard for years.

Stephen paid the taxi driver the dues and they carefully trod their path towards the house. As they neared the door they could see the structure of the house more clearly and it wasn’t as old as it appeared from a distance.  The walls graced plenty of tall windows covered with Castilian wrought iron, giving the house an elegant décor mien. A single panel wood door guarded the entrance to the house. There was a metal hinge right at the center of the door, serving the dual purpose of opening the door from outside and as a mechanical doorbell. Sanchez tapped the hinge and waited for Adriana to arrive. It seemed she was expecting visitors, for she answered promptly. The door made a loud creaking noise as if opened after a long time.

Adriana appeared starkly different from how she sounded on phone. A warm welcome smile donned her face as she appeared from behind the door. She was a petite looking woman and she looked much younger than what Sanchez had imagined. The house surroundings might have looked ruined, but not her. Her shoulder length hair was well kept, which she had tied to a single ponytail. The diamond studs on her ears were elegant, well complemented by the platinum necklace that graced her long neck. She was wearing a deep neck cut evening dress draped around a well-toned body. The makeup she was wearing was light, perfect for the occasion. The distinct fragrance of Miss Dior greeted their senses. It appeared as if she was waiting for her date instead of them.

She showed them the way to the living area. Unlike the tattered exteriors, the interiors were tastefully done.  The living room was a small dwell with wall embellishing pale plaster coating. It was roomy despite being a small space, as the fittings were few and sparsely placed. There was a sofa in the middle of the room and an antique Quaker chair towards far left. A wooden table was placed in the center of the sitting area with few books and magazine neatly arranged. There was a long rod dangling from the ceiling holding a bulb that was lighting the room. On the far-right side hanging off the wall was a 12 feet long picture frame of a young couple, of Adriana and her partner captured maybe a couple of decades back.

Sanchez and Stephen settled on the sofa. Adriana approached the kitchen to get some water, offered them the drink and sat across the sofa on an ottoman. Sanchez took a sip from the glass and with a low voice initiated the conversation, “Thank you, Adriana, for accommodating us in such short notice. John said you have great knowledge of maps, especially the one that comes from TransPacific. We are in a great rush and would greatly appreciate your help in deciphering the one we have with us.”

Sanchez paused as she pulled out her phone and started fiddling with it to pull out the map pictures. Adriana’s bright smile showed a shade of pale in the casted shadows of the light bulb. She was perplexed to observe how Sanchez came straight to the subject without showing any inquisitiveness to inquire about that ill-fated night. She forced herself out of her brooding, smiled and responded,

“I will be happy to. John is a dear friend and it will be a pleasure if I can offer any help.”

Sanchez by now had the map on her phone displayed, which she passed it to Adriana. Adriana looked at the picture with great interest and after several minutes of discerning observation, she spoke in a lost voice, “I need to enlarge and print it.” Still glancing over the phone, lost in her world, she blurted “Do you have a place to stay for the night?”

Sanchez looked at her brother but failed to decipher his views. She went ahead and stated about their plan to leave Portugal tonight.

“I need time to go through this.”, Adriana stated without taking her attention away from the map.

“We haven’t booked any hotel yet. We are running blind since morning. Besides we want to avoid staying in a hotel as we noticed some people looking for us at the Lisbon Airport.  If we need to stay for the night we will need your help in finding a place”, there was a clear hesitation in her voice, confirmed in the way she was playing with her wristwatch.

Adriana looked away from the phone, brought back her infectious smile and continued, “I live in this house alone. You guys can stay here tonight. Why don’t you guys settle down, in the meantime I will print the maps. I have food in the kitchen, nothing fancy, but good enough to round up a decent dinner."

Sanchez thanked Adriana on her kind gestured, mentioned to her that they had a good late afternoon lunch and dinner may not be necessary.

“You will not regret the food. I have made some Portuguese delicacies. Take the stairs up to the first floor. There are two rooms each has attached bathroom. And when you come back help yourself with the drinks. They are all by the kitchen area” Adriana went ahead with her business without waiting for their response.

Though Jules had done a great job decrypting the map, Sanchez wanted a second opinion. Besides Adriana had dealt with these maps before, and she hoped to get some finer details in case Jules missed out deciphering it. They took the stairs to the first floor and decided to occupy only one room. Sanchez got busy unpacking, while Stephen used the restroom.

Thud!! She heard as if someone fell right outside the house. The room was facing the backside of the house. She rushed to the window and raised the curtains to get a clear picture. She saw a person trying to climb up using the drainpipe. Before she could react, she heard the front door open with a bang. She hurled to the bathroom and with a silent, but firm whisper announced, “come out quick, looks like we have some visitors.”

**Chapter 34**

**2:00 PM 18th April 2014, Nazaré, Portugal**

“They are here!!” the police officer announced the news.

“Fabulous. When did they come? Are they alone?” 

“They arrived a few minutes back.” the officer mentioned in a hushed voice.

“Good. Keep a close eye on them and if you see anything unusual call me?”

Maxwell looked at the clock, it was 2:00 PM in the afternoon. He was lazing around the motel for a while now. It appeared Benedikt had forgotten Sanchez, for he refused to leave the house. He was debating for a while what should he do next, when the transmitter came to life.

He had already paid the motel dues in advance in anticipation of a situation like this and without losing any time he rushed to the car. The receiver was throwing the distinct voice of Benedikt, “We need to be there as soon as possible.”

Something didn’t seem right to him. Benedikt sounded victorious as if he had succeeded in unearthing Sanchez from her hideout. He knew he had to act with urgency to avoid any mishap. Without giving it a lot of deliberation, he instructed his police officer friend to get Sanchez out of the house as quickly as possible. He saw the van finding its way to Nazaré, confirming the perturbations he was contemplating with. “How did Benedikt found about Adriana?” He mumbled with anxiousness. Other than following the van he had no other concrete plan to curtail Benedikt’s advances, so he followed the course.

He was perplexed. He couldn’t afford to ruin the last three years of his sedulous plan. And Sanchez, deep inside he was more worried about her than anything else. Her safety was his prime concern and everything else was trivial.  While he was reasoning with his quandary he noticed the receiver was in complete silence. it appeared aberrantly unusual, for he knew the van was on the move.  He concluded either the people in the van were observing silence or something went wrong with the transmitter. With no better alternative, he went after Benedikt, despaired and angered.

He was fifteen minutes into following Benedikt when he realized he had not heard anything back from his police officer friend.  In the past, for such situation, he would have given some more time, but this time it involved Sanchez and he couldn’t hold his nerve any longer. He called the number. The phone kept ringing until he heard the long beep tone. He tried the number again and nothing changed – no one answered the phone. His agitation was at the extreme. All he could hope was to get to Sanchez before Benedikt and regretted his decision to let Sanchez go off his sight. He tried the number again. This time the phone line was cut right after the first ring. His heart sank. He was definitive something wasn’t right. He tried the number again and this time he got directly to the voicemail. He knew he had a mountainous task in his hand and driving fast was the only resort he was left with.

Right after the van turned towards highway A12 to make the final dash to Adriana’s house a strange thing happened. Instead of going straight down the road, the van looped around and headed towards Lisbon instead. He had to decide soon. Either he could check on Sanchez’s safety or go after the van. His mind was convoluted. His intuition was inclining mercilessly towards the possibility of Sanchez captivation. He didn’t have much time to sanely play out his options, and in haste decided to go check Adriana’s house first. He trusted the GPS locator to help him trace the van later if needed. For now, Sanchez was his only priority

From where he was, the distance to Adriana’s house was less than 30 minutes, and it appeared the longest drive he had ever taken in his life thus far.

**Chapter 35**

**2:00 PM 18th April 2014, Nazaré, Portugal**

The lunch was ordered from a nearby restaurant. They ate in complete silence. And besides the occasional clinking of forks, there was no babel despite dozen people in a room. 

Benedikt was lost in his own world, thinking about the conversation he had with Adam. Adam was methodical, rational and always impeccable in executing any given job. When he informed him about Sanchez planning to visit a house in Nazaré, he was certain it would happen. However, with the passing time, his worries were slowly creeping back. Too many things had gone wrong in the last few days, rattling every one of his belief. The loud tone of the phone rattled him out of his cogitation. 

“We saw them entering the house!!”

“We are on our way. Keep a good eye on them. Remember no mistakes,” a sense of achievement reflected from his orotund voice.

“We need to go right now.”

Francis had no clue where they were headed. Benedikt’s indifferent behavior towards him was making him delirious. He knew his chief was working on a plan in complete discretion. And the fact that he was not part of it made him anxious and fidgety.

Francis took the driver seat, took his gun out from the pocket and threw it in the glove compartment. Soon they were on their way. The occasional passing vehicle was the only sound one could hear, for the vehicle occupants decided to maintain complete silence. About five minutes into the drive Benedikt got a call. After few minutes of listening, he announced, “Get them and meet me at the warehouse.”

They drove for another ten minutes when Benedikt got another call. The conversation didn't last long and ended abruptly. He instructed Francis to make a U-turn and asked him to drive towards Lisbon. Francis kept his curiosity under check and followed the instructions without questioning his chief's intent. 

Benedikt knew this was his chance. He kept checking his watch every other minute. And for the first time since his arrival in Lisbon, he appeared anxious.

**Chapter 36**

**4:30 PM 18th April 2014, Nazaré, Portugal**

Stephen came out of the bathroom startled. He grabbed the shirt he took off not too long ago and followed Sanchez, who led the way tiptoeing to the other side of the house. They took shelter in the room opposite to where they were before. The room was facing the front side of the house.  She zeroed down on the window as her escape route and she slowly opened it to arrest any unwarranted squeaking sound. There was a drainpipe running from the roof to the ground next to the window. She pointed to the water pipe, expressing her intention and took the lead.  As she slid down the pipe a loud screaming voice got her attention. She ignored the voice and continued sliding down.  Stephen mustered some courage and followed her. The height was manageable, even if he would have fallen, it wouldn’t have killed him.

Adriana was in her reading room when she heard some noise at the door. She wasn’t expecting anyone. And then she recalled Sanchez mentioning about some suspicious strangers at the airport. Before she could comprehend two intruders were standing next to her in the living room.

“Como se atreve a entrar na minha casa sem permissão” (“How dare you entered my house without permission.”)

The goons showed no interest to her hollow threat.  Soon they were in the room where her guest was there a few moments ago. They saw their belongings, but Sanchez was nowhere to be found. They peeped out from the window and found two of their partners in a scuffle with a man. Losing them was never an option. They scampered the house to find any trace of Sanchez but failed.

As they were running from room to room, one of the goons shouted: “Ali estão eles” (“There they are!”). He saw them running across the field towards a brick building. They jumped from the window and went after them. After a few minutes of chasing they were near them. One of them grabbed Sanchez’s hand from the rear side. Sanchez turned around in a jiffy, clutched the shoulder of his stretched hand and swung him to ground by rolling her body under him. Before he could react, she gave an elbow jab under his chin, and he collapsed to the ground unconscious. His companion couldn’t believe what he saw. He could never imagine a fragile looking girl could be this strong. He took his guard well and was over cautious as he approached her with a half kick. Sanchez stopped his kick half way and gave him a full-blown thrust under his groin. He too laid flat on the ground next to his friend.

Stephen was too shocked to react. She held his hand and forced him to run. A loud gunshot rattled the late afternoon quietness. A loud voice screamed, “Stop otherwise the next one will blow your head."

Sanchez turned around and saw the person holding a .42 caliber gun in his hand. Sanchez saw the grit on his face and she knew he meant business. They had no option but to stop. Sanchez was shattered to pieces. Her mission got curtailed too fast too soon.

**PART IV**

**Chapter 37**

**4:45 PM 18th April 2014, Nazaré, Portugal**

The man with the gun approached the girl with cautious steps. He saw it from the distance how the girl overpowered his friends without effort. He was in a state of disbelieve. He couldn’t imagine in his wildest dream how a fragile looking girl could demonstrate such power and execute with such perfectness.  During this entire hullaballoo, there was one thing he was certain about, not to underestimate the girl anymore. His eyes were constantly watching them with his gun pointed to their chest.  He was at a striking distance from them, but he wanted to ensure that his targets had no more tricks up their sleeves. Without taking his eyes off them he called upon his partner to search them. With the gun pointing at them, he was hopeful that the girl would behave.
