It was bizarre as he was worried more about the girl than the man who accompanied her.

His partner went ahead with his vigilance guard on. He turned towards Sanchez first. She was in her sleepwear. The man carefully tapped her pajama pockets and screened her body. There was nothing on her. The man behaved, and he meant business. He refrained from taking any advantage of the situation and avoided touching her with any shade of lust. Once cleared by his partner, the man who was holding the gun instructed her to turn around with the back facing him. Next, his partner went ahead and searched Stephen thoroughly.  Nothing was found on him either. Stephen was also made to turnaround and he was put about two feet away from Sanchez. Once done scrutinizing, he turned towards his unconscious partners to see if he could resurrect them back to senses. His attempt to revive them was interrupted by a loud ringtone. The quiet afternoon was soon engulfed by the loud shrill of the phone. The phone belonged to one of the people who lay insentient. With some hesitation, the man who tried reviving his unconscious friends searched for the phone existence. The loud tone was almost becoming unbearable as he fumbled to locate it. With some persistent looking, he traced it in his friend’s trousers.  His face turned pale when he saw the name flashing on the phone's screen.

“Who is it?” his partner spoke with a shrieking tone in Portuguese.

He turned towards him and with a timid voice announced, “It’s the chief” and with hesitation pressed the answer button.

A harsh voice cracked through, “What took you so long?”

“Chief we were in a situation...” before he could finish, his voice was curtailed by Benedikt’s impatient scream.

“I told you to call me every ten minutes and it seems you didn’t bother. Do you have the girl?”

The man mustered some courage and with a trembling voice continued, “Chief we have the girl, but we ran into a situation. The girl overpowered Joseph and Marty and they lay unconscious. I am William. This is Joseph’s phone. I answered as I saw you calling.”

Benedikt blurted, “What the hell are you doing there when you already have the girl?”

William’s voice was shaking with the onslaught of questions. He responded with a shaking voice, “We are trying to bring them...”

Benedikt snapped him before he could finish his words “Now listen carefully. I don’t give a damn about your friends. Too bad, a girl managed to beat the hell out of you guys. Leave everything and bring the girl to me. Also, as I had instructed before, they shouldn’t be harmed. Is that clear?”

“Yes”, came the reply from him and the line went silent.

William had never spoken to Benedikt before, but he hardly regretted it now. His partner knew from his expression that it wasn’t a pleasant conversation. “We need to move quickly. Chief is waiting for us” was all he could manage to divulge.

The man pointing the gun hesitated, but he knew they hadn’t been given any choice. Against their wishes, they left their unconscious friends stranded. Something they would regret for the rest of their life.

He commanded them to saunter towards the house. Their car was parked little away from the entrance. And the walk was about few hundred meters through the open paddy fields. Sanchez was sensing the physical and the mental trauma the two men were going through. She knew they weren’t fully focused on them, and their hearts were still longing for their friend’s wellbeing. She also knew this could be her only chance to break free. Had it not been for the gun she could have easily taken them down but for now, she had to find some other way to escape the goons.

She put a cursory glance at her brother in an attempt to get his attention, but he appeared too preoccupied to notice. She had no sight of the goons as they were walking behind her. Her senses were working on steroids and she had a vague idea that the man who was checking on their friends was close to her and his partner was guarding Stephen. She knew both possessed guns. How she wished Stephen could read her mind, but it appeared he had already surrendered to the fate.  She looked ahead and saw the path curving to the left before entering the backyard of the house. She picked that curve as her spot to execute her plan. For one last time she looked towards Stephen, but he was in no mood to come out of his deliberations. What she was thinking was dangerous, but she had to take her chances.

The bend was only a few meters away. Sanchez knew what she needed to do. She closed her eyes for few seconds, drove all her unwanted fears away and focused on the fast-approaching curve. Few meters before the path curved, she increased her speed and before anyone could comprehend she bent low, turned 180 degrees, and with utmost suave she kicked the person behind her on his knees.

He was taken off-guard as he screamed with pain. His gun flew away from his hand and he held his knee in discomfort. Before the other person could grasp what struck them, Sanchez wrestled him to the ground with a deadlock around his neck. She strangled him to a point he was breathless. He struggled for few moments before falling off to ground unconscious.

It took few moments for Stephen to apprehend the situation. But soon he sprung to action and kicked the person who was guarding Sanchez on his chest with all his might. He was already in agony because of the injured knee, couldn’t take the impact of the boot anymore and collapsed to the ground without resistance. In few minutes both the miscreants were lying senseless on the ground. Without wasting another minute, they ran towards Adriana’s house. They were worried about her and hoped to find her safe.

**Chapter 38**

**4:30 PM 18th April 2014, Nazaré, Portugal**

Damian had never seen Benedikt in such delirious state. He demonstrated a teenager's restlessness and displayed nervousness of a rookie. It was over a decade he had worked under him and never once he had seen him in such wrecked state. He remained on the edge, ready to implode, any moment. Though he refrained from mentioning anything about whom he was after, it was quite evident he was chasing the girl.

Damian was equally frustrated about his failure to get hold of her. He was tired of the constant nagging and the rage Benedikt had embraced from the time the girl had escaped him from the hotel. Every passing moment was making him more unreasonable and unpredictable. He dreaded the thought of her leaving Lisbon unscathed, as that would make Benedikt obstinate, and he knew it would make his life a living hell for sure.

Benedikt’s sudden surreal screaming shook Damian’s out of his pondering. He saw him bellowing in frustration, failing to get someone on the phone. He called a few more times without any response and threw away the phone with extreme anger. “Take the next exit and loop around. We have to go back to Nazaré.” He announced in a stentorian tone.

“What has happened to him?” Damian brooded. He was known for his calm stature, never giving away anything in his expression. He never recalled once when he had raised his voice in the past. There were situations, which were tough, and circumstances that were life-threatening, but never once he had behaved in such erratic manner. The only reason he could think of was the taboo of getting defeated by a naïve girl. He could have accepted failure against a rogue, but a girl keeping him on the run and thwarting all his moves was making him wild, exposing his real self.

The phone lying on the floor started ringing. Benedikt fumbled to get to it, finally managed to pick it up and screamed, “Didn’t I instruct you to call me every ten minutes? What took you so long?” The conversation went on for few minutes and once he was done conversing he announced, “We need to head back to Lisbon”

By now they were on their way to Nazaré. Francis was clueless, he was frustrated, but all he could manage was a nod, and swung to action following on Benedikt’s order.

**Chapter 39**

**5:15 PM 18th April 2014, Nazaré, Portugal**

Adriana’s house was in complete shambles. The living room table was overturned, the chairs were upside down, and the floor was covered with mud footsteps. It appeared as if the house managed to abate a colossal tempest.

Sanchez’s and Stephen’s eyes were searching for Adriana, but she was nowhere to be seen. Sanchez took the stairs to check the upstairs room while Stephen continued his search in the ground floor. The room they were housed not long ago, was in total disarray. The place was ransacked and items from their bags were scattered all over the place. Sanchez couldn’t find any trace of Adriana. All she found were traces of the rouges turning a beautiful house to a complete disaster. Once convinced of her absence on the first floor she rushed down and found Stephen equally startled and shocked. There was no sign of Adriana anywhere in the house. Sanchez was puzzled by her mysterious disappearance. She knew Benedikt’s men were after her, and not finding Adriana wasn’t making any sense. Besides, all four intruders were lying unconscious in the backyard.

A sudden flash of wisdom crossed her mind and in a subliminal state she mumbled, “She must be in her workshop”. She looked at Stephen and with a hurried tone blurted, “Did you check the basement?”

Stephen looked blank and swayed his head sideways. And in unison, they sprinted to the basement.

This was the first time they were entering Adriana’s workshop and it looked no different from what they saw in Jules house. Scattered papers, unkempt piles of books, dust-ridden computer and tons of cartographic instruments. She looked around and couldn’t find Adriana anywhere. There was a large velvet covered sofa on the left of the entrance. The height of the sofa was hiding the area between the sofa and the wall. Sanchez’s instinct drew her to the sofa, and she took a big leap to jump over it.

There she was! Sacred. Sitting in the corner, hiding behind the sofa. She had no resemblance to the Adriana they met a few hours ago. Her hair was all over her face, clothes were crumbled, and her face had hues of terror and fear. Her eyes appeared lost, starring in the vacuum. She had no visible mark of being hurt, the only good thing in the otherwise disheveled surroundings. She was in a state of shock, and she hardly reacted despite Sanchez sudden appearance.

Sanchez held her by her arms and enquired softly, “Are you ok?” There was no answer. Sanchez shook her hard to bring her out of her traumatized state. After some moments in despair, she came out of her appalling state, still shuddered with horror, looking at Sanchez with disbelieve.

“Oh! Thank God you are ok”, Adriana spoke, coming out of her shocked state. Her voice sounded brittle, she couldn’t hold her emotions for long and soon she bawled like a child. Sanchez comforted her by holding her close to her chest and spoke softly, “We need to be out of this place at the earliest. They can come back anytime. Can you walk?”

Adriana confirmed she was fine to walk.

“Do you have a car?” Sanchez asked as she rushed out of the basement

“Yes, I do. It is in the garage and the keys are on the kitchen table.”

The garage was a standalone unit, detached from the house, a few meters away from the door. Stephen led them as he sprinted to get the car out. He hurled the key in the ignition and with a gentle nudge the car engine revered to life with a large roaring sound. He reversed the car and aligned it to the muddy road and waited for the ladies to get in the car.  Sanchez paced to the old Peugeot van with cautious steps, for she avoided rushing Adriana. She put Adriana on the back seat and rushed to join her brother in the front passenger seat.

A loud gun burst made Stephen jump, and his head hit the car roof. Before he could comprehend what struck him, he saw Sanchez collapsing in a pool of blood.

“SANCHEZ!!” he shouted at the top of his lung.

Sanchez fell a few meters away from the car. Stephen opened the door and was about to leave the car when Sanchez shouted, “Don’t get off the car. Go. At least one of us will be safe, and with you around I will have a chance to escape."

Stephen was perplexed. He was worried and nervous and had no clue how to respond to the situation. He finally spoke, and his tone demonstrated clear resistance, “I can’t."

The man was fast approaching; he shot another fire, which hit the car trunk giving away a loud ear-splitting BANG”

“Don’t be a fool. Can’t you hear the footsteps?”, she screamed.

In that blink of the moment, Stephen saw the sense in her sister’s words. He knew another minute of delay would jeopardize any chance of his escape. He put his foot on the accelerator and sped away. The screeching sound of the tires was dreadful as if providing its final resistance from leaving Sanchez behind. He was delirious, he screamed with frustration and tears rolled down his eyes. He kept looking at the rear mirror till the house faded away from distance. Adriana was too shell-shocked to react to anything. She kept staring at Sanchez without uttering a word. Soon Sanchez went out of her sight.

**Chapter 40**

**5:35 PM 18th April 2014, Nazaré, Portugal**

With a dizzy head, Marty slowly opened his eyes. His vision was blurry. A nerve-wracking pain sprung down his spine as he reluctantly raised his head. He tried to get on his feet, but his legs refused to support him too. With brute force, he managed to stand using his hand as a support. He almost screamed for a sharp pain ran through his spine as if he was pricked with shrapnel. For a few seconds, he struggled to wrap his head around his whereabouts, and gradually the events started unfolding.  He looked around and all he saw was miles of open fields. He pushed himself hard and with some gritted effort managed to walk, but with a scornful eye and a bad stagger.

As he was hobbling his way towards the house he found his friends scattered around in an unconscious state. He tried waking them, pushing with his feet and speaking loudly but failed. As he dragged his disheveled self away from them, he saw a gun lying a few meters away. It took all his might to bend, as he attempted to grab the gun. After a few painful attempts, he managed to fetch it and sauntered his way towards the house. He was throbbing with pain, but the thought about Sanchez escaping and Benedikt's rage made his torment look trivial. As he walked past the backyard he heard a car engine coming to life. He turned around the corner and saw Sanchez sprinting towards the front of the car. Though he was at a striking distance from the car he couldn’t do much, besides watching her go. He knew his options were running out and he had one final chance to resurrect the situation. And against his superior’s orders, he took the decision. Shaving off all inhabitations he aimed at Sanchez’s leg and fired the shot. There was a loud bang followed by a scream. He knew he got her this time. Next, he aimed the gun at the car’s tire but missed the shot by a few inches. He was thrilled about something and used all his might to come near the car in the shortest possible time. As he moved forward the car zoomed away leaving Sanchez behind. He wasn’t concerned, for he wanted the girl and she was in an arm's length distance from him sitting in a pool of blood.

He knew what the girl could do so he was extra careful. He held the gun with both hands, pointed it to her temple and walked towards her. With the gun still pointing at her, he growled with a commanding tone, “You trick me this time I will kill you. Do you hear me?”

Sanchez gave back a blank stare. Besides the wrath of pain on her face, she gave nothing away. There was no fear, no apprehension reflecting anything about her reticent state. Marty got jitters seeing her intrepid reaction. He had been in several occasions pointing guns at people before, and every time he had seen fear, horror, and dreadfulness in the other persons' eyes. Never once he had encountered anyone with such grit and fearlessness.

He tried again, “Listen, I am serious, so you better behave. I want you to take my support and walk towards the car.”

He stretched his left hand in anticipation, but Sanchez didn't buzz. He knew his threats wouldn’t do any good, so he decided to take things into his own hands. With the gun, in one hand, he tried to make her stand by pulling her arms with his other hand. Despite putting a great deal of effort, he only succeeded moving her by an inch. The car was a few strides away, but with the pace, he realized it would take him the entire evening.

It wasn’t working. As he contemplated his next move, he heard some approaching footsteps. He turned sharply and found two of his friends, bedraggled, walking towards him.

He now found a reason to smile. After briefing them about the situation, he sought their help to get her to the car. The men came to action. While Marty got the car, his friends dragged her to the backseat of the car. Sanchez exhibited great resistance, but with a weak limb, mounting pain and two men holding her arms, she conceded to the situation. She was in the middle seat surrounded by the two goons.

Marty decided to drive on the opposite road, away from the path Stephen took a few minutes back. It was a longer route, but he knew this way he could avoid any untoward skirmish. The bruises from Sanchez blows were still hurting him and he knew none of his friends had any strength to faceoff with any other reckon force.

One of the men tore his shirt and wrapped it tightly around Sanchez’s leg.  It helped the blood to recede. The bullet went pass the calf muscle without hurting the bone. She looked exhausted and her struggle to keep herself awake was fading fast with each passing moment. The monologue of the motor humming sound on the deserted road of the laid-back coastal town soon drifted her off to sleep.

Ten minutes into the drive Marty decided to call Benedikt. He fumbled around to find the phone and finally located it in his back pocket. He saw numerous missed calls from Benedikt flashed all over the phone screen. He hesitated a bit, but somehow mustered some courage and dialed the number.

Benedikt picked the phone at the first ring. A sharp orotund voice pierced his ear, “What took you so long?”

Marty was petrified. He hadn’t dealt with Benedikt before. He pulled himself together and stated, “This is Marty, we have the girl. She is in the car with us and we are on our way to the place.”

Benedikt restlessness got instant pacification. This time with a belligerent tone he stated, “How far are you?”

“We should be there in the next twenty minutes.”

“Ok. I will see you at the place.” Benedikt tone was much calmer this time. For once that afternoon he looked to be in control of the proceedings.

Marty's face was pale as if he had weathered a storm. He couldn’t congregate enough courage to announce about Sanchez being hurt. He remembered Benedikt’s clear instruction, “Do not hurt the girl, no matter what the situation is.”

“She didn’t leave me with any options”, he mumbled trying to reason with his troubled soul. He knew his hope was pretentious, the best he could do to distract his mind from the confrontation he would face in a short time.

**Chapter 41**

**5:40 PM 18th April 2014, Nazaré, Portugal**

Stephen was driving in an absolute reckless manner. Tears blurred his vision and his mind was a confetto, clouded with fretting apprehensions. He was unable to comprehend how he left Sanchez alone in such imminent danger. And with a confused mind, he kept driving on the wobbly desolate roads of Nazaré, unsure where he was headed.

Despite all the proceedings, Adriana demonstrated no penitence. She kept staring outside with no display of emotions. The evening sun was casting long shadows of trees on the deserted road making everything appear ghostly and eerie. Besides his own wheezing, all Stephen could occasionally hear the constant humming of the motor engine resurfacing every time his deplorable being took a halt. In that sinister setup, a car went past him with great urgency. There was a sense of fortitude the passing car exuded, which jolted Stephen out of his helpless shell.

“How can I leave her alone?” he mumbled.

He regretted his decision of running away from the situation, and between his trepidations and confusion, he knew he had lost crucial time fleeting. With that sudden urge of wisdom, he swung his car, but failed to negotiate the turn well and crashed into the paddy field. He dug the car out, and with some struggle managed to get it back on the road. The tires made a screeching noise as he pressed the accelerator with all force but was instantly forced to apply the brakes to avoid banging into an oncoming car. Adriana maintained her silence despite all the commotions happening around her. He was about to cut loose when he saw a young man rushing towards him raising his hand high signaling him to stop.

There was a genuine concern on the man’s face compelling him to stop much against his wish. He rolled the window down and gave him a curious stare. The man dashed to the car and his incisive eyes became glum when he couldn’t find what he was looking for.

"Where is Sanchez?” His voice reflected deep anxiousness.

Stephen was confused. Everything appeared jumbled up for him, and all he could manage was to keep staring at the stranger with absolute blankness.

“Why aren’t you speaking?” with a penetrating voice the man demanded.

“She got shot. Those men…” his voice trailed laced with fear and helplessness.

“Move over!” the man demanded. Stephen followed the command without any resistance.

The man drove with purpose and determination. There was speed and aggression, as he covered the distance with finesse. He couldn’t believe Stephen left her in the hands of the goons.

“How could you? God Damn! You left her alone to save your life!!” he shouted with a clear sign of frustration.

Stephen was too ashamed to respond. He hid his face in his palms and kept his disastrous state invisible from any further confrontations. He had no curiousness, no threat, and demonstrated no impediment to find about the man who knew everything about him, Sanchez and the situation.

**Chapter 42**

**6:30 PM 18th April 2014, Lisbon, Portugal**

This time the men committed no mistakes. Everything worked according to the plan, and they reached their destination without any more untoward incidence.

The place appeared isolated, about a few miles south of the Lisbon dockyard. The building was a long flat concrete structure with a dark greyish exterior. The warehouse appeared abandoned for a while, endorsed by the long wild vines and unruly shrubs covering the time-stained walls. It was a storage place of some kind from a faraway time.

A tall rusted wrought iron gate valiantly guarded the place. The egress was open halfway, not wide enough for the car to go past. Marty Got off the car and pushed the gate. The structure made a squealing noise, resisting the push, and slowly made way.  He took the car to the open area and parked it right in front of the entrance.

The men got off the car with urgency. She was not in a state to walk so, they decided to carry her. One of them held her by the arms and the other two wrapped their hands around her legs.  The flow of blood had regressed, but a good amount of gore was lost, evident from the pale look on her face.

The door to the building was ajar. The warehouse interiors complimented the exteriors. There were no windows or ceilings, restricting any form of light to trickle in, and a few dim lights were fighting hard to keep the darkness of the evening away. The structure was one long hall with no rooms or partitions. There were steel rods and pipe running throughout the roof, and it appeared during its prime days the pipes were used to provide the adequate cooling and heating to the vast interiors. The whole place was covered with mosses and the floor at regular intervals had caved in, exposing the earth, and allowing wild flora to exist in abundance.

The men along with the girl walked towards the center of the hall. It was evident they knew the place well. Once at the center, they turned left and continued walking until they reached the wall. There was a small flap on the wall hidden behind few vines, camouflaging with the wall color. Marty tapped on the flap exposing few small buttons. He pressed the buttons a few times in a pattern, and soon one of the floor tiles slid open exposing a set of staircases. The men trod path with utmost care climbing down the spiral stairs. There was another switch right at the bottom of the steps. Marty pushed the button, and the tile slid back to its original position.

The place appeared to be in a different world. There was a surreal difference between how the warehouse looked outside than its squawky counterpart. It was a spacious place with a modern outlook. The ceiling of the basement was almost twelve feet tall. There were plenty of recess lights dropping from the roof in S-shaped structures, illuminating the place in pale yellow brilliance. The wide entrance was neatly covered with brown leather cushioned sofa, and a coffee table graced the center place with a wide collection of journals and magazines. The walls were colored with a bold shade of light yellow, sporting some of the modern-day paintings. Towards the left of the staircase was a transparent glass door, which was guarding few closed-door rooms. And on the right, there was an oval shaped conference room with opaque glass surroundings. Marty tapped few buttons on a keypad, located right outside the conference room and the door opened with a click sound. All of them were in the room. Francis and Damian were standing at the corner, and Benedikt was pacing across the room.

Benedikt face lit for a moment, but soon it got arrested with a deep concern. His heart was pulsating with such vigor that he could hear it oscillating between his chest and the back of his spine. A thousand emotions crossed his face. He was pulled between the excitement of seeing her and the wrath of watching her in such a disheveled state.

“What happened to her?” he screamed demanding an answer.

Marty without mincing any words stated in an obligatory tone, “I shot her on the leg. I didn’t have any option. Otherwise, she would have run away."

Benedikt’s rage had no boundaries. His face turned red and his eyes fumed with anger. “I told you not to hurt the girl at any cost. Not at any cost!” He took his gun out and almost decided to pull the trigger but managed to abstain. The best others could do was to keep any conversation to a bare minimum.

Benedikt knew Sanchez needed medical attention. He dashed with all his might to be near her. He had spent the last few days in desirousness, with an unbearable longing and embracing an unfathomable quest. He had spent hours brooding, playing the moment on the head things he would do when he would meet her. But the fate had brought close to her in the most infelicitous manner he could have ever imagined.

He gently held her unconscious body in his arm. The spiral locks of her disarrayed tresses were all over her face. With one hand still holding her, he put the hair aside to make her face visible. The touch of her soft cold skin sent shivers through his spine. Her face hardly showed the signs of the ordeal she went through. The serenity she was exuding made him nervous. He craved to heal her with his touch, wanted her to regain senses, and hoped to express through his libretto how much he was yearning for her.

Instead, all he could manage was to keep looking at her in silence with many shades of hues hidden deep within his blue eyes.

**Chapter 43**

**6:05 PM 18th April 2014, Nazaré, Portugal**

Maxwell cramped the car in the narrow driveway of Adriana’s house. He sprung open the door and rushed towards the main entrance. The house looked deserted with no sign of any soul.  The surroundings complimented the derelict house, welcoming him in a most undesirable manner.

Stephen shouted from the back, “This way!”

Maxwell turned around and traced his way back to the sidewalk. There was blood all over the place and a long stretch of reddish dark gore across the driveway. He could make out that Sanchez got dragged. The smear of blotchy brownish spots indicated her resistance while she was hauled. he knew he had to act fast, but he had no clue what to do. He held Stephen by his shoulders and demanded with a frustrating tone, “Now listen to me carefully. Tell me everything. What happened here? Every second is precious so be brief and exact."

Stephen rubbed his eyes to get a clearer vision and told the entire episode in an embarrassing tone. He concluded with a shaking voice, “The man who shot her was standing there” he showed with his finger pointing towards the narrow pathway, which opened to the paddy fields. He regained a spark in his trailing voice, as if remembering something important, and uttered with urgency, “They were four of them, but when I fleeted, from the rear-view mirror I could only see three. One of them must be still there in the fields!” Without wasting any time Stephen led the way towards the open area where he and Sanchez had confronted the goons not too long ago. Maxwell followed him. As he went past the backyard he saw his police officer friend lying in a pool of blood.

Against his wish, Maxwell ignored his friend, for he wanted to catch hold of the goon first, provided he was still around. William, the last man whom Sanchez knocked off, was still there, lying flat in an unconscious state.

“Guard him”, demanded Maxwell and he sprinted back to check his officer friend. He was shot twice on the chest. He checked for his pulse, but it was too late, he was dead a while back. He rushed back to check on the goon. The man was motionless, lying inert on the vast open field. With eyes staring the insentient soul he ordered Stephen, “Go and get some water from the house.”

Maxwell’s only hope was this man. The transmitter he had put in Benedikt’s car had stopped responding a while back. He knew the car carrying Sanchez took a detour, for he hadn’t noticed any other car passing by. Reviving this man was the only hope he had to get hold of Benedikt. He regretted his decision of not being near Sanchez. He could have easily avoided all the mishap if he would have decided to stay close to her.

Stephen came back with a water pail. He sprinkled some water on the man’s face. After few tries, he came to senses from his cataleptic state but struggled to open his eyes. As he regained his senses he became aware that he was captured. He gave a faint attempt to escape but was too exhausted to execute his vague plan.

Maxwell took his gun out and with a sharp, yet calm tone declared, “I neither have the patience nor the time. Tell me where you were planning to take the girl?”

William was lost. He wasn’t sure where his friends were. He couldn’t spot the girl anywhere close by. From the conversation, he concluded that his friends got the girl and this person was trying to find her. He was brought out of his brooding by the cold touch of the gunmetal against his chin.

Maxwell continued, “Look I don’t have time. If you aren’t speaking, I will make you bleed to death."

The man remained quiet without uttering a word.

Maxwell meant every word. He fired a shot right on his leg. A loud painful scream came out of William, and he held his leg with disbelieve.

Maxwell stated, “I am asking for one last time. The next bullet will hit the hand that is holding your leg."

William screamed for mercy, and he blurted in broken English about the place where his friends were planning to go after capturing Sanchez. With the help of Stephen, Maxwell lifted him and put him in the car. He looked at him with fierce eyes and with a daemon outcry he clamored, “The sooner you take me to them the better is your chance of survival."

William agreed despairingly as he squirmed with pain.

He instructed Stephen to stay back with Adriana and told him that he would call him soon. Stephen resisted. But he had no option but to follow orders.

While driving, Maxwell called the Lisbon police and told them about the unfortunate incident. He was tempted to take the local police help to trace Benedikt, but he knew that would need him to do a lot of explanation. Moreover, he wasn’t sure whom to trust in the system.

He spotted the warehouse from a distance, deserted and in a dilapidated condition. He dragged William inside the hallway. William had no verve left to fight back, and he cooperated in showing him the secret pathway. The spots of blood were in the area confirming Sanchez presence in the building. He touched the blood and sensed the viscous substance. The fluid was still warm with no clot formation, endorsing that the episode happened not a while back.  He let William lay on the floor as he took out his gun and advanced forward with watchful steps. The place appeared deserted. There was no sign of any living soul.  He climbed down the staircase and came to an open hall. There was no trace of anyone there as well. Maxwell saw a large conference room towards his left. The doors were open and he barged in but found no one. For the next few minutes, he searched every corner of the place without any success.

“They must have taken her to a hospital.” He concluded. Although he was not sure what incentive Benedikt might have in saving her life.

He leaped out from the underground hideout to the place where he left William. He found him lying there, almost inert and bleeding profusely. He showed no mercy whatsoever, as he bent and lifted him by grabbing his collar. He was half dizzy, on the verge of passing out.

“Your luck is riding high. I had no intention to offer you any medical attention but looks like you are going to get it. How far is the nearest hospital?” Maxwell queried with a rue of sarcasm in his tone.

William looked dazed and with a heave, he managed to speak with a thin voice, “about twenty minutes away."

Maxwell dragged him back to the car, dumped him in the front seat and started driving with William acting as his navigator.

In about twenty minutes he was in the lobby of the city hospital. The emergency area of the hospital looked crowded than what he expected. He put William on a waiting chair, approached the lady at the counter, flashed his ID and stated in a sharp low tone, “The man is shot. Treat him, if you wish. Call the cops and don’t let him go till I come back”.

He blurted the words without any reflection of emotions. The lady was startled, and she hurried to the doctor’s room to get some assistance.  There was another lady in the reception area that witnessed the conversation. She too was in a state of disbelieve and carried a frightful expression on her face. Maxwell turned his attention towards her, flashed Sanchez picture and in a hurried manner enquired, “Did someone bring this girl here in the last hour or so? She was shot in her leg.”

The lady at the counter looked puzzled and disapproved of seeing her. Maxwell had no time to waste and decided to take things into his own hands. He rushed towards the emergency room, swung open the door and entered inside. He went room by room to check but couldn’t find Sanchez. He checked with the resident doctors, but everything turned out to be negative.

His heart sank. Deep inside he knew every passing moment was making his task of finding her more arduous. He appeared absolvent, and in a delirious state, he started his journey towards the old dilapidated house where Benedikt was a few hours ago. He was running out of options and falling short of ideas.

**Chapter 44**

**6:45 PM 18th April 2014, Lisbon, Portugal**

Benedikt was devastated as if a bolt had struck shattering him to pieces. He was a man of thousand shades, oscillating between hues of love and shadows of pain. He had waited for this moment for a lifetime and when the moment presented itself he was unable to fathom. It was surreal, and with every passing minute, it was becoming hard for him to comprehend. He wanted to embrace her, hold her forever and never let her disappear again.

For the first time, he was convinced about his yearning for her. And now that he was certain, he was determined to do anything to get her out of the misery. He wrestled hard not to make his innocuous admiring appear as ogling to others. And before he could free from the quagmire Sanchez opened her eyes, coming out of her delirious state. Seeing her quivering with pain, the practicality of the situation sank in him. He lifted her in his arms and rushed towards the staircase. He wanted to get her the best medical assistance at the earliest possible time. As he climbed the stairs he announced, “Call the best doctor in town, and bring him to the nearest hospital.”

Francis led the way to the car. He was talking in a hurried manner, instructing some doctor to come to the nearest hospital. He opened the back door of the car and helped Benedikt to make her lay straight on the seat. Benedikt climbed the car, carefully lifted her head and put it on his lap as he made space to sit next to her.

Francis took the driving seat and Damian sat next to him. Francis realized because of the commotion he had misplaced his gun somewhere. With a faint hope, he opened the glove compartment and fumbled around to check for the gun. And then his hand struck some metal piece. He peeped inside and found a tiny instrument taped at the corner most position of the compartment. It appeared damaged. He pulled it out and showed it to Damian as he continued to study the tiny aperture.

“It is a bug!!” announced Damian. His voice expressed astonishment. For the first time that evening Benedikt paid attention to his accomplices' conversation.

“It makes complete sense. That police officer at Nazaré wasn’t an accident. Destroy it right away. Tell our men to vacate all places. I want to leave this place as early as we can." announced Benedikt.

“This piece is already damaged. I believe this happened when I threw the gun in the compartment. It must have hit it right and square”.

Francis was still amazed by his accidental discovery. He finally got something right even if it was by accident.

The car engine came to life with a big roar. Benedikt’s mind was racing, contemplating who could be behind him. He wanted to leave Lisbon as fast as he could. Now that he had Sanchez, he wanted to avoid all unnecessary confrontations. The evening sun was fading fast, spreading scattered tinge across the horizon. It resembled his state – as his trepidations were expunging and a sense of calmness was slowly engulfing his soul.

**Chapter 45 **

**6:30 PM 18th April 2014, Lisbon, Portugal**

The streetlights were appearing in a sporadic fashion on the busy roads of Lisbon. A dull wind was casually sauntering with a hint of laziness written over it. People were gracing the streets aimlessly walking with no set destination, but not Maxwell. He had a purpose, his face exuded anxiety and his mind clouded with a sense of urgency. He parked the car a street away from where Benedikt was housed a few hours ago. The porch was empty, and the place was in complete darkness. He tried opening the front door, but it didn't nudge. Frustrated he pulled out his gun, pointed to the doorknob and shot it. The silencer on the gun gave away a blip sound and the knob gave away its resistance.  He flung the door open and the worst of his fears came true.

No one was in sight. He searched the house with haste but found nothing. He looked for any sign of Sanchez presence, but nothing was substantiating enough to suggest anything. He felt asphyxiated. His intuition suggested he had lost trace of Sanchez. Time wasn’t by his side, for every passing minute was taking him further away from her.

He sprinted back to his car and sped his way through the busy evening traffic of Lisbon. This time his destination was Montijo airport. His last dwindling hope was Benedikt's jet, Benedikt's escape path from Lisbon. His hope was to reach the airport before Benedikt. Throughout the drive, he tried hard to avoid all the spiteful deliberations that invariably crowded his mind but failed miserably.

The drive to the airport was strenuous. The traffic didn’t help his cause either, and it took him over an hour to reach the airport. On the way, he got a call from the Lisbon police, inquiring about the death of the police officer. Maxwell tried to explain about Benedikt's transgressions and urged their attention in finding the van. But the Lisbon police weren’t interested in chasing a random van without any concrete evidence. He realized he was wasting time convincing them and promptly concluded the conversation.

The airport was an unusually quiet place. He spotted few planes resting in the open hangers but wasn’t sure which one belonged to Benedikt. He parked his car by the entrance and rushed into the small waiting area through the revolving door. He saw a reception desk at the end of the hallway towards the left alley. It was near the backdoor outlet, which opened to the airfield. There was a young girl guarding the place and she was busy talking on her phone.

Maxwell took a few long strides to the desk. The young girl took her attention away from the phone, looked at him and with a harmonious tone queried, “Good evening. How may I help you, sir?”

He forced a smile and blurted “Is Benedikt’s jet still here?” his heart was racing hard as he anxiously waited to hear what she had to say.

The girl continued to carry the infectious smile and enquired, “Do you have his jet’s tail number?”

He knew it was a valid request, and with a gravelly voice demanded, “Sorry miss I don’t have it with me. Can you please find by name?”

Maxwell’s demeanor strongly influenced the girl and she agreed to his request.

"Did you say Benedikt? They left about twenty minutes back.”

Maxwell knew he lost the chase. With a dejected tone, he asked with fading hope, “Was there a girl with him?”

"I wouldn’t know. My shift started only a few minutes back.”

He was exasperated. It seemed everything was working against him. “Where did the jet go?”

He tried not to sound rile as he imposed his query with some reluctance.

The girl knew she was giving away information that she wasn’t allowed by the protocol, but she proceeded anyway, “They are off to Tallinn."

Maxwell scribbled his number on her notepad and said, “Thank you. Do me another favor. Can you call the other attendant and find out if there was a girl in her early twenties with him?”

“I will. I have an off day tomorrow. If you schedule permit, we can meet. Maybe over Dinner?” The girl smiled as she put forward her ingenious request.

He carried a mischievous smile and responded, “Maybe.” He couldn’t have taken the chance to upset the girl. Besides she was extremely helpful.

**Chapter 46 **

**7:00 PM 18th April 2014, Lisbon, Portugal**

The nursing home was a mile off the highway exit. It was a small two-storied building appearing dwarf amongst the crowded concrete neighborhood. The van took a loop around the curve to reach the nursing home’s lobby. Two men came rushing with a stretcher for carrying the injured girl inside.

Sanchez was struggling to keep herself awake. She appeared anemic and had no strength to discern the situation or resist the proceedings. The voices appeared to come from a distance, and her body wanted to spiral down to rest.

She was put on an operating table. The doctor checked her vitals, found her pulse running low and decided to put her on a drip.   Benedikt kept watching her with a fretful expression as she went through the required surgical procedures. After about thirty minutes into the treatment, the doctor announced, “The bullet went past her calf muscle. It is a bruise, she should be fine in a few weeks.”

Benedikt thanked the doctor and put forward a question, “We have to leave now. I have a private jet and I can easily accommodate a bed inside. Do you see any issue with that?”

The doctor frowned, “I wouldn’t recommend. What if she needs doctor’s attention midair?”

“Well, in that case, you are coming with us,” Benedikt announced with a callous attitude and stormed out without waiting for his response. 

An ambulance pulled over at the entrance and soon Sanchez, the doctor, and Benedikt along with his team left for the airport. Benedikt was sitting beside Sanchez. Her eyes were closed, and her face oozed a trickle of radiance.  It was a renewed hope, an anticipation of the arrival of good times. He was relieved and waited for her to regain senses.

The doctor appeared upset. He had no option, but to follow the course. But for Benedikt, everything was falling in place. He could now be in Tallinn for the big meeting, and even better, he had Sanchez with him.

It took them less than twenty minutes to be on the plane. The jet slowly inched towards the runaway. All this while Benedikt’s entire attention was on Sanchez. She was in a deep slumber. He was glad she was getting the much-needed time to recuperate from the sufferings she had undergone.

“How would she react once she reclaims her senses? Will she get to know my state of mind? Will she accept my love?” His mind was racing with competing thoughts, and his heart was drifting away to a state of alacrity.

**Chapter 47**

**7:45 PM 18th April 2014, Lisbon, Portugal**

There was no other sound, besides the roaring sound of the jet. Everyone assumed silence, except for Benedikt. He appeared delirious. It seemed he couldn't get enough of her and kept looking at her with defiant eyes.  All his visible annoyance, unwanted trepidations, and pulsating vexations seemed to have faded away after he was assured of her good health. However, shades of despair occasionally clouded him arresting his elated soul. Deep within he knew it was his anxiousness of losing her that was causing him to quail. He had many questions for her, but he was unsure whether he would ever have the courage to speak his mind.

They were two hours into their journey when Sanchez came out of her deep slumber. She looked around with confused eyes. After a few minutes of struggle, she decided to disdain her state, and tried getting on her feet, but failed. Benedikt held her and stated in a calm voice, “You need to rest. Let me call the doctor.”

For some reason best known to her, she listened to Benedikt and remained still. Benedikt called upon the doctor. He appeared half asleep as he took Sanchez’s hand to check her vitals.

“How do you feel now?”

“Where am I?” she enquired, confused and startled.

He had no answer for her. He himself was in captivation and unsure where he was headed.

“Well, I would suggest you have some soup. I will administer an injection. It will help you to fight the fatigue." He succeeded in avoiding her question.

"She is fine now. All she needs to do is eat and rest. She should be back on her feet in a week.”  He turned his attention to Benedikt to avoid any further confrontations from her.

While Sanchez remained disconcerted Benedikt was in peace. He called upon the attendant to get Sanchez some soup and requested the doctor to join Damian at the rear end of the plane. He wanted some time alone with her.

Sanchez’s memory from the past evening started retracing in patches. She had a vague remembrance of getting dragged into a car. Besides that, everything else was a series of hallucination. And now she was on a plane, unsure of where she was headed. One thing she knew for sure that the hope of finding her father was eroding at a rapid pace. She flinched and assumed a glum outlook. Benedikt saw how her face turned pale and how a sign of uneasiness engulfed her.  He felt terrible seeing her in such a helpless state

“Let me help you sit up so that you can have your soup.”

Sanchez resisted taking any help from him. She shook her arms from his hold and looked away from him. Benedikt tried again but she held the bed rails with firmness and blurted in agitation, “Leave me alone. Will you?” there was a clear sign of frustration in her shrill voice.

Benedikt was composed, and answered in a soft low tone, “No one wants to harm you. You weren’t supposed to be shot. It was a mistake. Now if you don’t get well how will you pursue your undertakings?”

Sanchez was surprised to see the caring outlook displayed by Benedikt. She was unable to comprehend his behavior. She had eluded him for the past several days and in all seriousness, she should be a corpse by now. But Instead of questioning her, he was concerned.  She knew there had to be a motive behind his dubious behavior but failed to decipher it.

Benedikt’s strong arms defied her feeble resistance and though indisposed, she gave in to his request. He put a cushion behind her back and placed the soup tray in front of her. She followed the course and drank her soup. She was famished, and the soup tasted like heaven.

“How is the pain?” Benedikt enquired. His constant gazing was making her uncomfortable. "Where are you taking me?” Her voice had a clear shade of agitation.

"We are going to Tallinn. I guess you stole the map to be there. So, in a way, I am helping you.” He didn't mean to portray it like sarcasm, but it came out like one.

She never wanted to show her melancholy self to Benedikt so, she kept staring at the vacuum without giving anything away.

"I know it is hard for you to believe but let me reassure you no one is here to harm you. I know a lot about you, but not your motive. Tell me what is on your mind? I promise, if I can help, I would do everything to help you and in case I cannot I will let you know right away.”  Benedikt went ahead with his monologue.

Sanchez was still staring at nothingness and refused to speak.  Benedikt was a tad frustrated. By now he knew stubbornness came to her innately.   Exasperated he decided to leave her alone. He retraced back to his sofa, loosened a couple of his shirt buttons and closed his eyes in a desperate attempt to take his attention away from her.

Sanchez’s mind was in overdrive, thinking about ways to escape. One thing was evident - Benedikt had a soft corner for her. She knew he could lose a lot for her. More she weighed on the thought, more she was convinced about Benedikt being her key to escape. She wanted to reciprocate his endearment but in a deceptive manner. She wanted to be prudent to avoid making it look obvious. Luckily, she wasn’t hurt bad, but regardless she knew it would take a few days for her to recuperate. In her mind, she decided to use her healing time to come close to Benedikt.

She decided to maintain her silence for the remainder of the flight to make her charade appear more real.  From the corner of her eyes, she glanced at Benedikt and saw him sleeping in peace. The flight to Tallinn was long, and the best way to kill time was to get some respite from the daylong ordeal she had gone through, with a crowded mind and a hopeful heart she closed her eyes.

**Chapter 48**

**8:15 PM 18th April 2014, Lisbon, Portugal**

Maxwell used his charm to his advantage, for the receptionist confirmed the presence of a girl on that plane to Tallinn. He also learned she was brought to the plane on a stretcher. 

"I have to be in Tallinn as soon as possible. I am about 20 minutes away from the Lisbon Airport. Can you put me on the next available flight?” There were no greetings, no introductions, all the voice endorsed was urgency.

Maxwell had a strong hunch, he would not get any plane to Tallinn such late in the night, for Tallinn wasn’t a significant business city that could demand overnight flights. There was a moment of silence before the lady on the other side responded,” I am afraid there are no flights to Tallinn tonight. The first flight out is tomorrow morning at 6:00 AM. You can take a connecting flight tonight, but that will take you closer to Tallinn. You will still be in Tallinn only by tomorrow morning.”

Waiting until morning wasn’t an option so he decided to explore in getting a private jet. He knew it wouldn’t be easy. It demanded to talk to his superiors and convince them for such an exorbitant expense. To the extent possible, he avoided any interactions with his seniors. However, the situation demanded him to break the mores, so he continued with a reluctant voice, “Is Drake in office?”

The lady was surprised to hear Drake’s name. She knew Maxwell well, so in a teasing tone she blurted, “Well I hope you aren’t resigning.”

Maxwell saw the jovial sarcasm in her voice. He had neither the mood nor the time to flirt, so he insisted in a firm tone, “I am running against time. Put me to him. Will you?”

Without wasting any more time, she transferred him to Drake. She knew Maxwell meant business today.

Drake had a surreal attitude towards everything. Maxwell usually restricted his conversations with him to a bare minimum and procrastinate it till it became inevitable. Though Drake was aware of Maxwell's intractable behavior, he tolerated him because he was the best he had. It was an unstated protocol, and they both managed to stay out of conflict by ceasing their discussions to the extent possible.

Maxwell heard the deep-throated coarse hello on the other end of the line. He knew the voice well and continued, “I am close to busting the TransPacific Racket. I need your urgent help in getting a private jet arranged at Montijo airport near Lisbon. It would be a long flight, and I would appreciate if you could get me a good throttle engine.”

The callous attitude shown by Maxwell made Drake furious. There was no explanation whatsoever for such an atrocious demand. He tried controlling his anger and demanded, “Would you care to explain why you cannot take a regular flight out of Lisbon. Where the hell are you headed? How many hours you would save anyways?” There was a clear denial in his tone.

Maxwell was agitated. At some other time, he would have fought back, but not today. He wrestled away his bulging rage and responded with a flat voice, “One of the main accomplices is on the run to Estonia. I am afraid he would find his way to Russia moment he lands there. Every moment is precious sir!” He stretched the word sir to underline the importance of his demand.

It was late in the evening and Drake hardly had the energy left to argue. Besides, Maxwell always had the best arguments to convince him. After a long pause, he announced, “Thirty minutes you should have the jet” and abruptly concluded the conversation.

Maxwell was relieved to expunge out of the situation. Next, he had to talk to Stephen. He called Adriana’s house number and the line was picked before he could hear the phone ring.

Maxwell stated in a hurried tone, “Take the car and drive as fast as you could to Montijo airport. Do you have a GPS with you?”

Stephen responded, “I called Jules. He will drive me.”

“Good.”

He knew he had lost some crucial time, but he was still hoping to get Sanchez free from Benedikt. He had no clue how, however, now that he knew where she was, he was roaring with confidence and beaming with some newfound zeal. 

**Chapter 49**

**4:30 AM 19th April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia**

The jet touched down in Tallinn right before the break of dawn.  Damian sprung to action and got busy on his phone ensuring everything was in order. The jet came to a complete halt and the flight stairs were rolled down. Damian was the first to get off the plane. There was a large black van waiting for them. He had a quick word with the team on the ground and soon all of them were back on the plane.

Benedikt put a casual glance towards Sanchez and found her wide-awake. He cleared his throat and tried to put his point forward sounding as genuine as possible. “I request you not to resist the move. Like I have told you before, I don’t mean any harm.”

To his surprise, he found her in profound quietness, as if she had surrendered to the situation. And like before, hardly bothered to respond. When asked, she lay on the mobile bed without any resistance. Soon the three men scooped the stretcher and hurled her off the plane. It was still dark in Tallinn. The neon light hanging from the hanger was illuminating the open canvas in a bright white shade. It was quite cold for a spring morning, echoing the region’s continental weather sentiments. There was a light wind blowing adding to the coldness. Benedikt tucked his hand into his trousers pocket and paced down the flight stairs, unsure what was causing him to shiver more, the weather or the uncanny girl.

He hopped onto the front seat and sat next to the van driver while Damian along with another person sat alongside Sanchez. There were no greetings and no pleasantries exchanged, an unusual quietness impinged inside the van. The roads were empty, and they drove without any delays to their destination.

The grand hotel palace was one of the most elegant hotels in Tallinn located next to the town's Freedom Square. They had two presidential suites reserved. Sanchez was by her own in one of the suites. This time Benedikt wanted to take no chance. Her room was well guarded to prohibit any escape route. Benedikt decided to take some rest and went straight to his suite without any conversation with her.

It was ten in the morning when Benedikt woke up. After a long time, Benedikt rested well. His mind could finally repose in peace after last few days’ ordeals. After taking a quick shower he went to check on Sanchez’s wellbeing. The guard upon seeing Benedikt went ahead and knocked on the door and waited for a while before opening the door. He found Sanchez sitting on the bed and the doctor performing some examinations.

“Good morning!” he smiled as he entered the room. The dining area had a nice spread of breakfast ready to be devoured. The doctor responded back in a pleasant tone, “Good Morning.”

Sanchez remained quiet, and conveniently ignored Benedikt’s presence.

“How do you feel now?”

Sanchez looked at him intensely, and after a long pause responded, “I want to talk to you in private.”

A sudden surge of excitement engulfed him.

“Would you like to have breakfast as we speak?”

She tried stepping out of the bed but failed. Benedikt leaped forward to give her a helping arm. She hesitated first, but when she failed to balance without support, she gave away her resistance and held his stretched hand to balance her stature.

Benedikt was jolted by an unexplainable sensation by her touch. A sense of exhilaration regained him, and he slowly drifted to a euphoric state. The moments he had spent with her in the past came rushing back, making him thrilled, nervous, dizzy, all at once. He controlled his surge of emotions with great difficulty and helped her sit down at the breakfast table. His mind was racing. He wanted to speak but was refrained from the fear of stating something unwarranted that may be uncalled for. And before he could decide the best course forward Sanchez’s soft undertone voice broke the uncomfortable quietness. "Thank you."

“You are thanking me for what?” Though he was thrilled hearing those words he eschewed from showing any emotions.

Sanchez ignored his question and continued further, “How did you come to know about my location?”

Any conversation was good for Benedikt “I will not hide anything. Your mysterious disappearance from the hotel made me conclude that the only reason for you to come close to me must be the map. The map was linked to TransPacific. After many unsuccessful attempts to nab you my mind started racing and I was curious to find if there was anything common between you and TransPacific.” He took a pause to observe her. She was attentive, her eyes glued to him, and her ears were giving him a patient listening.

He continued further, “Soon I came to know that your real name is Sanchez, which made it easy to trace you. I found that you were an employee of TransPacific. I checked on everything you did at the company. I looked through the security logs, emails and everything electronically available about you. After laborious skimming of a plethora of information, I zeroed down to few names in TransPacific that were linked to you. John Patterson was one such name. “

He poured a glass of orange juice for her and continued further, “I must tell you, John didn’t buzz at all. We tried all measures but failed to get anything from him. We then concluded to use other means to find something meaningful. Checking John’s phone records was one such medium, which led us to a big breakthrough. We found that he received a call from Sweden and immediately after that he made a call to Lisbon.  Knowing your name made it easy to trace your whereabouts. We already knew from the airport records that you were in Lisbon. We put the two together and figured where you were. We knew John called Adriana and I put her house on watch. Rest you know.”

He took a pause to observe her reaction, but Sanchez gave nothing away. After a frustrating wait, Benedikt queried, “Do you have anything to say?”

She was playing with the coffee cup and had no intention to respond. Benedikt continued further, “You invaded my privacy, came close to me and made me behave like human again. I know it was for a few hours we were together, but those moments were enough for me to discern. And against all my perceived absurdity I fell for you. The more you eluded the stronger that craving became. I know it may sound boisterous and insignificant for you, but the truth is I long for you. When you are around, there is calmness. There is serenity.”

Benedikt couldn’t believe he spoke those words. He ceased the conversation, left the table and slowly sauntered his way to the large glass wall. He stood by the window staring at the vacuum with his back facing her.

Sanchez knew Benedikt was stating the truth. She knew if she could manage it well she might be able to use his help. A ray of hope, distinct and clear in the midst of all the madness. She wanted to make it look real, for she knew if she missed the mark, her plan would be in complete jeopardy.

Benedikt was still looking at the void when Sanchez responded in a low tone, “You don’t want to know what I am after?”

Benedikt sprinted back to her. “Try me once. I won’t disappoint you” There was excitement in his voice and his eyes were showering eagerness, reflecting the innocence of a young boy.

“Once you hear me you will forget your promise. If you truly mean your words why don’t let me go? I promise you, I will not keep anything against you.” Sanchez looked at him squarely as she uttered those words.

And at that very moment, Benedikt’s energetic stature got transformed to a despair being. His candor was miserably failing to convince her to trust him. He gave another attempt, “Try trusting me once. If I am unable to help you I will do exactly what you want me to do. I will let you go.” His voice was soft and reflected a deep yearning for her.

He was looking straight into her eyes, patiently waiting for her response. Sanchez couldn’t manage to hold her gaze for long. She saw a different shade of Benedikt, someone who meant his words. The last thing she wanted was to get swayed. She took her eyes off him, “I need time.” She kept looking away, refusing any further eye contact with him.

Benedikt found some hope. “Sure. I will leave you alone for now. I will meet you for lunch at noon. Hopefully, it would give you enough time to decide.” He appeared relieved. His voice had a subtle tone that failed to hide his excitement. He smiled at her and hastily scurried away.

Sanchez kept looking at him until he disappeared behind the closing door. She saw in him a child, a restless soul, and an honest man. Suddenly a wave of sadness covered her soul. She hated the thought of messing with his emotions. Her heart ached for him, but she had bigger problems to solve and until she finds the answers, falling in love didn’t fit into any of her schemes. She knew she would be encouraging Benedikt, and at times she might give him vibes she would never mean. A wave of guilt swept over her soul, leaving her bereaved and hapless. She tried hard to reason with her mind, but her heart wasn’t aligned to her conceived rational. To the best of her ability she put her earnest effort to divert attention, but more she tried, more strongly it came rushing back.

Benedikt came to her suite sharp at noon and found Sanchez reposing right on the breakfast table. He couldn’t take his eyes off her. Not a shade of the ordeal she went through was shadowing her graceful visage. Her hair was unkempt and was scattered all over her face. Her long eyelashes from the resting eyelids were touching the edges of her cheek giving her a fairy mien appearance. Even in her sleep, her fuller lips were radiating an alluring glare of a subdued smile.

The raging sound of Benedikt’s phone stirred the calmness of the room. He cursed himself as he fumbled to switch it off. Sanchez was awake by now as she looked through her sleepy eyes crinkling a shade of happiness.

Benedikt was mesmerized, as she slowly moved her lips and stated, “Can we go out for lunch? I am tired of resting”

Benedikt wasn’t sure what to say and he blurted, “Let me check with the doctor” and without waiting for her response he rushed out to call the doctor. The doctor checked her and gave her the clearance to go out. A pair of walking sticks was ordered and soon they were on their way out.

It was a mild afternoon and the sky was spotless with no trace of any cloud. The slow-moving spring breeze was mellowing the effect of the brightly shining sun. The hotel doors opened to the turquoise colored multi haze occult, which was standing tall in the vastness of the town’s freedom square. The atmosphere was gorgeous and lively. People from all spheres were crowding the cobbled web streets, some with a purpose and some to admire the gothic outlook of the continental town. Sunny days were short-lived in this part of the world and people flocked out to the openness at the slightest hint of a warmer weather.

They took a left turn from the hotel. Despite Benedikt’s repeated insistence Sanchez chose to walk instead of riding the car. She wanted to explore the city and break away from her confinement. Benedikt tried his best to match the slow pace of his limping lady. She was doing well with her walking sticks, and they aimlessly walked away from the hotel.

Freedom Square was the town’s center of attraction. The streets were offering plenty of options for visitors to explore. There were restaurants with open sitting areas, street food venues, and vendors selling souvenirs. The slight chilliness in the air was hardly a hindrance for people to sit in an open area and enjoy their lunch. After about five minutes of casual promenading Benedikt sheepishly enquired, “Do you have a place on mind?”

Sanchez responded with a teasing tone, “You don’t have patience. Do you?”

He tried hard to sound as normal as he could and blurted hastily, “You have a wounded leg. I am not sure if walking for long is good for you.”

“I am looking for Estonian eat out place but looks like this place is influenced by tourists.” There was a hint of tease in her faint smile as she put forward her observation.

This was in his comfort alley as he responded with excitement, “You should have told me before. There is a small place right around the street that serves by far the most astonishing local delight. It isn’t a fancy place, more like a hole in the wall, but the food is unbeatable. What do you think?”

Sanchez was enamored by his charm. He appeared like a teenage boy who was excited to show some hidden treasure to the love of his life. There was a spark in his eyes and warmth of togetherness. She couldn’t resist from smiling and announced in an equivocal note, “I am ready to explore.”

They took a corner seat by the large window ceiling with a refreshing view to the streets. As Sanchez was resting her legs to acquire a comfortable position Benedikt took the conversation forward, “Have you been to Tallinn before?”

Sanchez smiled and answered in a syllable, “Never.”

“Allow me to order the drink. May I?”

Sanchez smiled with affirmation and discreetly watched him from the corner of her eyes, as he got engrossed in the menu. The waiter appeared with a broad smile on his face greeting his guests, “May I get a bottle of Kali?”

Sanchez was getting dangerously attracted to him. His suave, always being in complete control of the situation and taking her in an authoritative possession was getting increasingly addictive. She sipped her drink and enjoyed its smooth texture. Soon she was hurled back from her thoughts with Benedikt’s inquisitive statement, “So tell me your story.”

For some reason, Sanchez did not want to keep anything away from him. She took another sip of her drink and reeled her mind back to that fateful Sunday morning.

**Chapter 50**

**12:30 PM 19th April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia**

“I can never forget that dreadful Sunday. I saw him for the last time from the windowsill of my room, slowly drifting away into the morning mist, never to return.”

Sanchez was gazing into the blank, lost in her own world. Benedikt didn’t want to invade into her secluded world with his perilous probing and held his inquisitiveness closely guarded. She took a long pause, looked dead straight into his eyes and continued,

“Our family was all we had. Mom, dad, brother and me, we were so happy together. We played in unity, laughed together and were so content in each other’s company. Those were such a blissful time, until one day when our father disappeared. He vanished in such an implausible way that I am still not able to comprehend the bearings of that fateful morning.

He never worked on a Sunday, but we kept our hopes alive, assuming he went to work, tending to some urgent request from office. But the day went by without any news or sign of him. We combed every corner of our small town, contacted all his friends, and called every person whom we assumed he would have any connection with, but he was nowhere to be found. When all our options ran out we called for the police. The police officer traced the path to the bakery where my father used to go every Sunday morning, and after some inquiries, he found about a suspicious-looking vehicle parked right opposite to the shop around the time when my father disappeared. While the bakery attendant didn’t see anyone, the choir group from the Kirk right next to the bakery, saw a person entering the car. The description provided by them matched my dad’s appearance, and all our dwindling hopes came crashing down. We tried everything to know about the car but failed miserably. As time advanced the police assumed nothing could be done and their effort to find him gradually faded away.”

The waiter interrupted their conversation, as he spread out the lunch on the table. Benedikt was keenly listening to her and refrained from voicing any views. He offered her the basket of bread and waited for her to continue.

This was the first-time Sanchez was narrating the entire proceedings to someone. The scenes from the past had played in her nightmares a thousand times in the past but recounting her experience to someone was strangely relieving. It was as if she was heaving away a long-carried burden. And in that obtrusive moment, all her ostensible guards were slowly but surely dissolving away. Unsure of her inner self, she gave a deceiving nonchalant look at Benedikt and continued,

“Six months went pass without any news. Our only hope was Mr. MacCallan, the police officer. In the beginning, he did everything he could to find dad, but soon he withdrew away from the case. He stopped answering our calls, and when we confronted him, he appeared callous, not giving any convincing answers. After many unsuccessful antagonizing face-offs our hope of getting anything meaningful insights from him diminished.

When the police officer turned cold, I knew the only way I could get dad back was taking things into my own hands. I was determined but had no clue what to do. Though there was chaos everywhere, I somehow managed to keep my determination strong. And one Sunday morning I decided to put together a plan.”

She took a quick break to chew her bread, gulped some Kali, and took her narration forward,

“I took an old notebook and started making a list of initial things that I thought were important to initiate my investigation. And after a lot of deliberation, I could pin down to three

- Talk to dad’s friend from work

- Look through dad’s office bag

- Find out more about dad’s workplace

I decided to start with dad’s friend first. My father was discrete about anything that faintly came close to office work, the only co-worker name he had mentioned in the past, in all likelihood by accident, was John Patterson. I wanted to meet John, but besides the name, I had no clue where to find him. I tried hard to recall anything that dad might have told me in the past that could help me find John. I tried rekindling old memories; my long walks with dad, but nothing came to my mind that could relate back to John.

As I was going through my options, I remembered how curious I always was to find the places he used to travel. He was a contract deliveryman and worked for multiple logistics companies on short assignments. Whenever I enquired about his travel, he used to say in his typical tenor ‘San, one day I will take you to all those places. I want you to see it for yourself’. Those words from dad led me to an incident that happened about a year before he disappeared.

A year before he disappeared, one morning I was getting my geography project done. The project was about tracing the migratory birds’ travel path in and out of Scotland. It was a Saturday morning and dad was at home. I approached him for help. While plotting the map I recalled enquiring about his travel paths and places he visits. This time he took the pencil from my hand and drew some lines on the map. I wasn’t sure if they were real or randomly drawn lines, but that was my only concrete lead. I decided to find the notebook and study it closely.”

In the middle of her narration, she concluded on her mind she was giving away way too much detail than what was required. Something in her was making her pour out everything she had held in her heart all this time. She sheepishly gazed at him and found him looking at her with intrigued interest.

“Sorry for taking you through all the unwanted details. Let me come to the point...” Unsure how to react she fumbled as she tried to shorten her narration.

Benedikt interrupted her in between before she could proceed forward. He stretched his hand to hold hers’, squeezed it gently and hushed in an imperious tone, “I like details.”

To state that the words were reassuring was an understatement. She couldn’t take her gaze away from him. Gnarled in his ensemble she slowly continued further,

“It was a lazy Saturday afternoon, mom was at the church and Stephen was outside dribbling a soccer ball. There was no one to disturb me. I sprinted to the basement, turned on the light and closed the door behind me. There were racks full of old toys, books, and bags. I went straight to the bookrack. There was a huge pile of books, kept in no order, filling the space haphazardly. I pulled a stool, put it close to the tall rack, stood over it and from the top shelf, pulled out a heap of books. The books were covered with layers of dust. My eyes were looking for the geography notebook. After a laborious long search, I finally located the notebook I was looking for. I pulled it out hurriedly, flipped through it and located the page that dad had marked with a pencil.

I latched my room door to ensure no one invades my privacy. Peeped through the window and saw Stephen still dribbling the ball in the front yard, pulled the window curtain to curtail any scope of trespassing and finally settled on the bed with the notebook. I turned to the map page where dad had put the imprints. There were several lines he had drawn showing the places he visits. Between the crisscross of lines was a place, which was marked with a star. The place was Ayr, a port town in the southern part of Scotland.

I knew Ayr was a port city and a major transportation hub. Dad was a delivery person so there was a high possibility he would go to Ayr. I vaguely assumed, John would be in Ayr too. Before I zeroed on Ayr I wanted to do one last validation. I decided to check dad’s office bag to see if there was anything related to Ayr. It was in a large closet in our parents’ bedroom.

I rushed to the closet, opened it in haste and found all dads’ belongings neatly arranged. His clothes, shoes and the office bag everything was there. It was overwhelming to see his belongings and the fact he wasn’t there loomed by the mind.  I forced myself out of my brooding and took the bag out of the closet. There were official receipts, some pens, and a notebook. I looked through the receipts and found all of them with Ayr address. I quickly flipped through the notebook, which mostly had addresses from various places in Scotland and England. I took the receipts and the notebook and left the bag in the closet.

All my findings so far were pointing to Ayr, and undoubtedly Ayr became my default choice to initiate my investigation. Though Ayr was about three hours away from our village, to go there for a while, I had to find a good reason. I safely secured the notebook and the receipt in my school bag. All I had to do was to work on a plan to convince mom.

It was half past six in the evening when mom came back from the church. Stephen was reading a book in the living room and I was in the kitchen fixing dinner. She came straight to the kitchen, gave me a big hug and I still remember what she told me, ‘you are a big girl now. Thanks for the helping hand’.

My mind was too busy playing back the plan I had rehearsed earlier. I knew I may have only one chance to execute it right or else it will be a long impending wait, which I neither had the patience for nor the time. As we sat around the dinner table I launched into the first part of the plan. I told her that I wanted to go to Ayr with the pretext of our home being too remorseful as it kept reminding me of dad, and it was impacting my studies immensely. Mom was reluctant, but she saw the sense in what I was saying. Seeing her soften a bit I put my second part of the plan forward. I mentioned Aunt Naiena, who was in Ayr, with whom I could stay while I was there.

I knew I hit the chord right. She took her time but eventually agreed.

I was in Ayr. Aunt Naiena was welcoming and ensured I had everything I need. I started my school and before I could notice a month passed by. I was anxious and desperate to find answers but didn’t have any means to get to it. I started doing some odd jobs after college hours and was earning enough money to rent a place.”

Sanchez reclaimed her mind from some diverting emotions, pondered for a moment and reflected in an undertone voice the details about her stay in Ayr. She told him how she started working for TransPacific, her quest in finding John Paterson and her interaction with Bradley. She told him how she found about him and how she managed to get the map.

Benedikt was listening with great interest. He absorbed every bit of what she narrated without voicing any opinion. Giving away the entire story was never her plan. She started off with the presumption to mention only the absolute essentials, but her heart was in complete control of her mind as she barely held away from any secrets.
