It was a long conversation. It took Sanchez good twenty more minutes after lunch to conclude her part. Her eyes were moist, but inexplicably she appeared liberated and exultant.

Benedikt was still quiet. He slowly crawled his hands towards her direction and held her hand softly. Sanchez's heart missed many beats. She experienced the same rush and the fervor, which she had experienced with him not too long ago. She tried hard to oppress her avidity but failed miserably as she squirmed vulnerably. Her desolate state was further rattled by the soft mesmerizing voice of Benedikt, “I am sorry you had to go through such ordeal. I will do whatever it takes to find your father.”

Sanchez saw the grit in his voice. And at that moment, she knew he meant every word he spoke. How she wished all this while to find someone who could drive all her problems away. She desired to crash into his arms and let him sweep her away to a faraway land and miraculously dissolve all her sufferings. This time she wanted to live all over again, but for her. She was floating away, hallucinated and delirious, gently dissolving in his aura.

“I have decided. I will put you on a flight home tomorrow and I will come and meet you once I resolve your father’s mystery.” Those words from Benedikt shook her existence. She came crashing down from that euphoric state to a melancholy being.

Sanchez slipped her hand away from him in urgency. She was flustered and couldn’t believe what she heard. She cursed herself for getting carried away. She was incensed and with angst in her heart she bellowed with an orotund voice, “This is my battle and I cannot let anyone else fight it for me.”

Her statement made Benedikt irritated and his tone reflected his frustration. “You know nothing about these people. I know them well. Knowingly, I can’t put you in any more trouble.”

Sanchez was fuming with anger and she didn’t hold back as she blurted pompously, “Who are you to decide what I should do?” She leaped forward to grab her sticks, and before Benedikt could react she was limping her way out of the restaurant.

Benedikt dashed behind her, held her by her arms and shouted with rage, “Are you out of your mind? You are nothing but a stubborn gunk.”

Sanchez couldn’t believe what she heard, and she responded in an equally shrieked tone, “How dare you?”

Sanchez anger was beyond comprehension. Benedikt could see her fuming with dander and he regretted his conduct. He changed his stance and with a lowered tone declared, “Alright I am sorry. I shouldn’t have shouted at you. Can we go back to the hotel and talk?”

Sanchez’s resentment was uncontrollable, and she stormed forward without uttering any words. He saw her turning towards the hotel. A strong pang of sadness loomed his mind. He knew she had gone through a lot and he had to be more considerate and emphatic, provided he gets another chance.   **\**

**Chapter 51**

**3:30 PM 19th April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia**

Benedikt was flipping through the television channels in random without much interest. His mind was reeling back every scene that happened in the afternoon. More he brooded, more delirious he became, thinking how he ruined a perfect afternoon because of his pernicious behavior. Deep under he was convinced he was planning the best for her, only if he would have acted in a more sensible manner. His heart urged to mend things, but he knew the best way to deal with the situation was by giving time, but the wait wasn't easy. 5:00 PM is the time he mentally locked on his mind to go visit Sanchez, and since then stared the clock countless times. He still had one hour to kill and had already exhausted all options to go past the hour. In that moment of despair, he scooted out of his quandary​ and leaped towards Sanchez’s suite with some renewed purpose.

With some hesitation he nudged the door, announcing his arrival. After a few long anxious knocks, he finally heard her voice, “come in."

Sanchez was by the writing desk scribbling something on a notepad with great interest. She stayed focused on her work and made his presence non-existent. Benedikt sauntered towards her with quiet​ steps and occupied the ottoman next to the sofa. He took a moment to regain his composure and with utmost care eased out one word at a time, "I am so sorry for my behavior. I have no right to decide things on your behalf."

Sanchez gave him a long gaze. And in that moment of supreme silence, he observed a sense of calmness in the cerulean texture of her eyes. And then her unexpected subtle voice rattled his brooding, "I am sorry too. You need to understand, I can't compromise when it concerns dad."

Those words were magical. All his anxieties melted, and with a sense of excitement he chirped, "I won’t cross the line again. That's a promise."

Sanchez chose not to respond, and instead passed a small smile. For a while, he kept staring at her dazed and disillusioned. He was pulled out of his diminutive state when Sanchez, uncomfortable from his piercing eyes, took her stare away from him and started to scribble on her notepad again.

Benedikt embarrassed with his conduct tried to mend the situation and announced, "This time I want to hear you first. Tell me what is on your mind?"

"I don't have a plan. All I know is that I want to be on that ship. I want to take that voyage and find him."

Benedikt found her staring in the vacuum, lost in oblivion. He knew she meant every word she stated, and rightfully it was her battle. He had thought about this before, but now he was certain.

"I have a plan."

He declared and took a momentary pause to observe her reaction. Sanchez's​ eyes revealed inquisitiveness, and she brought her attention back to him. Without waiting any further, with an increased enthusiasm he continued, "You will join me as my assistant. If your dad is around, we will find him. But I need a promise. You need to be open and honest with​ me. Don't do things without my knowledge and I assure you, together we will find your father."

Before he could descry, Sanchez managed to stand using the table as a support and hugged him. "I can't thank you enough. I promise I will do exactly what you want me to do, and I am sorry for my outrageous behavior in the restaurant."

Benedikt was appalled. For a moment, he stood there without any reciprocation to her embrace. Slowly he put his arms around her. The moment was ecstatic, beyond anything he could have ever imagine, and his mind drifted into​ nothingness.

Sanchez parted away from his gentle squeeze and said, “I also have a request to make” she paused to get his reaction.

Benedikt couldn’t find his voice. And after some struggle, he responded with a tremulous voice​, “Sure. Go ahead”

“My brother must be worried. I must let him know that I am fine and I will be gone for a few weeks”

Signs of hesitation came back on his face, and after a brief pause, he concluded, "Ok go ahead. But make sure he isn’t aware of​ the trip.”

Sanchez nodded in agreement. Benedikt left her room with the promise to return after a few hours.

Sanchez was consumed in shades of mixed emotions as she tried to rationalize her comportment. She knew for certain that Benedikt had fallen for her. For a moment, she was ridden with guilt for playing with his emotions. Then she comforted herself by justifying her actions towards the betterment of her family.

Above all the uncertainties, her mind was clouded with another apprehension, which she was battling hard to reason with. It was her consternation of falling in love. She knew sooner or later she had to confront with it. But now was not the time, at least that’s how she made peace with her confused self. 

**Chapter 52**

**4:00 PM 19th April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia**

The phone made a loud noise and filled the otherwise quiet setup of the coffee shop with a shriek dissonance. Stephen picked the phone and gave it a good stare, trying to recognize the caller. Maxwell was sitting right across him and was watching the episode without much interest. Something suddenly crossed his mind, and before Stephen could react, he leaped forward and snatched the phone from his hand.

With a shaking voice, he quizzed, “Who is it?”

“Where is Stephen?” came a curt reply.

Maxwell wouldn’t have failed to recognize that voice even in his sleep. It was Sanchez and he heard the subtle agitation in her terse response. His heart leaped with elation. He scampered to hand the phone back to Stephen, for he wanted to bring Sanchez’s anxiety to rest.

By now Stephen had a fair hunch that the caller was his sister. He was perspiring, and his heart pulsated with vigor. He struggled to find his voice, and in a disembodied tone he said, “Hello!”

“Stephen, it is me. You don’t sound okay. Are you, all right?”

Stephen happiness had no boundaries. He was euphoric, and it was difficult for him to conceal his exultation

“Oh! Sanchez, I can’t tell you how happy I am to hear your voice. Where are you? How is your wound? I want to meet you “, he was on a never-ending spree with his inquisitive barrage.

“I am fine. Who was the other person with you? His voice appeared so familiar." Sanchez queried.

“That’s Maxwell, you knew him as Bradley. It is a long story and I will tell you more when we meet. Where are you? I want to come and get you right now.” He exclaimed everything in one breath.

“I am not in Portugal anymore. I have traveled to Estonia” there was an unstated melancholy that loomed in her fading voice.

“Well, I know that. In fact, I am in Estonia too. Where in Estonia are you? How did you escape from Benedikt?”

His revelation astounded her.

“How did you find about my whereabouts? I am so happy to learn you are here!” There was a spark in her voice, and suddenly all the gloominess that persisted all throughout her conversation vanished.

“It was all because of Maxwell. Rest I will tell you when we meet. Give me your address.” Stephen knew there was no end to their conversations. There was a lot of explanation both had to do and he knew Benedikt’s worry hadn’t yet obliterated.

"Where are you? I will come and meet you.”

Stephen was puzzled, but he put his curiosity to rest and gave her the coffee shop address.

“I am starting now. Looks like you are in the area. Shouldn’t take long. See you soon."

Maxwell was silently listening to every word that Stephen spoke. By now he knew Sanchez was coming to meet them.

“Did she dodge Benedikt? How come she wasn’t in a hurry? She took all her time to talk. Why didn’t she want them to come and get her?” Maxwell’s mind was spinning. It was a mystery. A conundrum he knew he would only resolve after their meeting. For now, he wanted to enjoy the anticipation of meeting the person who had ruled his soul from the time he met her four years back.

**Chapter 53**

**4:30 PM 19th April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia**

Maxwell’s heart was pulsating hard and a hint of perspiration clouded his demeanor. This was it. After many failed attempts, he was finally going to meet her, and he was anxiously waiting for the moment to accost.

And then he experienced an unmistakable distinct aura of her presence consuming the atmosphere. He was desperate to get her sight, but she was nowhere to be seen. Was it a hallucination he experienced or was the uncanny waft brought her existence from a faraway place? In that state of delusion, he craned his neck to get a clearer sight. His probing eyes searched every corner but failed to trace her. However, her impressions were everywhere. Lost in his world, and driven by his intuition he turned around, and there she was standing at the entrance of the coffee shop.

Everything stopped for him, the noise disappeared, the people faded, and all inhabitation expunged. All he could see was her. It was the most serene emotion he experienced in a long time, and his apprehensions skimmed away in her bright composure. She was wearing her petite smile. Soon she spotted them and with a limp, sauntered towards the table. Maxwell’s heart skipped many beats. He was waiting for this moment all this while, and when the time presented itself his heart went numb. 

Maxwell couldn’t get his eyes off her.  The floral print shirt and the knee length black skirt was gracing her fragile frame affably. Her shoulder length brown hair was flowing freely over her brightened face. Her large inviting eyes were beholding some unfathomable stories and her full red lips were wearing some unexplainable warmth. The top two button of her shirt was left open exposing her long neck, and the fact that Maxwell still held calmness was the epitome of bravery. She was the prose for his poetry, the central protagonist of his stories and hope for what future beholds.

Sanchez was extremely uncomfortable seeing Bradley. He was always a mystery to him and his sudden reappearance was puzzling her even more. She was determined to send him away before breaking into any conversation with Stephen. She knew how vulnerable her brother was and with him around she didn't want to divulge a word about her plan.

She approached the table and demanded in a firm tone, “Bradley, what are you doing here?”

The sharpness in her tone penetrated straight through Maxwell. He was taken off-guard by the sudden unexpected assault. He was searching for the right words and before he could react Sanchez jolted him further, “If you are here for the map. I am ready to give it to you, but you have to promise me, after that you will stay away from us.”

Stephen was watching the episode with extreme displeasure, and decided to intervene, “Sanchez, his real name is Maxwell and he isn’t a bad person. In fact,” Sanchez cut him off in between and blurted, “You stay out of this.”

Despite Sanchez’s warning, Stephen interrupted again, “Look, you need to give the man a chance to explain."

Sanchez had never seen her brother so determined with his opinion before. His voice was firm, oozing with confidence. There was something in him that calmed her agitation.  She pulled a chair, put her walking sticks on the side, and sat down with reluctance, “All right. I will listen to your part of the story. Bradley, Maxwell or whosoever you are,  go ahead” the sarcasm in her voice was so evident that it was hard not to observe.

Maxwell knew today was the day to end all charades and raise the long-standing curtain from everything that he held all this while. "By now you know my name is Maxwell. I am an undercover agent at Scotland Yard. I have been on a secret mission for the past several years to expose TransPacific’s illegal trafficking. The police department helped me to get a job at TransPacific Ayr’s office. Few times I came quite close to exposing them, but every time I fell a tad short. You can either call it my failure or give credit to TransPacific’s prudence.”

Maxwell took a pause to take a sip of his coffee. He could clearly see Sanchez watching him with skepticism. He gazed away from her and continued further, “Staying close to Evan helped me to get clarity on how the racket was executed. Though I succeeded in building a level of trust with Evan, it was still not enough for me to be part of his inner circle and was never exposed to any insider conversation.

During my stay in Evan’s office, Benedikt’s name used to surface from time to time. A strange thing happened one day. Evan’s office corridor was cordoned off from everyone’s access. No one could be anywhere near that area, and the place was guarded as if it was a military zone.

After that incident, my curiosity to learn about Benedikt increased multi-fold. I noticed that Benedikt’s visit to Evan’s office was always unannounced and it was almost impossible to plan anything. So finally, I decided to plant a bug in the executive conference room where they usually used to meet.

Despite having access to the floor, it wasn’t an easy job to execute. There was a dedicated security person for Evan’s floor and the place was under 24-hour security surveillance.

Evan occasionally used to bring us to the executive room for some regular official meetings. He preferred the conference room than his office for all team discussions. I knew these visits were my only chance to put a stealth microphone, but with the cameras around, it was almost impossible to pull something of that magnitude.”

Maxwell took a break again to observe Sanchez's reaction. He found her guards dropped, and unlike before she was more involved listening to his narration. He gulped some more coffee and continued, “After carefully considering alternate possibilities I formulated my game plan and waited for our next team meeting. I didn’t have to wait for long. Soon we were called upon to discuss some quality improvement plan for our logistics division. There was a decorative pot on one of the side table. In an unpretentious way, I pushed the piece to the floor.  It was a ceramic pot and crashed on the hard-wooden floor breaking into pieces. I tried putting a travesty showing how sorry I was and offered to replace it with a new one. Though Evan didn't bother, the next day I bought a vase and put it back in the conference room. The only difference was, this time the vase had a hidden microphone.

It was a long two-month wait before Benedikt resurfaced. The microphone worked like a charm. During their meeting, they referred to a secret map, which was the pathway to all the illicit trafficking. I also learned that Benedikt was the custodian of it, and from their conversation, it appeared that the plan was revisited every quarter. I desperately wanted the map but getting to Benedikt’s den was not an easy task. He was secretive, never stayed at one location and hard to trace.

During that period, you joined TransPacific. You were the sharpest intern I ever met. You were intelligent, always executed your job to perfection and you had such a charming outlook. I found out everything about you. While my sole intention was to know more about you, in that process I also discovered that you were taking an extreme interest in Evan.

My first impression was you are part of some government secret service agency. I tried every source I knew to confirm my hunch, but it turned out to be negative. Unable to resolve your mysterious outlook I concluded it could be your sheer inquisitiveness. Then I thought I might use your help to get to Benedikt. You know how I confronted you and put you through the training. I learned that Benedikt participated in the annual poker game in Sweden. The only place I knew where Benedikt appeared in public and the only place you could have any chance to meet him.

After I gave you the tip of Benedikt’s Poker adventure, I had followed you in complete secrecy. My sole motive was to jump in for your rescue if the situation becomes unfavorable.”

He took a deep sigh and assumed a break from his monotonous spill. By this time, Sanchez was deeply engrossed in his narration. Her expressions have mellowed, and her rage gave way to empathy. Stephen was watching a different Maxwell - one who was tender, one who could be vulnerable, and one who cared.

Maxwell continued further, “I was there when you were climbing down from Benedikt’s room, I followed you on the long train ride, chased you on the streets of Zurich and the plane to Lisbon. I concluded you didn’t need any help. You were too good for Benedikt or any of his accomplices.

At the Lisbon airport, you not only dodged Benedikt’s men but evaded me as well. And instead of coming after you I decided to chase them.  Partly because I knew where you were headed. But that was a terrible mistake. I underestimated Benedikt’s ability and his clout.”

There was a long pause before Maxwell decided to break the uncomfortable eerie between them, “I have spoken nothing but the truth, and I hope you will trust my words."

Sanchez's eyes were soft, and she tried hard to keep her dissembled composure appear calm. She never expected Maxwell to provide such a candid response to her implacable probe. She ignored Maxwell’s question, and in a gruff voice stated, “I am not alarmed by your presence anymore. I must tell you guys something. I have an opportunity to be on the voyage that starts two days from now. Don’t ask me how I got access, but I want you to know that I will be safe.”

She took a pause to gauge their reactions. She expected them to be astounded, and rightfully they were downright appalled. She continued further in haste, as she knew she might be interrupted if the silence is prolonged for long. “Maxwell our goals have not changed – we want to demolish TransPacific. I will get you all the evidence to bring down this proscribed swindle.  You need to trust me on this." She looked into his eyes to decipher his thoughts.

"I can’t leave you with those wolves. They are dangerous. Losing dad tormented our family, and if anything happens to you, both mom and I will perish” this time it was Stephen.  His voice took a flail turn as it trailed laced with anxiousness.

Sanchez leaned forward, held Stephen’s hand and spoke almost in a whisper, “Nothing will happen to me. You must trust me. I will never take such a decision if I am not confident to succeed.”

Maxwell was shaking his head all this time and he spoke with complete disagreement, “How can you trust Benedikt. He is a crook. This is a dangerous path, Sanchez. We will find some other means to get to your father.”

Sanchez knew she would never be able to convince them on her pronouncement. While she was trying to avoid being uncouth, it appeared it was the only way she could find her way out of this conundrum. She looked at Maxwell and announced in a dead voice, “I didn’t come here to take your advice. You can either support my decision or we go our own ways.”

There was complete silence. Stephen knew that if Sanchez had firmed her mind it would be impossible to alter it. His face showed frustration. With some deliberation, he spoke with a tremulous tone, “Give us something to believe that you will be safe. How can I let you go like this?”

Sanchez's heart tore into pieces, but she restricted any emotions to appear on her face. She took a pause, and spoke with a firm tone, "I am not a fool to put my life at risk. Besides, I will be in constant touch with you. Jules description of the map was accurate. Why don’t you guys follow me? That way you can always come to my rescue when needed.”

She saw some hope regaining back in their appearance, and went ahead with the momentum she had built, “I will leave the place the moment I have my dad and enough proof to bring down this organization."

She waited for some concurrences from them. There was a long pause before Maxwell spoke, “Can you tell us how you will be safe?”

This was the question Sanchez wanted to avoid. She never wanted to tell them that Benedikt was in love with her and he was doing everything out of love. “All I can tell you now is we have a business deal. I will be fine. I need to go now. You follow the map and we will be in touch."

She sounded uncomfortable as she concluded the conversation.

Maxwell made his final comment in haste, “You need to call us daily. If you fail to call us, I will come looking for you.”

“I will,” she blurted with a grim face.

She left the place walking with the help of her sticks. In a few moments, she disappeared behind the doors of the car waiting for her outside the coffee shop. And soon the car vanished in the busy serpentine streets of Estonia.

**PART V**

**Chapter 54**

**4:30 AM 21st April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia**

TransPacific's cargo ship was docked at Palijassaare Harbor. Unlike the other harbors in Tallinn, Palijassaare was a much quieter port. The moorage was meant only for cargo vessels and it had the least traffic, mostly unnoticed, as compared to the other harbors in Tallinn. Benedikt provided a perfect explanation about the choice of the port to Sanchez, "This harbor was selected by design. As we didn't want any undue attention." Palijassaare was in the northwestern part of the peninsula. The name itself meant ‘Barren Island’, which aptly fitted the description. Besides the beautiful scenic view, the place hardly offered any excitement.

Benedikt decided to board the vessel first thing in the morning and advised Sanchez to retire early.  Though she went to bed right after her early supper, she struggled to get any sleep. Throughout the night, she oscillated between spiraling apprehensions, surging hopes, and fretful uncertainties, mostly in anticipation of what the capricious tomorrow might behold. It was an anxious long wait, which finally came to an end when Benedikt knocked at her door announcing it was time.

The morning welcomed her with absolute quietude. The darkness was slowly receding, giving way to the morning glow. The casts of silver radiance encouraged by the pre-dawn sunrays created a mesmerized stature. And the subtle chilliness dissolved in the air with perfect discretion. The fresh morning brought an unperturbed exhilaration that expunged all her anxieties from the last night.  She sat in the car with endured fortitude, prepared to quest the impossible.

It took them thirty minutes to get to the harbor. They parked the car by the docking area. A porter helped them with their luggage, and they paced their way from the pier to the fast approaching ship's gangway. From the distance, she could see some handful of docking people and few ship residents busy in their routine work. Unlike what she thought, the area was sparsely populated.

Sanchez had never been on a ship before. She had always detested traveling on water and the concept of cruise ship had never excited her. Today was no different, but she was there for a purpose. The place appeared a little intimidating than what she thought before. And the morning excitement she was enthused with, slowly morphed into a subtle anxiousness. Her mind was crowded with many apprehensions. She hoped that her trepidations were transient and soon things would turn out fine.

As she walked towards the vessel, she got lost observing the large structure standing tall in front of her. She had never seen a ship from such close distance before. It was gigantic in size and almost overwhelming by any measure for her to comprehend. Unlike larger than life pictures of a cruise ship she had seen in advertisements, the vessel looked relatively dull. It was jet black in color with ‘DAZZLE” scribbled on the hull, which stood out from its otherwise gloomy appearance.

Benedikt was received at the end of the gangway by the captain of the ship. He was waiting in perfect attention along with his troupe.  It was hard not to ignore the clout Benedikt had. She noticed that there was more fear than respect for him. His presence brought a sense of uneasiness, and a hint of discomfort engulfed in the otherwise calm milieus. Benedikt instructed one of the shipmates to take Sanchez to her room. He had told her before that on the ship he would act like her boss and she shouldn’t be surprised by his change in behavior. As she was about to part he made a quick passing note, “I will see you in thirty minutes in my office. Alex will be at your cabin door. He will help you find me.”

She moved along the path led by a good-looking boy who appeared to be in his late teens. They looped around the aisle to a bank of elevators. The elevator took them to the upper deck. Her cabin was few yards away from the elevator towards the end of the shaft. Alex opened the door, put her suitcase on the luggage rack and politely stated, “madam, I will wait for you right outside the door."

The cabin was a spacious polygonal room and interiors were tastefully done, starkly different from the bland look of the vessel. There was a suede couch gracing the front part of the cabin. Away from the settee, on the opposite side was a study table. The king-sized bed was behind the sofa, covered with white linens and many golden color decorative pillows. The far end of the cabin was covered with tall glass doors and the curtains were folded halfway giving way for the morning sunlight to seep into the room. The room though had a contemporary appearance, it also sported a modern outlook confirmed by the liberal use of bold colors across the room.

Sanchez sauntered towards the glass doors. She nudged it gently and the transparent portal opened to the balcony, giving sight to a breathtaking view. She could see the sun rising beyond the edges of the ocean. The orange hues were not piercing but soothing to the eyes and the morning breeze was heavenly as it gently caressed her flesh. It was a blissful experience and she was so engrossed in the trice, for she forgot everything that was making her restless. Against her wish, she retraced her steps back to the cabin. Benedikt couldn’t have offered her a luxurious room, but she knew he gave her the next best possible option. The gorgeous view from her room was worth compromising with any luxury that a suite might have offered.

**\**

**Chapter 55**

**7:30 AM 21st April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia**

Sanchez took a quick shower and got ready for her first day at work. Besides the fact that she was Benedikt's assistant, she had no clue what she was required to do. The fact that she was on the ship was enthralling enough, and everything else was insignificant.  

Alex was standing in an attentive position outside the cabin door. Moment Sanchez stepped out he greeted her with a benign bow and led the way to Benedikt’s office. The place was a maze and she was glad Alex was there to guide the path. Benedikt’s office was located on the upper deck right next to the captain’s great-cabin.  The office was gigantic, almost spanning the width of the stern. Two marines guarded the entrance to the office. It seems they were aware of her arrival as they opened the door without imposing any questions.

Sanchez was startled by the charming appearance of the office. There was a large table in the center of the room, crowded with piles of plot papers and different mechanical and electronic instruments. The rear end of the cabin tapered forming an oval shape. Large French windows covered the posterior of the room opening to the balcony. The light hardwood floor was elegantly polished, evident from the nice bright shine it revealed. On the left side of the entrance, there was a roman style spacious curved couch, which could easily sit a group of ten. The walls were tastefully decorated with paintings from the medieval period.

She looked around the place with great interest and soon realized that amidst the entire beautiful decor Benedikt was nowhere to be seen. And then she found a door like structure camouflaging with the sidewall. She observed the structure with great interest and gently tapped to check if it was a for real.

“Yes” came the response.

She gave the door a gentle nudge and the outlet opened to an extended bedroom. Benedikt was not anticipating her arrival so early.

“Sorry, I wasn’t expecting you so early.” He was perplexed and spoke in haste. 

Sanchez avoided the words and instead responded with a crinkled pair of eyes.

“How is your leg now?” Benedikt faintly tried to take his attention away from her enticing veneer.

“I am lot better now, and I am glad I am off the sticks” her eyes still smiling.

“That’s wonderful to hear. Let us go through the schedule for the next few days. Don’t forget, you have to act and behave like my assistant” His smile was laced with a vulnerable grin.

Like always Benedikt looked aristocratic. He was wearing a checkered grey suit with a white shirt that was tightly hugging his well-toned body. The turquoise blue cufflinks were standing out in the yellow pale light of the cabin. The Charcoal colored tie was contrasting well with the white shirt, and the dark grey boots were shined to perfection. She was lost in his demeanor and failed to grasp his words.

“Do you have something on your mind?” queried Benedikt.

Sanchez blushed and mumbled in an embarrassed tone, “Sorry, I wasn’t paying attention. Did you say something?"

Benedikt was puzzled. He restated his point, only this time it was without the humor, “I want to make you aware of my schedule."

“Sure, let us go through it” she stated in haste and scurried to the center table, trying her best to avoid his gaze.

In the past, he was always in control of everything that happened around him. Life was simple, give orders and see them getting executed. But things were no longer unpretentious.  Since he had met her, his life was in complete disorder. Most of the time he remained unsure how to deal with her. She was capricious, temperamental and at times whimsical. He was never trained for such circumstances, and her weird outlook was making him go even more berserk.

He cleared his throat, pushed the laptop towards her, forced a smile and stated, “from now on you will manage my calendar. Tomorrow is a big day. I am meeting key delegates at 10:00 in the morning. It is their cargo we would be carrying and off-loading at various places throughout our voyage.

The next three days we would be loading the cargo. I have inspection meetings with the head of the stevedore twice a day. Also, every day I will meet the captain at 8:00 in the morning and in the evening. Before we leave, I am required to personally inspect the load. And I have an important dinner meeting tomorrow. All the arrangements are made. You can find everything in this folder. Make sure you go through the details."

He pushed the folder towards her direction and took a pause, giving her time to browse through the plan.

“I see my name in tomorrow’s dinner meeting” She had a puzzled look on her face as she announced her observation with utmost innocence.

“Can I not have dinner with you?” there was clear ingenuity in his gullible voice.

“From now on direct all your calls to me,” she answered, ignoring his question in a complete unconventional manner.

Unsure how to react to the quandary, he lowered his excitement and stated with a dull tone, “There will be a desk here tomorrow. You will occupy the seat for as long as you are on the ship.”

Sanchez pushed the laptop aside, folded her arms across her chest, looked squarely at him and enquired, “So how do I go about finding my father?”

He saw her transforming from a perky damsel to a girl exuding deep concerns. It was hard for him to comprehend how she could switch context so effortlessly. He was convinced he would never be able to read her mind.

He retraced himself from the debate he was silently going through and responded, “While I go for the rounds I will take you with me. Let us see if we can find any clues on the ship first. Do you have any picture of your father with you?”

Sanchez's eyes bellowed hope. She fiddled for some time in her purse and after a few fumbles pulled out her father’s picture, “Here, you can keep this with you."

Benedikt took the picture from her and gave it a cursory glance. And soon his face turned white, a sudden surge of fear encroached him and he felt as if the world was folding on him. He tried hard to control his emotions but failed. In haste, he looked away from her and stated, “I need to take an important call. Will be right back.”

A few minutes later he announced from his bedroom, “Let us reconvene in a couple of hours. The call is going longer than I expected.”

 

**Chapter 56**

**11:00 AM 21st April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia**

Sanchez came back to her room confused. The frightened look on Benedikt's face loomed on her mind, fixated, refusing to go away despite all efforts, making her delirious. More she got entwined in the labyrinth of her miscreant thoughts more she was convinced Benedikt knew something about her father. Lost in her own world, she sauntered towards the balcony and sat on the armchair staring at nothingness. The midmorning sun was fighting hard with its ether to warm the atmosphere with a distinct verve. It was a perfect setup, for the occasional sunrays seeping out of the vast spread clouds was gently warming the cold gelid airs. But despite such fervent weather, she struggled to come out of her gloomed self.

"Have I done a mistake trusting him? What if Maxwell was right?” She doubted every past action.

While she was fighting with her predicament, the phone alarm went off. The screen flashed a reminder note, “Call Stephen.”

It was almost top of the two hours of her forced break. She had a lot to get done before heading out, and calling her brother was least of her priority.

She checked the door and ensured the inside security latch was on. Looked around for any hidden cameras in the room but couldn’t locate any. Swiftly she moved her luggage bag to the bathroom, opened the locks and carefully put all the belongings on top of a towel laid on the floor. Once the bag was empty she located the hidden zip under the bottom surface and carefully removed the cloth covering the bag. There was a black horizontal pouch sitting stealthily between the handle.  She removed the pouch and placed its content on the powder table.  The content included a miniature camera and a video recorder in the form of earrings, a small knife, few extra memory cards, a voice recorder pen and a transmitter.

She checked the functioning of the camera and once tested to her satisfaction she wore them. The left earring was the camera and the right was the video recorder. A simple tap toggled the device between on and off mode. She put the voice recorder pen in her pocket, kept the rest of the items back in the pouch and repacked her bag.

It was Maxwell who helped her out with these sleek gadgets. She recalled her last meeting with them and the way they parted in an unceremonious way. Few minutes after she left, she got a call from Stephen to come back and see them. She remembered how at first, she blatantly refused but capitulated after Stephen explained the reason. They decided to meet at the same coffee shop where they convened before. Sanchez spotted them right at the entrance of the café. They took a seat away from the access door, more secluded from the usual hustle-bustle. Maxwell handed her a small bag, which could easily fit into her handbag. Before she could react, Maxwell opened the pouch and showed her the contents. In the beginning, Sanchez was puzzled but when Maxwell explained the functions she was impressed. Soon she parted, leaving behind their concerned faces, making her a tad doubtful for the first on her bearings and actions.

She looked at her watch and it was time for her to leave her cabin. She gave a final glance on her earrings, once convinced she stepped out of her room and locked the door firmly behind her.  Alex was waiting outside the cabin. While it was great to have him for the first time, she detested the idea of he hanging around all the time.

“Now I have a fair idea about the ship. You can resume your other responsibilities.” Her tone was uncanny and definitive.

Alex was puzzled for he had strict orders from his seniors to escort the lady.

“Madam, this is my only work? Did I offend you by any chance?”

The fretfulness in his voice was clearly visible. She knew there was no point talking to him and decided to take it up with Benedikt instead.

“Forget what I mentioned. Let us go.”

They started spiraling down the narrow path towards the aft of the ship. Throughout the walk, her mind was racing, analyzing every motive, every thought that scrupulously confined her mind. She wanted to confront him but wasn’t sure if that was a good idea. Her mind was clouded with several questions but sadly she had answers to none.

 

**Chapter 57**

**11:00 PM 21st April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia**

The day progressed without any other noticeable episode.  Her interaction with Benedikt was restricted to a few syllables, making her intuition about something not right grow stronger. The only achievement of the day was getting Alex off from guarding her.  Many a time she desired to confront him, inquire about his changed demeanor. But despite all the commotion she battled with, she couldn’t muster enough courage to accost him. It was an unknown hesitation, something she was unable to comprehend. And to her complete bewilderment, Benedikt was behaving as if nothing had happened. Besides limiting the conversation, he had returned to complete normalcy, hardly exhibiting any shade of discomfort.

Around 10:00 in the night Benedikt called it a day. Exhausted from the day commotion, she decided to retire.  She tossed and turned on the bed but failed miserably to get any sleep. Frustrated she decided to call Stephen with the hope of getting respite from her enduring conundrum.

Stephen picked the phone on the first ring and blurted hysterically, “What took you so long?”

Sanchez agitation got further escalated with her brother’s complaint. She was about to snap, but decided otherwise, for fighting with her brother was the last thing on her mind. “I am surrounded by people all the time. I cannot call at my leisure.” Her sarcasm still spilled despite her arduous attempt to stay calm.

Stephen anxiety receded. They spoke for about five minutes. Sanchez promised to call him again the next day and concluded the conversation. Sleep was still defying her. She spent some time checking the videos and the photos she captured during the day. The recordings and photos came out to her satisfaction. The initial lot was only for trials and had no significance. With nothing else to do, she forced herself to sleep again. This time she tried distracting her mind recalling the good old times. It seemed to work and soon she dozed off to sleep.

 

**Chapter 58**

**7:30 AM 22nd April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia**

It was a new day and a new beginning. A sense of determination embraced her being. Sleeping over her anxiety was all that was needed to get her mojo back. It was an important day for both of them. While Benedikt had an important meeting with the Russians, Sanchez was eyeing to get the meeting secretly recorded. She had also decided to confront Benedikt on the previous day episode, but that she planned to execute later in the evening.

She was by her desk sharp at 7:30 AM, 30 minutes before her reporting time. Benedikt’s meeting was scheduled not until 10:00 AM. He too was in the office checking some final reports. He wasn’t expecting Sanchez so early but was pleased to see her and couldn’t conceal the slunk smile on his face.

He was extremely secretive about the meeting. Sanchez had no access to the reports or the documents pertaining to the meeting. Her purpose of coming early was to find a way to be a part of it, and his presence in the office wasn’t helping her by any means. She was withdrawn from her brooding with the intruding voice of Benedikt, “You are quite early. If you wish you can come back in an hour.”

Sanchez wasn’t ready for that question. With some struggle, she regained her lost self and put forward a leading question, “Am I disturbing you?”

“Hell, no. You are welcome to stay.” He passed a gullible smile.

Sanchez took advantage of his softened outlook and continued, “Can I help you with anything?”

“Thanks. I am fine for now.” And he got busy looking through some papers.

Her attempt to get involved was meekly put down by him. She was at her wits to find a way out when his sharp voice jolted her.

“Can you check the main conference room once? Make sure everything is in order."

“Sure.”

She beamed with renewed hope and headed straight to the conference room. The guards watching​ the area was puzzled to see her. She explained the reason for her visit and requested them to open the room. The guards scanned her, and once satisfied, they let her in.

The room was a spacious hall. There was a rectangle shaped large table in the center of the room, which could easily seat twenty people. She called upon the technical executive and got the AV and video checked. The chief steward was called next to check the food arrangements. After she was satisfied with the layout and amenities she settled down to execute on her plan.

On the extreme left corner, there was a side table, ​holding the AV equipment. She placed the pen, which was a stealth video recorder, below the AV frame hinge, perfectly blending with the color of the table. As she was checking on the position of the pen, Benedikt’s voice startled her. He was standing right next to her. Her face turned pale as if she was caught red-handed.

“What is going on?”

His voice had a clear reflection of inquisitiveness.

With every passing moment, Sanchez was becoming more fidgety, as if she had fallen into an abyss. Before she could assimilate her thoughts, he continued further, “I have some work for you, are you done checking things here?”

Those words fell on her ears like blessings. Though she was relieved​, she realized​ her flustered state might put her back in danger.

“Everything is fine. I finished doing a thorough check.” She blurted in a hurried tone.

“Good. Let us head back to the office. I need some help,” and he left the room promptly.

Sanchez was drenched in sweat. Fortunately, the conference room light was dimmed, which worked to her advantage. She headed to the restroom, washed her face and went straight to the office. As she was sauntering her way back only one concern crowded her mind, how would she get access to the room before the meeting. In the entire hullabaloo, she forgot to​ turn the recorder on.

**Chapter 59**

**8:30 AM 22nd April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia**

Benedikt was furious. He couldn’t come to terms with his incredulous gasping when Sanchez flashed the photograph. Sure, it was shocking enough to find who her father was, but the fact that he exposed his vulnerable side to her made him delirious. For him, it remained an unpardonable offense and despite several attempts to deviate his mind from recalling the unfortunate proceedings, he failed miserably. Though deep down he was aware he had recovered from the situation, he wasn’t naïve to ignore the perfect storm brewing inside Sanchez’s mind. And he knew sooner or later it would explode on him. To arrest the situation from further deteriorating, he tried making few calls but got nothing concrete to help him frame his strategy towards tracing Sanchez’s father.

His meeting was in a few hours and he had many unfinished jobs to complete for making it a success. Knowing he couldn’t afford to muddle it, he forced his attention back to work. He was not too long into it when he saw Sanchez walking back into the office.\
He was tad impatient, and to avoid exposing the nervousness he blurted, “I need some help, can you help tallying few accounts for my meeting today?”

“Sure, no problem. I am good in accounting.” And she grabbed the file from his stretched hands.

She couldn’t believe getting her hands on the file she was eyeing for since morning. “Maybe he was inadvertently keeping it away from me”, she concluded as she settled on the desk to work on it.\
She opened the file and started flipping through it, and what she found left her agape. The information was irrefutably apposite. She knew right at that moment that the file could be used against TransPacific’s in the court of law and it could be an excellent source to bring the racket down. She had to get the file out of the room, but with Benedikt sitting next to her, it was almost impossible to execute any plan. She kept brooding for a while but failed to formulate any meaningful strategy. And when she was convinced nothing could be done, she pulled herself back from her spiraling thoughts to focus on her work.\
It was over twenty minutes she was into it when Benedikt decided to leave the room to attend a phone call. She was so captivated in her work that she failed to notice him stepping out. It took her another ten minutes to wrap-up the work, and when she finally took her attention away from the file, Benedikt was nowhere to be seen. For a moment she was disillusioned, but as the reality sunk in, a frisson of hope swept her being.

She took a few deep breaths to calm her stature. After she had her nerves under control, she gave a hurried gaze to the surroundings, to ensure no one was watching her. Once convinced, she took the camera out of her purse. The camera was a piece of art and was small enough to hide in her hand’s fist. She hastily placed it inside the file, and with extreme caution started taking pictures of the pages.

After she was done taking the last snapshot, she tried rearranging the pages, and in hastiness, the file slipped from her hand. She leaned forward to catch it midway, but to her horror, this time she lost the grip on the camera. Both the camera and the file came crashing to the wooden floor with a thumping sound.  There were scattered pages all around the room.

Hearing the noise, Benedikt stepped inside and saw papers all over the floor and he noticed Sanchez hastily trying to put them together.

Watching Benedikt entering the room, her feet turned cold and her mind went numb. All hell broke loose; she was flirting in the danger zone of getting caught with her miscreant. She recovered quickly from the situation, and before Benedikt could offer any help, she pushed the camera to a small heap of paper.\
Benedikt was still on the phone. He put the phone on hold and enquired, "Is everything ok?"

She tried hard to conceal her anxiety, and with a nervous outlook blurted, "I accidentally dropped the file. I am sorry."\
"Let me help you" and he bent down to collect the dispersed papers.\
It was happening too fast too soon. Benedikt was approaching the stack of papers where the camera was stashed in. Not sure what to do next, she swept other scattered papers towards the stack where the camera was hidden.

Benedikt collected his share, put it on top of Sanchez’s pile, and before she could react, he balanced the phone between his ear and shoulder and lifted the entire stack to place in on the table. As luck would have it, the camera slid through the pile and went rushing to the floor. Benedikt saw the device flying out. He stretched his hand to grab it from falling and in that act, the phone slipped from his shoulder grip and crashed on the floor.\
Benedikt’s entire attention got diverted towards the phone. Taking advantage of the situation, like a hawk stooping towards its prey she leaped towards the camera. She promptly lifted it and rushed it into her jeans pocket. Benedikt was too immersed in checking the functioning of the phone. Once he found it working, he stepped out of the room to continue with his conversation. Sanchez narrowly escaped from a disastrous mishap. She was relieved seeing Benedikt leaving the room.  Perspiration covered her from head to toe, and she knew she wouldn’t have survived if Benedikt had got hold of the camera.

After she regained her senses, she arranged the file neatly on the table, rechecked her work, and put her final report on the table for review. Her only hope was Benedikt would have forgotten about the strange looking device, and there would be no questions raised about its existence in the future.

 

**Chapter 60**

**9:45 AM 22nd April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia**

The delegate’s arrival time was fast approaching. Benedikt went through the file one final time. Once satisfied with the workings he stated with a deep voice, “I have to go down to receive the visitors. Would you mind keeping the documents in the conference room?”

Since the time, she left the conference room, her mind was plotting innumerable strategies to find a way to be in the room again. Never once she assumed it would come to her so easily. She looked away from him to conceal her excitement, grabbed the file and disappeared as fast as she could humanly do.

The conference room was a flight up the deck. Sanchez was pacing her steps with urgency. As she turned around the curb she saw the guards outside the room, standing in their usual vigilant posture. She pretended to ignore their presence and went past them to open the door.

“Sorry miss, no one can go in”, the guard voice was firm as one of them raised his hand to block her from going any further.

“Benedikt wants the files on the table for the meeting. May I?” she stated displaying genuine urgency.

“You stay here. I will accompany her inside.” The guard declared and opened the door.

The guard stood at the entrance of the meeting room keeping a close eye on her. She moved around, pretending as if she was checking the functioning of the gadgets. While she was next to the AV system, she swiftly pulled out the recorder pen hidden behind the frame hinge and turned the switch on. Satisfied with her work, she thanked the guard and left the room. The recorder could record for about a couple of hours. Her hope was to get enough evidence to give TransPacific's illegal trafficking a final halt.

They arrived in an entourage of stretched limousines. There were four executive cars with dark glasses and were chauffeured driven, infusing an aura that all meant serious business. A team of well-built escorts leaped out of the vehicles and surrounded the area quickly forming a human shielded lane.

The delegates, dressed in dark suits, came out of the cars and paced towards the gangway. Benedikt was standing at the entrance. He greeted each one of them personally and led them to the elevators, which would directly take them to the conference room.

The meeting lasted for an hour. While the meeting was in progress no one was allowed to enter the room, not even the chief steward, who was allowed to serve the guest after the meeting was concluded.

They left the ship quarter past one, with brisk steps and in complete discretion, like how they had arrived. Soon after, Benedikt appeared in the office with a broad grin, suggesting that the meeting went according to the plan.

“In one hour, we should go and check the stevedores. Also, if you could check the payment file once that would be a big help. I need to clear their primage. We would be sailing \`soon, and I want nothing to be left for tomorrow.”

Sanchez mind was working hard to find a way to be in the conference room again. She made a faint attempt, as she blurted in an uncomfortable voice, “I am not able to locate my notebook. Have you seen it in the conference room? Maybe I left it there by accident."

“I haven’t seen any. Why don’t you go and look for yourself,” his tone was overtly casual?

The trick worked. The guards were still there and gave Sanchez a confused look. She told them about her lost notebook. They were least bothered for the delegates were gone and opened the door without any hesitation. She leaped forward, grabbed the pen, switched the recorder off and left the meeting room promptly.**\**

**Chapter 61**

**3:00 PM 22nd April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia**

The visit to the loading dock was uneventful. Everything was happening as per the schedule. For Sanchez, it was frustrating, to say the least. There was no sign of her father's existence and she had no concrete plan to initiate any enquires with the people on the ship. Benedikt's suspicious behavior, her inability to quiz the workers and void of a no go-forward plan, she was like a boat without an anchor, drifting to oblivion.

It was around 6:00 PM when Benedikt announced it was time to head out for dinner. They had plenty of work to wrap-up before the voyage could commence, and the decision was made to conclude the unfinished work after dinner. The chauffeur was waiting right outside the gangway. It was a short fifteen minutes’ drive. The restaurant was in an upscale neighborhood, crowded by Lamborghinis and Maserati’s. The streets were pebbled, standing tall were decorative 18th-century streetlights and the atmosphere had a distinct aura of richness, well demonstrated by the affluent people who graced the streets with a flair of luxury.

The place was an authentic Lebanese restaurant with an ambiance resonating from the medieval times. The place was designed like an old castle with statues demonstrating sword fighting and Jousting. Benedikt, like always, took control of the menu and ordered the drinks and the food. With great interest, he explained about the place and the delicacies it offered, but it all fell on the deaf ears of Sanchez. Her mind was searching for the right moment or the right words to heave away her burden.

It didn't take him long to realize she wasn’t with him. The reason for her indifferent posture was not unknown to him, and he was innately hoping that his presumptions were wrong. While he was tempted to inquire about her pensive outlook he dreaded, as he wasn’t sure where it might lead to.

Sanchez decided to break the monotony. She hardly minced her words as she spoke, “Did you know my dad?”

Benedikt was taken off-guard. And after a long uncomfortable pause he responded, “No, why did you ask?”

“Your face turned pale when I showed you his picture. Listen; even if it is horrible, please tell me. You know I am in this never-ending hunt for years now. Please help me.”  She made a pleading outcry and her voice trailed with a clear shade of dejection.

For a moment, he was lost. He had no clue how to react to the situation. The last thing he wanted was to upset her. She had too much importance in his life and it was hard for him to see her in such a miserable state.

“I never knew your father, but I have seen him once” the words came out invariably and it was never in his plan to reveal any details.

He took a pause to see how Sanchez was taking it. She had anxiety in her eyes and restlessness in her appearance. He knew any more silence would drive her to insanity. Hurriedly he continued further, “It wasn’t in my span of controls. He was caught in Belarus and I happened to be there on a regular business trip. That’s all I know.”

Benedikt couldn’t face Sanchez and in an attempt to avoid her constant gaze, he looked away from her. Sanchez was barely holding herself.

“Is he alive?” Her voice trembled with fear.

“We are in an illicit trade, but we are not murderers. Not that I want to give you any false hope, but I firmly believe he is alive.” His voice reflected uncertainty.

Sanchez wasn’t convinced but she knew her options were limited. She stopped quizzing Benedikt. She had lost her appetite. After some uncomfortable silence and a quiet dinner, they headed back to the ship.

Benedikt knew he had temporarily weathered the storm. What he mentioned was partly true and he feared to faceoff on this topic again. He advised Sanchez to take rest, but she refused. They worked until midnight and wrapped up the left-over work.

Sanchez had only one thing on her mind, to take charge of the situation. She had a rough sketch of the plan in her head and waited until the next morning to execute on it.

 

**Chapter 62 **

**4:00 AM 23rd April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia**

Sanchez couldn’t sleep well. She spent most of her time replaying the plan on her mind. The more she weighed the options, more she unraveled flaws, making her restless and exasperated. Perturbed, she somehow managed to find some sleep. 

The ship's loud whistle woke Sanchez from her brief repose. The vessel flagged off from Tallinn, making its onward day-long journey to Ventspils. Sanchez slipped out of the bed, splashed some water on her face, dressed casually and moved with a rapid pace towards the loading station.

It was a quiet morning. The loading dock was missing the hullabaloo that existed for the past few days. She approached the entrance with discrete steps.  Two mariners laced with sophisticated weapons guarded the place. They were curious to see a lady at such ungodly hour. Though inquisitive they weren’t alarmed, for they had seen her frequenting the area with Benedikt before. “I am here to see the head stevedore for an important matter.” She took her chances about his presence.

“Sure, he is by his desk” came the reply, and she was let inside the cargo area.

She knew this was the easy part. The hard part was to convince Finch, the head stevedore, to speak.

Finch looked tired. The commotion from the last few days had worn him down. He was going through some file when the gentle tap on the door interrupted him.  He looked at her with a galling stare. He sure recognized her but expressed a pretentious look, unsure of the purpose of her visit.

Sanchez broke the brief prelude and started gently, “would you have few minutes for me?”

"What is it about?” His voice reflected clear hesitation.

Sanchez stepped inside his office, pulled her father’s picture from the file she was holding and asked, “Do you know this person?”

She looked straight through him and waited for his response. Finch briefly looked at the picture and immediately took his eyes off it.  "Please leave?” he announced with a flat tone.

Sanchez wasn’t surprised. She was expecting a response something in the same lines.

“I mean no harm, and whatever you say will be off the records. Please, any bit of information would be a big help.” She maintained her composure without losing her calm.

By now Finch had lost his patience. He was almost screaming. He threatened her with dire consequences if she refused to leave.

Sanchez had no option, but to leave his office. There was a strong possibility of Finch complaining to Benedikt, but that was least of her worry.

While she made no progress, one thing was certain, Finch knew her father. It was still dark and except for the sailors, the entire ship was in deep slumber. Sanchez’s tiredness from the last night had surprisingly vanished. She turned her computer on and carefully downloaded all the images, videos and pictures from her gadgets. She was curious to learn what transpired in the meeting room. She put on her earpiece and played the audio recording from the conference room. They were mostly discussing the loads, money and some future deals. She fast-forwarded the audio to hear anything that could lead her to her father but found nothing. Except for being a good evidence to trap the rogues the audio offered nothing significant to get to her father.

Frustrated she decided to retire for the night. She had to rest well to be ready for Latvia.**\**

**Chapter 63**

**10:00 AM 23rd April 2014, Ventspils, Latvia**

Benedikt’s aggravation was at the extreme. Ever since Sanchez had boarded the ship it was nothing but a series of misdemeanors. It was never part of his plan to flair her antsy emotions, and more he tried mending the situation, the worse it became. He assumed staying close to Sanchez would help her know him better. But to his dismay, everything was working against him. His yearning for her was growing by the day and his inability to accost her was breaking him apart. He was convinced that the only way he could restore the lost glare was by finding her father, but it wasn't an easy task. A small inquiry could alarm the executives in TransPacific, which would do more harm than good. He had to execute his investigation with utmost secrecy, and he still hadn’t figured his way forward. There was no one in the circuit he could trust to further his probe and all his options appeared weak and flawed.  And Above all the fear of Sanchez coming to know what he knew about her father was unsettling him the most.

As he dwelled in this predicament, the screaming siren broke his subtle brooding. He picked his hard hat and rushed outside to find the reason behind the alarm. He saw a swarming crowd rushing towards the abaft. Upon enquiring he learned one of the workers got hurt. Once confirmed that it wasn’t any danger to the ship he retracted his steps back to his cabin.

There was a commotion near the ship's stern. A doctor was attending to an injured worker who lay unconscious on a wooden shaft. From a distance, Sanchez could see the person was bleeding profusely. She was baffled to see the packed crowd suffocating the already distressed worker. She maneuvered her way by pushing people aside, to the open shaft where the doctor was attending him. Once she managed to come close to the spot, she turned around and screamed at the top of her lungs, “Guys, you need to let the doctor do his job. Give some space please.”

That announcement worked, and people started dispersing. There were five of them left, the doctor, the injured worker, Finch, Sanchez, and the second officer. The worker appeared to be in a bad shape. His left limb looked almost smashed. No one knew how it happened, but it appeared one of the loads accidentally hit him. The doctor ordered Finch to get him to the onboard dispensary. He went to action, and with the help of his fellowmen, the injured worker was carried on a stretcher to the doctor’s office.

As they were walking, the second officer broke the silence, "How serious it is?”

"It looks bad. If we don’t get him to a hospital, he might lose his leg. Can we turn the ship back to the port? The most I can hold him is a couple of hours,” The doctor stated with a hopeful plea.

The officer was puzzled. “I am not sure. This is a captain’s call.” And he scurried away to check with the captain.

Sanchez knew the answer wouldn’t be favorable. The dates were fixed and there was an army of people aligned for the delivery of the goods. Any change in date would be a mammoth undertaking, which she was sure the captain wouldn’t do for a worker. Finch looked concerned, for his views were not different from her.

The second officer came back in a few minutes, and sure he had disappointing news, the ship couldn’t be turned around. It was evident that the chances of his limb restoration were next to none. Finch was frustrated, so was the doctor.

Sanchez left the dispensary with a purpose and barged into Benedikt's cabin without any warning. He was on the phone. He glanced at her and by her looks, he knew something was serious.

“You need to turn around the ship. Otherwise, the worker will die”, blurted Sanchez.

By now Benedikt was aware of the situation. “Few things are beyond my control. I am sorry for him, but I cannot turn the ship.” He said with a flat voice.

“Listen, if that worker dies I will never forgive you” Sanchez was hysterical, and she stormed out of the room without waiting for his response.

The heavy sedation had put the worker temporarily in a numb state. Though the flow of blood had receded, he was still in deep danger. "He has lost a lot of gore. I need few volunteers to donate blood.” The doctor announced.

"Can he survive until evening?” Latvia was still six hours away and Finch looked worried.

“I doubt. He needs urgent medical attention”

A deep-throated voice rattled their conversation. It was Benedikt, who appeared from nowhere. “I have arranged for a medical helicopter. It should be here any moment. Take him to the top deck”, he announced.

Everyone in the room was pleasantly surprised. The doctor took the lead and thanked Benedikt for being considerate, followed by Finch who genuinely looked moved by the unexpected gesture shown by him.

“Don’t thank me. Thank the lady. She made this happen.” He left the place as quietly as he arrived.

Finch regretted about the way he had behaved with her earlier. He wished he could have been a little considerate with her before. He thanked her in a sheepish manner and turned to talk to the doctor to avoid any further conversation, mostly out of embarrassment.

The worker flew away, and people went back to their work. Sanchez had few unfinished jobs to conclude and lost in her world she sauntered towards Benedikt’s office.

 

 

**Chapter 64**

**10:00 AM 23rd April 2014, Ventspils, Latvia**

The fact that Sanchez was on her own, the danger that might be lurking on her every moment and the undeniable truth that Benedikt was with her all the time were making Maxwell extremely uncomfortable. It had been three days since she boarded the ship and she had called Stephen only once. Although she confirmed about her safety, he wasn’t convinced. Sanchez had put her trust on Benedikt, and he knew Benedikt wasn’t reliable.

He was going through the map details circling Gdynia repeatedly. “From here they should travel on road”, he spoke to himself. And after brooding for long he was clear what he wanted to do. Stephen was in the kitchen fixing lunch. He raised his voice so that Stephen could hear him. “We need to leave this place right now.”

Stephen came to the room wearing a confused look, “Where are we heading? Didn’t we decide to stay in Latvia until they arrive?”

“We need to be in Gdynia. That’s where the voyage ends. We don’t know where in Warsaw they will be. And the only way to find it out is by following them. I want to be there before their arrival to plan everything. And chasing cars is not easy. Trust me on that experience."

They packed their belongings in haste, left the half-cooked lunch and soon they were on their way to Gdynia.

**Chapter 65**

**11:30 AM 23rd April 2014, Ventspils, Latvia**

The door to Benedikt’s suite was left wide open. Sanchez appeared exhausted from the morning ordeal, and though she detested the idea to meet him, she somehow dragged herself into his executive cabin. There was not a soul around, and the quietness was giving the surroundings an eerie stupor.   

She was hesitant at first but soon decided to knock the bedroom door. She tapped a few times, but there was no response. Disillusioned, she came back to her desk and got busy going through some random files. She was in some deep retrospections when she heard the distinct guttural voice of Benedikt. He was standing right in front of her looking at her with great interest.  

“I truly appreciate what you did. Thank you.” she took a brief pause and continued further, “And I am sorry for the way I behaved” she took her eyes away from him half embarrassed and half nervous.  And from the corner of her eyes, she saw him smile, making her overtly uncomfortable. How she wished to escape from the place but couldn't bolt away from her labyrinth of thoughts. 

“You know, you are changing me as a person. I hardly remember when I did any good in the past. I don’t know if it is a change for good or for worse, but I want to thank you for discovering a new me."

He knew Sanchez was not comfortable talking about him, and seeing her anxious he changed the topic, “We have few hours to Latvia. Why don’t you take some rest?"

Sanchez was looking for a way out of the conversation and was relieved to find her escape path. She was battling competing thoughts, imposing questions she had no answers to, making her worn out and jaded. And with a lost soul and a confused mind, she walked back to her cabin.

 

**Chapter 66**

**8:00 PM 23rd April 2014, Ventspils, Latvia**

It was late in the evening when the captain announced it would take an hour for the ship to dock at Ventspils.  Sanchez got off the armchair and pushed herself to come out of her futile brooding. Before heading out she had one job to conclude. She confirmed the door latches were on and the path to the balcony was guarded. Once assured, she connected her satellite phone to the laptop and tried using it as an Internet gateway.  After few attempts, she succeeded getting on the Internet. She uploaded all the videos and pictures from her camera to her private account. The speed was painfully slow, and it took her longer than she expected. She checked the sanctity of the uploaded files, and once convinced, she deleted the files permanently from her camera. She threw herself on the bed and a sense of quiescence reclaimed her.  For the first time in a while, she felt liberated.  A sharp knock on the door rattled her from her reminiscence. She hastily enquired, “Who is it?”

“This is Alex. Chief wants to see you”

“Tell him I will be there in 5 minutes”.

Benedikt was busy going through some reports. He had few questions about some documents, which she answered and after that, she had nothing else to do. While Benedikt was busy with his work and calls, Sanchez kept her mind occupied rehearsing the plan she wanted to execute in Latvia. Benedikt was occupied for some time. Once done with his work he glanced towards Sanchez and found her scribbling arbitrarily with great focus. While he kept looking at her with interest, she hardly noticed him and kept engaged with her rumination.

He retrospect on the past few days proceedings and concluded how unfair he was all this while. He had promised her to help find her father, but till date, he hadn’t told her what he was going to do about it. Besides, he had confused her more with his perilous demeanor, particularly, his unwarranted reaction on seeing her father’s picture. To add to her misery, he had made her work relentlessly, which she was never responsible for. And never once she complained.

He came out of his brooding, and with a soft tone stated, “Sanchez, go and take some rest. Let us resume tomorrow morning. Hopefully, Latvia can provide us some leads to trace your father.”

She was still busy scribbling, paying no attention to his words. After a long uncomfortable pause, he meekly continued, “I, unfortunately, cannot go around enquiring about your father. It can put everyone in danger." More than him it was his guilt talking.

Sanchez was still drawing some random pictures and without taking her focus away from the notepad, she said, “By everyone you meant you” she gave him a hard look, “Isn’t that correct?”

Benedikt ego was ruffled. His face turned pale. Though he somehow managed to control his anger, he couldn’t refrain from blurting a subtle sarcasm, “I hope you are intelligent enough to know that if by any stroke of luck your father is still alive, any snooping around can put his life in deep danger.”

He knew Sanchez must be wild by now, but he couldn’t control himself from not stating the facts. But Sanchez chose to remain silent. And soon she excused herself and stormed out of the room.

As she walked away, teardrops started rolling off her cheek. She paced out of the room as fast as she could, for she didn’t want to show her gullible side. The last thing she wanted was his sympathy.

Every single time Benedikt tried to correct things, he failed. His uncouth approach and intense anger were making Sanchez slip further away from him. He rushed out to the deck to find some relief. The gelid airs were acting like a natural healer, and it instantly calmed his perturbations. He started enjoying the dark night, the cold air and the aloofness. Despite all the recent proceedings a surreptitious smile encountered him. The clouded apprehensions faded away at that moment. He realized the barriers he experienced all the while was nothing, but an illusion created by his miscreant heart. He now had a purpose. For once he was certain and he knew what he had to do to mend things.

 

**Chapter 67**

**8:00 AM 24th April 2014, Ventspils, Latvia**

Sitting alone in her secluded cabin, aloof, lost in her world, she gave an emphatic attempt to decipher the state of her mind. It was a strange emotion, a subdued pain that kept resurfacing without any visible explanation. She had never felt so vulnerable before. A lot had to be accomplished in Latvia, and she neither had time to be miserable nor had any good reason to comprehend her deplorable being.  She forced her mind to focus on the day’s proceedings, and as the art of possibilities crowded her thoughts, her despondent self slowly faded away. With the hope of a better tomorrow, she drifted off to sleep.

It was a loud cacophony. A blend of innumerable voices all trying hard to explain something with urgency. She was trying hard to focus but was failing miserably. Then she saw them appearing one by one morphing their way into her cabin through the open door. Some were aghast, and the others were too eager to cast their hands on her. The voices were becoming unbearable. With all her might she tried to escape, but her body refused to follow her mind, and she helplessly saw those ghost-like creatures sauntering towards her with unprecedented angst.

The demeanor of one of them slowly morphed into a distinct structure, and out of the shallow darkness, she could see the hazy shadow of Benedikt. She craned her neck to get a good sight but failed haplessly. Benedikt’s hand was slowly extending towards her. She gave one final push, managed to find her lost voice. She screamed and jumped out of her constrained state, perspiring profusely from head to toe.  The room was pitch dark and she couldn't see any of those disturbing souls anymore.  The voices also had surprisingly receded.

It took her few minutes to adjust to the darkness and to ascertain that she had a bad dream. She turned the lights on and checked the time. It was 8:00 AM. A sense of relief regained, and a wry smile crossed her lips.

"I have to stop overthinking,” she made a promise to herself and put her mind to replay the plan she had for the day.

Going to the warehouse with Benedikt was the first thing on her list. Her hope was to find someone who might have seen her father, someone who may be his friend. She knew her plan was vague, but she couldn't figure out any better alternative. Benedikt could have been a great help, but after the last evening episode, she was averse from taking any help from him.

“Why my heart breaks when he talks harshly? Why am I bothered when he misbehaves?” she tried reasoning but couldn’t attribute to any convulsive answer.

“Am I am avoiding the truth? Am I in love?” those competing views rattled her in entirety, and she knew no matter how vehemently she refused to accept it, she was in love!

“What have I done?” she mumbled. She paced to the bathroom, splashed some cold water on her face, stared at the mirror and spoke to herself in a quiet, but firm voice, “You are here for a mission. You don’t have time to fall in love. Find your dad and get the hell out of this place.”

With renewed energy and determination, she headed back to the office.

Benedikt was on his desk, busy going through some files. He was charming as ever, wearing his pixie smile. The rugged look, the unkempt hair and the infectious smile made all her fortitude slipped into oblivion. Before she could recover, he announced in a husky tone, “I am heading to the warehouse would you like to join me?”

She was spellbound. It appeared he had read her mind. All she could manage was continue staring at him with awe.

“I know warehouse is no fun but here is what I can do to make it interesting. Right after our visit, we can check out the beautiful Market Square.”

Her mind was running in many directions and she failed to offer a response for a while. Little embarrassed she sheepishly spoke, “Sure. When do we leave?”

“Well, the undocking will take another few hours. Around 11:00?”

“Sure, that works” she appeared much relieved for she found a way out of her mortified brooding.

“So, I will see you at 11:00 by the gangway."

She reciprocated with a small smile and headed out to her cabin more confused than she was before. 

**Chapter 68**

**10:30 AM 24th April 2014, Ventspils, Latvia**

The dark clouds were looming over the rough sea made the port city look exquisite, like some artwork from Guggenheim. A strong gust of wind was moving at a fast pace, appearing unruly, for it dared to take everything away with it. Sanchez was by the gangway, way before time and she hardly regretted her decision. Ventspils port was breathtakingly charming. Despite the large concrete structure that draped the port, it had a distinct aura of its own, and there was something in it that reminded her of Ayr.

“Madam I need to have a word with you”, the disembodied voice almost made her jump out of scare. She turned around and found Finch standing by the edge of the hatch right where the gangway ended. He looked edgy and when he got Sanchez’s attention he turned and started pacing towards the stern of the ship, far away from the crowd.
