Sanchez was confused but decided to follow him anyways. She tried keeping some distance to ensure no one notices them. After a few minutes of walk, he slipped inside a hidden rail by the end of the raft. Sanchez followed the course and soon she was standing across him.

In a low hushed tone, Finch stated, “I know him. He used to work on this ship. One day he and a friend of mine, Albert Avilov moved to Viciebsk. They were good friends. I am not in touch with Albert for a long a time. And he is the only person who can help you find Daniel.”

This was the first substantial lead she got hold of after a long period of time. Before she could react, Finch continued with the same urgent hushed tone, “Take this letter. Tell him it’s from me. He won’t refuse to help.”

Sanchez was all teary as she thanked him. He smiled and mentioned the most genuine words he could ravel, “You are a good person. Take care of yourself.” And he left.

It would be another four days before they would arrive at Viciebsk and she knew these four days would be excruciatingly long.

**Chapter 69**

**11:00 AM 25th April 2014, Ventspils, Latvia**

Sanchez's stay at Ventspils was laced with frustration. The annoyance of what future would unfold kept her in an ambivalent disposition all the time. She was engulfed in an uncalled frisson of fear, something she knew she could only bring to rest after meeting Albert.

While she was at the warehouse, she managed to get some valuable evidence, but nothing significant enough that could help provide any substantial lead to find her father.

Benedikt tried hard to lift her spirits but failed. They visited the beautiful market square, ate at an upscale restaurant, and he took every opportunity to humor her, but nothing worked.  The only place where he found her engaged was when they were in the Baltica Concert. However, it was short lived. She returned to her despondent state moment they stepped out of the hall. He tried many times to talk through her anxiety, but without any noticeable success.

He had so far failed to get any information about her father. The trusted sources he called had no clue, and rightfully so, for Daniel disappearance was an extreme hushed affair. He knew a call to the higher ups would give him all the answers, but he was unable to find a good reason to convince them on the probing. He knew once they doubt his intention he would be scrutinized and that might expose Sanchez, which he was not ready to compromise at any cost. The only way he could take the chance of calling his higher-ups was when he would be convinced about her well-being, and her safety could be only confirmed when she would leave the ship. Convincing her was the only resort he was left with. Though he knew it would be a tough conversation, he decided to confront her one last time.

It was the day before their scheduled departure to Gdynia. They were sitting in the office taking a final look at the work when Benedikt announced a short break. Sanchez was about to leave the office when Benedikt requested her to accompany him for a stroll on the deck. With some hesitation, she naively accepted to come along.

It was a gorgeous evening. The gush of wind made the temperature plummet, but not enough to make it unpleasant. They kept walking for a while without uttering any word. After a few minutes of uneasy silence, Benedikt blurted in his most unconventional way, “I have a request to make.”

“What is it about?”

Sanchez appeared lost, sensing something untoward.

“You need to first agree that you will give me a good listening and react rationally not emotionally”

Sanchez was anxious to know what Benedikt had to say and with an inquisitive mind she continued, “Sure, I’m listening.”

“I have been contemplating for the past few days about how to find your father. Yesterday, I spoke to some of my trusted contacts, but no one knows anything about him. After a lot of deliberations and considerations, I am left with only one option, calling the top executives.”

Sanchez was listening with great interest, and she waited for him to make his point.

“They are dangerous people. If I go and inquire anything about Daniel they will come after me. They will scrutinize me. Eventually, they will come to know about you. And once they find about your real identity, you won’t be spared.”

She didn’t react and patiently waited for him to conclude.

Seeing her captivated in the conversation he continued further, “I want you to leave the ship. I care a lot about you and knowingly I cannot put you in any danger. I promise I will do everything required to find your father. Trust me, I won’t fail you.”

“You are hiding something from me. Tell me the truth. What do you know about my father?” Sanchez struggled to calm her senses and it was evident in her fidgety deportment.

“Trust me I don’t know anything about him beyond what I have told you before.”  His words were laced with uncertainties.  And he struggled to speak with affirmation.

Sanchez couldn’t hold her stoicism anymore and spoke with a raised voice, “How do you expect me to trust you. You have told me before, you don’t know my dad then how do you know his name? I am wrong assuming we have a connection, a bonding that is built on mutual trust and respect. My father means everything to me and by hiding things you are breaking that special thing that exists between us."

Benedikt wasn’t sure how to react. His voice was fumbling, as he continued, “You know you are special to me. The last few days I have done things that I have never done in my entire lifetime. Trust me I don’t know where your father is. I don’t want trouble for you, that is the only reason why I am requesting you to leave”

Sanchez was aware that he was hiding something about her father, but she also knew that he genuinely wanted to help her. She couldn’t leave the ship until she meets Albert. She was in a quandary, for she didn’t want him to know what transpired between her and Finch.

“If you truly respect me then let me stay. I want to personally see every location. Let me stay until the end of this trip. If I still fail to find him, I will do as you say.” She demonstrated clear hesitation as she uttered those words.

Benedikt was unsure how to react to her request. After a long pause, he continued, “Alright, if this is what you want. I will not insist you to leave. But I wouldn’t go and actively look for your father until I am sure you are safe.”

Sanchez saw how dejected he looked. She was struggled hard to comprehend why she becomes bereft whenever it concerns Benedikt? Particularly when she was aware he was hiding something he knew about her father.

How she wished she had a preview of what might be transpiring on his mind, and with great reluctance, she left for her cabin.

**Chapter 70**

**11:00 AM 26th April 2014, Gdynia, Poland**

The port at Gdynia was one busy place. Maxwell had an old connect who worked in the local port authority. He took his help to get the arrival information of Benedikt's ship. With no concrete plan in hand, waiting for the cargo was the only viable option left for them. They chose a small coffee joint right outside the harbor to kill time. 

Their last conversation with Sanchez barely gave them any insight into her plans.  They were clueless about what she was up to and had no idea how she was planning to get any information about her father from Benedikt. Maxwell was unable to comprehend Benedikt’s motive. He was unclear what kind of business deal she could have with him. He was struggling to find any riposte to pacify his intrusive mind. Every time he pondered, he was left with resentment and discomfort. He had a faint inkling that Benedikt might be in love with Sanchez. Something he tried not to deliberate on, but it kept resurfacing. The more he contemplated disposing of the idea, more forcefully it revisited.

The phone rang with a loud noise, rattling the contemplations he was battling with for the past few hours. He looked at the phone and saw his friend's name on the display. It was time for action. The ship was about to arrive.

**Chapter 71**

**3:00 PM 26th April 2014, Gdynia, Poland**

During her stay at Gdynia Sanchez discovered a disturbing fact about the goods the ship was carrying. They were trafficking items of archeological importance. She learned about it by accident during one of the inspection tours she took along with Benedikt. The wrapper of one of the boxes was ripped, and gold-plated statutes, of some historical significance, were dangling out. Using her stealth body camera, she took some good number of pictures. Later when she checked the captures in private, she found the resemblance of the images to the residuals of second century BC temple from Vietnam. Something she had learned about during her school days in Ayr. It was a substantial finding, and she was certain this along with the other evidence she had gathered could easily help to nail down TransPacific. She carefully stored the pictures in her online account and cleared every scathe of its existence from the camera.

The unloading of cargo got concluded late in the evening, and they were on schedule. Next morning, they were leaving for Warsaw. “Two more days before I arrive in Viciebsk”, she mumbled as she lay on the bed restless, staring at infinity. An unknown fear was looming her mind. She tried hard to distract her mind, but it kept revisiting her, making her restive. She kept tossing and turning for hours before succeeding in getting some respite from her overthinking mind.

The morning shimmering hue found its way through the open edges of the window curtain and filled the room with its glare. She woke up a tad crapulent, influenced by her night long brooding. The cold shower helped her to rejuvenate and be ready to brace the day. The drive to Warsaw was smooth. The traffic was light, and they took less than the anticipated time to reach the hotel. Benedikt had few meetings organized for the morning. Taking that pretext, Sanchez excused herself to spend some time visiting the downtown.

The weather was beautiful, and the temperature was soothing, making her stroll enjoyable. She traversed the cobble-webbed streets in no defined order, experiencing the architectural brilliance the city offered. From the sprawled gothic churches and the neoclassical palaces to the modern skyscrapers, the town reflected the time-tested turbulent history and its resurrection. The pewter evening sky was casting long shadows on the town's façade. It was time to head back to the hotel. She realized she had not called Stephen for a while. A lot happened since she had boarded the ship and she had invariably missed calling him. Sometimes by error, but mostly because of her emotional worn out state. Now that she was alone, she concluded it was the best time to call him.

Stephen like always sounded anxious. It was over three days she was on that ship and they were looking forward to meeting her in Warsaw. When Sanchez announced her presence in the old town, at first Stephen was thrilled but then signs of concerns dwarfed his excitement. He was near the hotel all the while with his eyes at the hotel entrance. He was unsure how Sanchez could go pass their vigilant watch.  He was baffled and concerned. And promised himself to be extra prudent from next time onwards.

Sanchez agreed to meet them at a nearby coffee shop and raised her hand to stop an approaching taxi.

The wait was not long. The fact that his sister was walking without any visible limp, made Stephen beam. While they wanted to spend the evening with her, Sanchez refused. She was away for a while, and she didn’t want Benedikt to come looking for her. After the usual prattle and giving reassurance on her safety she came straight to the point.

“I have enough evidence to pull the racket down. I have them all in my private online account. Before I hand it over to you, I need one assurance” Sanchez looked squarely at Maxwell waiting for his response.

“What is it?”

“You won’t do anything until I advise you to do so. I don’t want to jeopardize my father’s hunt.”

“Have you found any leads?” Stephen curiously posed the question.

“Nothing concrete, but I am on it”, her unwillingness to answer the question was visible in her asphyxiated voice.

“Are you hiding something from us?” this time it was Maxwell who put forward the question looking edgy and uncomfortable.

“That isn’t the answer I am looking for. Do I have your word?” the irritation in her voice gave a clear sign of how much she detested the probing.

“You have my word. Nothing I will do against your wish. If you can share with me what is going on, I may offer some help,” Maxwell explained with a somewhat troubled tone.

"Here are the account details. Please don’t forget the agreement we have. I want to bring TransPacific down, but not at the cost of my father.” She kept the conversation short.

Maxwell reflected uneasiness, but he was constrained. They parted ways. He knew something was brewing in Sanchez’s mind, and this time he was determined to deal with the situation like a professional, ditching his emotional grouse mind.

**\**

**Chapter 72**

**8:00 AM 27th April 2014, Gdynia, Poland**

It was pouring in Gydnia. The relentless rain and the gloomy weather were not helping Benedikt to drive away his unwarranted restlessness. A sense of dejection engulfed him, and no matter how much he tried escaping, he failed. He could sense Sanchez drifting away from him but was unsure how to evade it. The stay at Warsaw was busy.  He couldn't find time to have any real conversation with her. Most of the time he was either in some business meetings or was engaged in some site visits. And the little time he had she always appeared aloof lost in her own world, impeding any dialogue between them. After a lot of deliberations, he convinced his deviant mind to confront her. This time he was resolute to express his feelings. The yearnings, which got subdued in the past for reasons beyond his control, he wanted those emotions to flow uninterrupted exuding some purpose. The trip to Belarus was a four-hour journey by plane, and he wanted to use the travel time to accost her. He had a plan, and with a renewed hope he waited for the morning to arrive.

Benedikt was awake before the break of the dawn.  A morning jog and a long shower worked towards calming his anxieties. Sanchez was waiting for him in the breakfast area. She couldn’t take her eyes off him. He was wearing a faded denim trouser, an off-white shirt, and an ultramarine slip-on. His back-brushed long hair and the 5’0 Clock green shadow was driving her insane. It was a quiet breakfast and soon they headed to the airport.

The plane took off without much ado. They were halfway through the journey when Benedikt mustered some courage and handed her a paper. Before Sanchez could react, he turned and moved to the back of the plane, leaving her all by herself.

Sanchez opened the folded paper and read the content with interest.

“You know it's true that I never wanted to disappoint you, but despite my sincere efforts, I failed miserably.

Imagine a day when you and I are together, and we enjoy the moment of our camaraderie. There will be no baggage, no commitment to honor and no strings that will behold us. It will be beautiful, two people enjoying each other’s company, intellect and things that make the friendship beautiful and real.

I want to act on it today. For there is no tomorrow, and life is so unpredictable.  I want to take you to a far place where we can sit holding each other's hand, treasuring the unspoken words.

Everyone can find a reason or two to abolish this viewpoint or you may come and ask me why now, it isn’t the right time. But I will have only one reason, enough for me to disregard all rationale and the reason is - I love you. This one truth is strong enough to fight any amount of reasoning and questioning.

I want to meet you now beyond the boundaries of right and wrong, at a place where there are no obligations, no constraints, and none to justify. Where you can be you, speaking your mind freely without inhabitation, or any concern for repercussions.

Believe in my words. Let us give each other our hand and let the destiny lead us where it wants us to take. I assure you it will be the best thing you will ever experience. It will not be transient but for eternity, which we will keep in our own world away from anyone's prejudice or judgment.”

As Sanchez read the message, her heart wrenched for him and her emotions overwhelmed her. She struggled to contain her feelings. Few deep breaths helped to ease her surged sentiments and once she had her emotions in control, she approached the rear of the plane. Benedikt was sitting on a couch, randomly flipping through a magazine. As he sensed her presence, he passed a casual, but attentive gaze. There were anticipation, curiosity, and love in his blue eyes. He draped his surging heartbeats with a faint smile and waited for Sanchez to speak.

She knew it would be hard, but she was determined to speak the uncalled words. And with that fortitude, she looked straight into his eyes and spoke in a soft, yet firm tone, “I cannot fall in love with a person who is responsible for my father’s disappearance."

The words pierced Benedikt’s heart like a lightning bolt. He lost his voice for a while before he managed to say, “Do you really believe that I am responsible for your father’s disappearance?”

“You, your organization everyone is responsible”, She blurted with a harsh tone and stormed away.

As she walked back, tears rolled down her cheek. She knew she had torn him to pieces, but she detested committing to a person until she found out the real reason behind her father’s desertion. Till then this was the only way to keep Benedikt at bay or maybe this was her pretentious belief.

**\**

**Chapter 73**

**4:00 PM 29th April 2014, Viciebsk, Belarus**

Benedikt found Sanchez more engaged in Viciebsk. The gloominess that stayed with her for past few days disappeared. Though she was far from being cheerful, it was enough for Benedikt to amicably coexist. She had requested him to take her to all the sites that he was planning to visit. Her aura was viciously mysterious and her temperament invariably impulsive, if at all he had a peek into her mind.

On the day of their arrival at Viciebsk, Benedikt was required to visit the warehouse. He insisted Sanchez to rest at the hotel but surrendered to her persistent demand.

They drove for an hour before jumping onto a dirt road, which led them to the warehouse. By the time they arrived, the place was enclosed in darkness. Though summer was only a few weeks away, it was cold enough to send the shiver through her spine. The calmness in the surroundings was deceiving the bustling environment that was prevalent inside the warehouse.

Benedikt got busy with the facilities head and it gave Sanchez the perfect time to trace Albert’s whereabouts. She spotted a person, who was overseeing the unloading of cargo. She approached him with a broad smile and with a sotto tone enquired, “Can you spare a minute for me?”

The person looked at her with a confused expression and moved away without giving any response. It took some time for Sanchez to realize that she had a language issue. In Belarus, they either spoke Mova or Russian, both she had no clue about.

“Do you know Albert? Albertttt,” she stressed on the word Albert.  It made no difference. The man smiled and walked away without answering.

The next couple of hours she tried hard to get people's attention, but no one understood her words. The hours went by and it was time to head back to the hotel. She left the warehouse, saddened and anxious. 

 

**Chapter 74**

**8:00 PM 29th April 2014, Viciebsk, Belarus**

Viciebsk stay was coming to a closure and Sanchez knew tomorrow might be her only chance to find Albert. The night was rough. She spent hours on the Internet learning the basics of the Russian language, enough words to help her converse with the workers.

They left for the warehouse around mid-morning. Benedikt had many questions for her but couldn’t muster enough courage to speak his mind. During their entire commute, she was either staring aimlessly in the vacuum or occasionally going through the notebook she was carrying.

In the daylight, she could clearly see the warehouse from a distance. The lone building stood aloof in the middle of the vast open field. Soon they were back inside the warehouse. Nothing had changed since they left. The place was still crowded with people, and the monotony of the hustle-bustle still existed. She waited for Benedikt to get busy, and once she was convinced, she went ahead with her plan. She looked around and saw an elderly looking man busy getting stock of some packages. She opened her notebook and spoke slowly in Russian reading out of the pad, enquiring whether he knew anything about Albert. The reaction was no different from what she had experienced before, the person ignored her. She tried imposing the same question to a few other people and couldn’t succeed in getting any answers from them. She was not making any progress with her approach and she decided to change her stance.

She approached the old man again. This time she mentioned that she had an important message for Albert, and it was from his friend in Gdynia, where he used to work before. The response didn't change. He simply walked away, ignoring everything she said. She tried it with a few other people. She either got a cold stare or no response. She was unsure whether it was because of her inability to talk their language, the unfriendliness of the locals, or fear of some unknown force that was inhibiting them to speak. Frustrated, she concluded to exercise her last resort, Benedikt. She wanted to avoid taking his help for many reasons, the prime one being her inability to trust him. But her time and options, both were running out and he was the only person left who could help. She looked around and spotted him few yards away, busy having some intense conversation with the locals. She waited for their discussion to cease and took the time to reassess her options. Her emotions were betraying her, as she pondered how she was so close yet to so far from Albert. How she wished she never had to approach Benedikt for help, and how she desired some miracle to happen in her favor.

She was hastily brought out of her brooding by a gentle tap on her shoulders. She turned around and found a fragile looking frame standing right in front of her. He handed her a piece of paper and went away. Sanchez hurriedly opened the folded chit and found something written in Russian. Though she couldn’t read it she knew it was an address. She was thrilled. She got hold of something concrete when she was least expecting.

It was noon and it was time to break for lunch. Sanchez had already scripted a plan to get out of the place. As she saw him approaching her, she leaned on the desk with her head hidden in her folded arms.

“Are you, all right?” Benedikt enquired.

She raised her face with great difficulty. Her face was exuding pain, and she spoke in a faint voice, “I am not. Can I take the car back to the hotel?”

“What happened? Is everything ok with you? We will leave right away.” Benedikt blurted in a hurried tone.

“Nothing serious. I am a little dizzy. You don’t have to come along. All I need is some rest.” she knew Benedikt would insist to come, and she tried hard to stop him, but failed.

On their way back to the hotel Benedikt insisted her to visit a doctor, but she refused and impeded the discussion by agreeing to visit if her condition further deteriorates. She rushed to her room with the pretext of taking rest and called for room service.

Soon a young man appeared at her door. She called him inside, handed him 500 Rubles and enquired about the address. The boy passed a wide grin and happily put the money in his pocket. He mentioned that the place was about thirty minutes away. She handed him some more money and instructed him to arrange a cab for her. She asked him to get the cab to the coffee shop next to the hotel after twenty minutes. He was a happy man. What he earns in a good week, he earned it in a day. He was more than willing to help and rushed to get the taxi arranged.

It was a long fifteen minutes wait before she stepped out of her room. She covered her face with a scarf and took the stairs. She waited by the stairway and peeked out to confirm no one she knew was around. Once convinced, she paced out of the hotel with utmost discreetness. Soon she was on her way to Albert’s place.

**Chapter 75**

**3:00 PM 30th April 2014, Viciebsk, Belarus**

There were rows of houses sparsely spread out in no defined order. All the homes were built in a similar pattern, but each one was still standing out because of the liberal use of random bright colors. Albert's dwelling was draped in turquoise blue. She kept staring at the house for a while before approaching it with weary steps. From a distance, the house appeared deserted with no sign of any soul. She dreaded the thought of not finding him and with trembling hands she tapped on the wooden door. After few knocks, a 6 feet frail looking structure opened the door. She opened her notebook and read in Russian enquiring if he was Albert.

Though he looked skeptical, he reluctantly confirmed. Sanchez’s hope took a leap. She handed him the letter from Finch and patiently waited for his reaction. He read the letter and instantly made space to let her come inside. He locked the door firmly, and to her surprise, he spoke in fine English. “He surely had picked his English from dad.” She thought.

“Are you Sanchez? He used to talk about you all the time.” his old eyes had an indispensable spark, which was hard to ignore.

“Do you know, where he is?” there was anxiety, fear and a blend of hope in those words.

“How did you find me?”

Either he was avoiding the question, or he was sincerely curious.

“I will give you all the details, but first can you please tell me where my father is?”

He passed a wry smiled and with a poised stature continued, “They took him away. And...” He hesitated and took a pause. "And what? Please tell me.”

“He is in Russia. I haven’t seen him for ages. They have confined him, and I believe the only reason he might be alive is because of the evidences he has against them.”

He took a pause and continued,” The evidences are his lifeline.”

“Why did you say might be?” the anxiety was visible in her shaky tone.

He looked in her eyes and with a straight face stated, “Because it still baffles me why they will keep him alive. Besides he was taken to a place where there are no rules.”

“Would you know where in Russia is, he?”

“I have heard these goons have a settlement in Smolensk. I cannot confirm for certain, but Smolensk is your best bet. That place is well guarded, and people are notorious. I wish I could have helped you, but unfortunately, I don’t know anyone there.”

She thanked him and wandered aimlessly back to her waiting taxi. The emotions were overwhelming her. She finally had some concrete evidence of her dad’s existence, something she had waited for a long time. She had no clue what to do next, but she was certain she would soon find a way to be in Smolensk.

 

**Chapter 76**

**6:00 PM 30th April 2014, Viciebsk, Belarus**

She got off the cab a block away from the hotel, solely to avoid anyone sighting her.  It was a cold evening. The unruly wind was spiraling down with utmost ruthlessness, and the brittleness was surging with great intensity through her spine. The street was sparsely populated, and a dense layer of mist was forging a stark gloominess that was hard to subdue. After a few minutes of brutal walking, she stepped into the hotel lobby with cautious steps. She looked around and was relieved to find no sign of Benedikt or his accomplices. With supreme urgency, she paced down the hallway towards the elevators. She was about to get into the elevator when Benedikt voice startled her. She turned around and found him standing right behind her. Her heart sank, and her feet went numb. For a moment, she felt as if she would crumble and fall. Despite the coldness, she sensed a trickle of sweat unceremoniously appearing on her temple refusing to diminish. How she wished what she witnessed was nothing but a bad dream.

She mustered some courage and raised her view to get a glimpse of his stature. His eyes were hollow, fixated firmly on his pale gloomy face. She had seen him in different shades before, but not the one he wore today. He appeared like a dethroned king, lost and dismayed, aloof and confused. An aura of betrayal reflected from every shade of his dejected presence.

Before she could react in any meaningful way, his voice echoed, “It doesn’t appear you are sick anymore” the shade of sarcasm was clearly visible in that distinct gruff tone.

She forced a smile and sheepishly claimed, “I was suffocated in the room, so I went for a walk.”

“Good, that must be a long stroll to Noŭka”

Sanchez was exposed, the entire charade she had put so far, folded like a deck of cards. She kept quiet, surrendering to the situation.

“We need to talk. Can you please follow me to my room?”, The tone though gentle was exuding annoyance.

She trailed him into the elevators with no sign of resistance. They settled in the living room. The void of words and the unusual quietness made the place appear ghost-like.

Benedikt finally broke the monotony and with an exhausted tone he exclaimed, "Why can’t you trust me?"

Those words refuted the scariness she was undermining with.

"I didn’t trust you? Despite all the odds, I believed in you. I came on this trip against my brother’s wish. I respected you, I admired you, and all this while I listened to my heart, ignoring everything that my mind ever mentioned otherwise.”

Sanchez was a shade below screaming. She didn’t know how, but she found the voice to defend her, tired of pretending that everything was fine between them.

“The thread that brought us together was my dad. No matter how much I try, I can’t forget the reaction you had when you saw his picture. I know for certain that all this while you knew everything, but you pretended otherwise. Let me ask you – why should I trust you?"

From being meek not too long ago, she transformed into a raging tigress. She was breathing heavily, and her cheeks were burning red fuming anger.

Benedikt looked squarely at her in aghast. He never expected her to retaliate with such bluntness. "I admit I hid few facts, but that was because I didn't want to lose you. There is nothing that I know that can help trace your father." His voice was timid and demeanor edgy.

He hesitantly looked at Sanchez to observe her reaction and quickly veered away to escape from her piercing gaze.

Unsure of what to do, he continued further, "I was there on the day your father was captivated but trust me I had nothing to do with his disappearance. That day I wasn’t even in charge of the operations. I was on a regular business trip."

The intensity with which Sanchez was looking at him made him restless and jejune. He gulped some water to get some respite. This time she was determined to know the truth, and he knew it.

Benedikt continued further, "That day when you showed me your father's picture my guilt overshadowed me."

"What was your guilt? Sanchez snapped.

"I... I told the Russians to get rid of him, but trust me, nothing untoward happened. They wanted to get hold of all pieces of evidence your father had collected and so decided to keep him. I swear to God I know nothing beyond this. I have no idea where they have taken him and what they have done with him.”

He paused to watch her expression, drained and exhausted from the monologue. She was unusually quiet. She kept staring at the vase on the table without blinking her eyes, still and unobtrusive, like lightning had struck her and bolted her on the chair. All Benedikt wanted was to conclude his part soon and bring a final closure to his prolonged misery.

“I didn’t hide facts to keep you away from your father. I didn’t want you to go away.”

She couldn’t take it anymore. She was suffocated in his presence and decided to leave.

“I need some time alone.” And she disappeared leaving Benedikt in a helpless stifled state.

**Chapter 77**

**10:00 PM 30th April 2014, Viciebsk, Belarus**

The night was long and restless. While Benedikt hopes were crashed, Sanchez was in a continuous battle with her mind reasoning his behavior. She was hurt, but unable to substantiate any reasons to support it. Her mind was clouded with agitation, frustration and unresolved queries. Part of her wanted to capitulate, but there was something in her that was looking for vengeance. The conundrum kept her awake the entire night and before she realized it was morning. She decided to go for a run. “Maybe the morning freshness could help me get a clearer vision.” She concluded.

The morning coldness opened all her senses. There was a newfound zeal, a whim that was irresistibly infectious. She was like a free bird, and at that moment all her nightlong apprehensions vanished.   This time she had no competing thoughts, and no doubts holding her back. Everything appeared clear like the early morning brightness, distinct and visible.

After a mile long run she looped back to the hotel and walked straight to his room. On the third knock he responded, and when he saw her through the peephole, he was startled. He opened the door in haste. The unkempt hair, the disheveled look, and his confused stature made him appear dangerously attractive. After some struggle, she found her lost voice, “I hope I didn’t catch you at a bad time. Do you want me to come back?”

“No, you are fine. Have a seat I will be with you in a minute” and he vanished behind the closed doors. He was back in a jiffy. He was wearing a crumpled shirt, his hair was partly combed and his appearance disheveled. Hastiness was written all over his effort. But despite all madness, she could see the sparkle of eagerness in his wide blue eyes. She tried hard to take her eyes off his rugged looks but failed.  Frustrated, she detested her sordid pretense and blurted, “I am tired of pretending to be a different person with you. I want to trust you one last time.”

Benedikt was at loss for words. He interrupted the awkward silence and with his deep sotto voice, “What you told me now means the world to me. You won’t be disappointed.” There was elation, an unprecedented joy in his colloquial tone.

Sanchez continued further,” I have a fair idea where my father is and I want your help.”

“Where did you find him? Who told you? Is this real? Are you sure?” his barrage of questions seemed never-ending.

His inquisitiveness and childlike enthusiasm made Sanchez smile. And at that moment, she knew she hadn’t made a wrong decision.

“I will tell you the everything, but I am famished. Can we order breakfast first?” she reflected a sense of possessiveness, melting away the discomfort they had between them for the past several days.

**\**

**Chapter 78**

**10:00 AM 1st May 2014, Smolensk, Russia**

The name Smolensk was not alien to Benedkit, for it had appeared few times in his past business conversations. The setup at Smolensk was not under the control of TransPacific, and no occasion had presented in the past that could have taken him there. The place was the East European operations center for their Russian counterpart and was beyond his span of control. The place was strategically located at the border of Belarus, making it easy for the Russians to operate their dealings within the European subcontinent. It made perfect sense. Keeping Sanchez’s father in Russia would keep TransPacific out of any unwanted troubles.

His plan to visit Smolensk came as a surprise to his seniors. He presented a detailed plan on expanding business with the Russians, but despite a well-thought strategy, he got a firm pushback.  In the past, he would have bulldozed his way in, but this time the situation demanded him to exhibit a different demeanor. He ensured not look too persuasive for he never wanted anyone to cast any doubt on him. And because of his overcautious approach, it took him a good two weeks to convince his higher-ups.

The plan was all set. The Russians were briefed, and they were expecting him in their Smolensk location.  At first, Benedikt wanted to go alone, but Sanchez insistent inexorable deportment dissolved all his reasonings.  He enjoined her not to display any emotions if she happened to see her father, to a point of annoyance. He instructed Sanchez to have Stephen wait at the border. It was a fallback plan. In case the Russians come after him, he could let Daniel and Sanchez escape with Stephen. The plan was crafted with the hope that he succeeds to make Daniel escape.

It was a cold spring morning. The drive to Smolensk was little less than an hour. The place was located a few miles away from the highway in a deserted place. The tall trees and the wilds in the surrounding were concealing what looked like a long-standing bastion. They were stopped at the entrance and were let in after their car got thoroughly checked by an armored guard. The place from inside was nothing charming. There was one large building, which looked like a monolithic dome. A person named Anatoly received them at the driveway. Anatoly was the head of operations for the Smolensk facility. He was a stout well-built man and appeared to be in the late forties. The wide grin on his face looked permanent and his blue eyes had a subtle shadow of shrewdness.

They were taken to a large office and were joined by two more of his accomplices. All the discussions were in Russian and Sanchez could hardly contribute to it. Though she was carrying a pleasant appearance, there was a shadow of uncertainty in her eyes, which was hard to conceal. She knew they had landed without any plan and soon they had to figure out one, and unfortunately, the time was not their best friend. The place looked uninhabited. Besides the guards, the valet parking person, few helpers, and the three officers there was hardly anyone around.

A quick tap on her hand took her out of her brooding. He tilted his head so that he can be close to her ears and whispered, “They are going to show me the place. I want you to wait here.” He wore a phony smile in disguise.

With a reluctant heart, she forced a smile confirming her agreement, and he left. It was an intolerable wait. She did everything she could do to divert her attention, walked around, played with her phone, scribbled on paper but nothing helped. In about thirty minutes Benedikt returned and it was time to leave the place.

Sanchez curiosity had gone from bad to worse. She tried imposing her requests in many ways to Benedikt, but each time he expressed in monosyllables that he would tell her later. The valet person brought the car to the driveway. They bid their host goodbye and soon they were out of the bastion on their way to Belarus.

Before she could open her barrage of questions, Benedikt spoke in a firm tone, “I saw your father and I have a plan for his escape.”

**Chapter 79**

**3:00 PM 1st May 2014, Viciebsk, Belarus**

Sanchez's father was not in any confinement. During his conversation with Anatoly, he mentioned how the law enforcement in Europe was increasingly becoming draconian and how the forces were getting more united amongst the countries. He emphasized the likelihood of informers blending with their tribe. And with that pretext, he desired to use Smolensk as a makeshift hideout to interrogate some of the suspects. Anatoly was happy to accommodate his request. He mentioned about a person they got a while back and had still in their internment.

Sanchez was overwhelmed. Her emotions had no boundaries. The fact her father was alive dissolved all trepidations she had garnered over years. How she wished she could get a glimpse of him, talk to him and tell him how much she misses him. She held on her emotions with absolute resolute and succeeded from not breaking down.

Benedikt saw her struggling to hold on to her schmaltz and he swiftly changed the topic, “Do you want to know what my plan is?”

She was still looking away from him and managed only a nod, dreading to choke if she chose to talk.

He explained the plan to her. Sanchez was unsure how it would turn out but remained inscrutable for she had nothing better to offer. It was going to be a long night and she hoped for a better tomorrow.

 

**Chapter 80**

**10:00 AM 1st May 2014, Smolensk, Russia**

The next day Benedikt arrived at Smolensk early in the morning.  He tried every possible way to convince Sanchez not to come, but his efforts were trifled down. Anatoly was waiting at the lobby to receive them. There was no change in his stance. He exuded the same warmth as he displayed the day before. After exchanging pleasantries, Benedikt initiated the conversation, “We are excited about the possible partnership, and we are eager to take this forward. However, we need to resolve a trivial matter.”

Benedikt exercised extreme caution in choosing his words and took a brief pause to watch Anatoly’s reaction.

“What is your concern?”, asked Anatoly.

"Nothing significant. It is about Daniel." Benedikt paused giving Anatoly ample time to consume what he was about to divulge.

"What about him?" Anatoly snapped. He didn't seem pleased.  The forced smile disappeared for a flash second, but soon the gleam was back on his countenance.

"While giving the report to my seniors I mentioned about Daniel. It seemed there was some miscommunication between us. According to Mr. Slender, Daniel should be long gone. They see him as a threat. We need to resolve this before we can go any further on our business expansion plans."

This time his grin faded permanently, and he assumed a more puzzled stature. After a long pause, he responded, "He had been confined here for a few years now. He is not a danger to anyone. Why would your seniors be worried about him?"

Benedikt never expected such strong support for Daniel. Though he was unsure about the outcome he still went ahead with his plan, “I believe your words, but as you must appreciate I have to answer my seniors. Here is a suggestion I have. Why don’t I take him along, with the pretext of releasing him? My man will secretly follow him to see where he goes and what he does. If in the next one month he doesn’t do anything alarming, the man is yours. You can keep him or let him go, your choice.”

Anatoly looked uncomfortable. After some brooding he concluded, “I can't do this without my seniors’ approval. I need to talk to them once.”

This was what Benedikt wanted to avoid. If Anatoly’s executives chose to discuss this with his seniors the charade, he had put so far would fall apart. Besides, he would need to do a lot of explanations, but he had no option but to agree.

“By all means.” He smiled to appear genuine.

Anatoly excused himself and went out to make few calls. He returned after fifteen minutes wearing his signature broad grin, “You are a good business partner and we have a promising future together. My seniors have agreed. You can come tomorrow morning and take him along.”

Benedikt couldn’t understand why he was asked to come tomorrow. “I would prefer to take him today. I hope it is not an imperious request to make?” he forced a smile to hide his bafflement.

“I would have loved to, but Mr. Kosovo is not here until late in the evening. He wants to meet Daniel once before you take him away.” His smile appeared more like Russian Mores, and his jauntiness gouged Benedikt.

Benedikt concealed his frustration and like any other professional dealing he concluded by stating, “Sure, I will see you tomorrow.”

There was something in Anatoly's voice that appeared unconvincing. The way his eyes rolled on him, it failed to abstain his wide grin. Perplexed he drove the car battling with his competing thoughts.

It took a shade short of screaming for Sanchez to pull him out of his deep thoughts. In an ambivalent state, he looked at her, still trying to comprehend the reason behind her escalated tone.

“Will you tell me what transpired inside?”, hysteria consumed her being, as she blazoned the words with an asphyxiated tone.

He was unsure how to respond. He was certain the Russians were putting a coy.

“On the face, it looks exciting. And I want to believe it is real. But it is not”, he was gazing the lone road and said the words without blinking his eyes.

Sanchez couldn’t keep up with the incessant behavior Benedikt was demonstrating. In a rage, she blurted “For Christ’s sake answer me. I can’t put up anymore with your runic spell.” Her hands covered her face and her breath was gravelly abnormal.

Benedikt realized his rumination was putting her into a miserable state. He looked at her with a sense of fortitude and continued, “I apologize, I was too busy deciphering Anatoly’s behavior. As per the plan, I asked him to let your father come with us. I was not surprised when he said he wants to check with his seniors. What confused me was the follow up response.”

The conversation with Anatoly was in Russian, a language Sanchez had no command over. Her palpations were dissolved. She appeared more rational as she wore an inquisitive look to learn more.

After a short pause, he continued, “He asked me to come tomorrow to get him. Why not today? why is it important for Kosovo to meet him?”

“Are you saying they have agreed?”, Sanchez blurted with a sense of urgency.

“You are not understanding. It can’t be that easy? Don’t you see the ruse?” The paranoia in his voice was hard to conceal.

“Maybe you are doubting unnecessarily. He may have meant what he said”, there was hope, a sense of belief in her voice.

Benedikt realized no matter how copious it looked she didn’t want to gutter it down.  For a moment, the glee on her face convinced him to go by her intuition, but soon he dejected the thought. He didn’t want to subscribe to a false design and with some reluctance, he continued, “I want to believe you, but I know them well. I am sure there is something behind this arrangement. Something he was hiding behind that grin.”

“So, what are you suggesting? Are you not going to come back tomorrow?” her face was like a shroud as if someone had wrung her soul.

Benedikt took his gaze away from the vacuum and looked at her with intense eyes and said, “I will certainly come back. All I want is to be cautious. The last thing I want is to put a drab on your years of strenuous efforts.”

The anxiety receded from her face and she chose not to speak for the rest of the journey.

**Chapter 81**

**12:00 PM 1st May 2014, Smolensk, Russia**

Liozna was a small town at the border of Belarus and Russia. Stephen was waiting with anxious laden eyes to accost the moment. He wasn't expecting Sanchez until half-past one, yet his perturbations were beyond limits.

“Stephen must be waiting for us. Do you want to call him? Maybe you can ask him to leave?" Benedikt tried striking a conversation after some momentary lull.

Sanchez was lost in an unprecedented gloom. She didn't respond but acted on the request. It didn't take too long for Stephen to comprehend the situation and his face assumed shades of burned char.  The dwindling hope of meeting his father came crashing down. Maxwell was standing next to him. He sensed something wasn't right.

“Is Sanchez fine?” Maxwell's queried with a trembling voice. 

With a grim outlook and frail voice, he responded, “They couldn't get dad out. We have to leave this place.”

Maxwell got himself invited in an unceremonious way. Ever since Sanchez had decided to board the ship, he had kept Stephen's company, like a hawk guarding its prey.  When he learned about Benedikt's plan to free Daniel from the clutches of the Russian mafias he was startled. Though he didn't believe the proceedings, he refrained from expressing his views. He wanted to be at Liozna to witness it first-hand. Stephen was too naive to understand his intention and gave in to his persuasive demand. He had all the evidence against him and to ensure he gets Benedikt this time, he planned his arrest.  With utmost discreetness he deployed his team, all hiding in tall bushes by the side of the road. But, his ulterior ploy got foiled with the call from Sanchez. 

“We should still wait for her”, Maxwell didn’t sound convincing as he hastily put his thought forward.

Stephen looked at him with a confused look. "But Sanchez asked us to leave. It is not happening today?”

“I can’t trust Benedikt. What if she is in danger? What if she needs help but couldn’t speak out?” Maxwell tried to put some reasoning behind his frail words.

Stephen didn’t put any further resistance and passively submitted to his demand. Both waited, counting every passing minute in absolute desirousness. The time had halted for them.

**Chapter 82**

**10:00 AM 1st May 2014, Smolensk, Russia**

Something didn’t appear right. Though Benedikt countenance reflected calmness, Anatoly knew it was a disguise.  He ensured not to display even a trickle of a doubt on his visage and excused himself from the room. He headed straight to Kosovo’s office. Kosovo was his boss and head of the Smolensk’s operations.

Kosovo was on a call when Anatoly entered. He ignored his presence and continued with his call.  The room’s surreal setup appeared intimidating, but it didn't bother Anatoly. He was in this room many times before and was used to the decor and the protocol. Kosovo continued for a while and when he was finally done he gazed towards Anatoly. It was an indication to start the conversation. Kosovo tapered green eyes became further narrower as Anatoly provided a detailed narration about his conversation with Benedikt.

"I sense some foul play. He is up to something. I need to call Evan." Kosovo declared, and without much ado, he went ahead to make the call.

He called a few times but there was no response.

"Ask him to come tomorrow. Come up with some excuse. Make sure you appear genuine. I need to talk to TransPacific on this. Daniel is a closed chapter and I have no intention to revisit his case." Kosovo had a distinct way to communicate. When he spoke, others arrested their opinions.

Anatoly left the room. He had clear direction from his senior, and that's what mattered.

**Chapter 83**

**12:00 PM 1st May 2014, Somlensk, Russia**

The road was mostly deserted except for some occasional passing vehicle. This time Maxwell was determined to arrest Benedikt. He had made all the arrangements to capture him provided he shows up.  But somewhere down within him, he couldn't believe Benedikt would come. It was unlike Benedikt to act in such imprudent manner. The Benedikt he knew was fierce, vicious and ruthless. He doubted he would come, but he wanted to believe otherwise.

While he was busy debating with his competing thoughts, a blurry image of a car appeared not too far away from where he was hiding. It was a beamer, the type of car Stephen mentioned Benedikt would be driving. All his senses came to life as he observed the car with vigilant eyes. The summer sun formed a mirage on the long-stressed road and the car appeared as if it was floating on a body of water. Maxwell stayed behind the bushes on the side of the pavement, observant and ready to surge.

Stephen, unaware of his companion's ulterior motives waved at the car directing it to stop. The car came to a halt and a puzzled-looking Sanchez rolled down the window.

"Didn't I ask you to leave?" Her voice reflected more surprise than frustration.

"We want to ensure you are safe?" Stephen blabbered without much conviction.

"Who else is with you?" This time it was Benedikt. Stephen uneasiness made him curious. Besides, he said "we" instead of "me." Benedikt’s eyes were searching the surroundings as he waited for Stephen to respond.

Before Stephen could speak, the sound of loading guns took over the tranquility of the deserted setting. Six armed men approached them, three from each side of the car and Maxwell from the front. Maxwell wore a cunning smirk as if he had won the battle of some celestial titans.

Sanchez was trapped in a quagmire, unsure how to react. On one side, she had her brother whom she trusted with her life, betraying her belief and on the other hand, she had Benedikt, who despite all odds had gone out of his way to help her. All she could comprehend at that moment was Benedikt being in danger. Everything was happening too fast too quick. She gave a hard stare at Stephen. Her eyes reflected signs of agony and frustration. Time was running out and she had to do something quickly if she had to save Benedikt. And then a thought crossed her mind. She turned towards Benedikt. But before she could say any words his pensive look froze her. A faint smile graced his pale lips and the guise of getting betrayed loomed his visage. He softly rolled his tongue and stated with a frail voice, “I will always love you, no matter what happens."

She took her pistol out of her bag, the one that Benedikt gave her before leaving for Smolensk and whispered with urgency. “Make me your hostage”

Before he could react, Sanchez uttered again, this time with agitation, “You don’t have time. Can you hurry up?"

A confused Benedikt swung his arms around her neck. Draped in some stupor he pointed the gun at her temple and screamed with a shrill voice, “put your guns down or else I will shoot her."

Maxwell was flabbergasted. He couldn’t believe the table turning against him. Sanchez was too precious for him to do anything stupid. He screamed, “You are making a mistake. Put your gun down, and I promise I will request the authorities to show leniency."

“You don’t get it, do you? Another step I will shoot her.” Benedikt tone was dead and firm.

Maxwell knew he meant business. As he was contemplating, the hysterical screaming of Sanchez shook his being. With a trembling tone, Sanchez bellowed, “Do as he says. His men had shot me once and I have no doubt he will shoot me again.”

“Alright. I will do as you say”, and an aghast Maxwell instructed his men to step back.

“Drop your guns, turn back and walk a hundred steps away”, Benedikt demanded.

Maxwell instructed his men to follow the instructions.

“Stephen, get all the guns and put it in the car trunk,” commanded Benedikt. A startled and ashamed Stephen followed the course. 

Benedikt reversed the car with urgency, the tires made a screeching sound as he drove with full force towards the direction he came from. He knew once he is in Russia Maxwell could do no harm.

Maxwell tried hard to chase him but failed. Maxwell looked at the vanishing car with horror, but there was nothing he could do to change things. He was worried, perspired and perplexed. All he had was a broken heart, smeared thoughts, and a hope that she would be safe.

**Chapter 84**

**12:30 PM 1st May 2014, Somlensk, Russia**

Benedikt's car was away from the clutches of Maxwell far into the Russian side of the border. Though the immediate perilous situation was behind him, his plan was in complete shambles. But despite all the hullaballoo and the commotion he went through, Benedikt appeared ecstatic. The ruse Sanchez played on Maxwell was glued to his vision and he still couldn't get enough of it. He wanted to say so many things, but words defied him.

"Thank you for your help.” In the end, those are the only words he could manage to speak.

“Why do you love me so much? All I have given you is pain and trouble.” She gently squeezed his hand and continued, "You risked your life, your job, just for me. And all I have offered you is despair." Her voice was deep and had a distinct shade of empathy.

Benedikt was shocked. He had never witnessed Sanchez opening up before.  His mind was battling hard to find the right syllables when his phone created a sudden dissonance. He glanced at the phone with a disdained look, but the name flashing on the screen needed his attention. He asked Sanchez to be quiet and answered the phone with utmost disinterest.

"You are creating more problems than answers. Why did you volunteer to take Daniel out from Smolensk? Worse, you took my name? Can you explain what is going on?"  The voice on the other end reflected annoyance.

At other times Benedikt would have fought back with equal vigor. But today was different. He parked the car on the side of the road and with a composed tone responded, “It was impromptu. I was there when Daniel was captured, and I know how much of risk he is to our business.”

A harsh tone interrupted him, “It happened a long time ago. When we trusted our partners to take care of the matter, it makes no sense to intervene again. You have an important assignment to finish and you should focus on that instead of nosing around on trivial matters.”

This time the voice was further elevated, but that didn’t deter Benedikt to put forward his point, “If we would have killed him at that time we wouldn’t be having this discussion now. We don’t know what secrets he knows, and what evidences he may have hidden somewhere. We are sitting on a ticking time bomb. One day it will explode and then it will be too late.”

The person on the other side of the phone tried to intervene, but this time Benedikt didn’t let him speak, “I have a plan. With the pretext of letting him go, I will follow him. If he turns rogue I will get him killed. If not, will do as you suggest. Why is it so difficult for you to understand, the exposure we have with Daniel cannot be taken lightly?”

It was making sense to the person on the other side of the phone. With a skeptical outlook he continued, “You have created a lot of unrest, make sure you handle this one with perfection.” and the line went silent.

Sanchez heard the entire conversation. Before Benedikt could put forward any rationale argument she said, "you don't have to explain. I know why you said what you said."  She wore a pensive smile and with a dejected outlook, she took her gaze away from him.

**Chapter 85**

**8:00 AM 2nd May 2014, Somlensk, Russia**

It was a bright morning. The breeze was gentle, and mercury was at its optimum best. This time Benedikt decided to go alone. The way the things were turning, he was unsure what to expect. Unlike last time, Sanchez was reasonable and understood the rationale behind. She was waiting for him a few miles away from the bastion, around the Liozona Border.

Anatoly was at his deceitful best. He met Benedikt with the same verve as he displayed the day before. Since Benedikt's departure lot had happened. Evan returned Kosovo's call. He was appalled to learn about Benedikt's demand. Kosovo expressed his discomfort and stated he was not interested to let Daniel go.

After a few hours, Evan called him back and tried convincing him to let Benedikt take Daniel with him. He explained the reasoning behind their demand and assured to closely monitor Benedikt. And promised if found guilty, he would not be spared. Kosovo though not convinced still agreed, for he couldn't have said no to TransPacific. After the conversation, he called upon Anatoly and asked his men to discretely follow Benedikt. He wanted a second pair of eyes from his clan to ensure there was nothing dubious planned.

Daniel was brought to the room.  He looked frail like before and wore a confused look. And to Benedikt’s disbelief, he left the place with Daniel unscathed.  He had his share of doubts in his mind, for it went too smooth to appear real. He was watchful and for now all that mattered was Daniel’s safety.

Sanchez annoyance on her brother had not receded. She couldn’t believe Stephen would hide such a thing from him. She was so furious she refused to call Stephen to plan for Daniel's safe passage until Benedikt insisted. He knew if he escapes from the Russians, he would be followed, and the only way he could secure Sanchez’s security was with Maxwell's help. The plan was the same as before, but with one change. Sanchez would walk across the border along with Daniel. Once he would secure their safety, he would think about his escape plan.

Sanchez spotted the car from a distance. She was numbed as if her feet were frozen to the ground, evident from the fact that despite the strong urge she failed to run towards the car to check her father's presence.

Benedikt’s car came to a complete halt, and Sanchez saw her father behind the car doors. He looked wearily thin. The clothes he was wearing were larger than his frame. His face was covered with a white beard and his hair appeared disarrayed. He was thinned down, and his demeanor was a shade below pale. One thing that didn’t fail was his smile that was glued to her memories for all these years since he disappeared. This time she leaped forward with all might and hugged him tightly. Tears were flowing in abundance for all the right reasons. There were no words. All that was visible was teary eyes and smiling faces.

“Dad, you are free. We will all live together like we used to before.” The words found its way out as she spoke with a trembling tone.

Her dad could manage only a few words through his choking voice, “Thank you. I never thought I would ever see you again.”

She got in the car and started their journey towards Liozona. Benedikt vigilance was at the extreme.  His prudent eyes spared none. And without any untoward incident, they reached their destination.

“You should go now.” Benedikt was curt and had no sign of parting remorse as he spoke for the first time since Sanchez got in the car.

She put a deaf ear to his instruction and instead spoke to her father, “Dad, Stephen is waiting for you. Maxwell has arranged all the necessary protection. Soon TransPacific will be exposed and we will be all together."

She took a deep breath and continued, "You need to walk across the street alone. I can't come now as I need to take care of a few things."

His countenance displayed a worrisome outlook, and with a disconcerted tone, he said, “I am sure you have a reason. Don’t make me wait for long this time.”

The time halted and they hugged for long. They parted with reluctance and slowly Daniel walked away, disappearing along the road as it bent away from her vision. She leaped forward and from a distance saw the faint image of Stephen. Once convinced about his safety, she dashed back to the car and took the seat next to him.

Benedikt was frozen as if lightning had struck him. He kept staring at her with astonishment.

She had a wry smile as she spoke, "After what you did to the Russians they wouldn’t spare you. I am not worried about TransPacific, within the next 24 hours it will be busted, but the Russians would be at loose.”

Benedikt had a puzzled look as Sanchez continued further, “I had collected enough evidence against them and I am sure my father should have a few more. Maxwell will have it all. Let us get out of this place before the Russians come looking for us."

"Us?" Benedikt was still in a shocked state.

“Yes us. Provided you are willing to tolerate this abstruse person.” she had that mischievous smile on her face, which he was quite aware of.

Benedikt’s joy had no boundaries, “Yes and for a lifetime.” He cried with an undisputed ecstasy in that elated tone.

“When you were not around your illusions comforted me and now that you are here my endearment for you is beyond any comprehension." Benedikt expressed without any inhabitations. He was hopeful the tides would turn and soon she would accept his love. The scattered clouds slowly engulfed the sun forming floating shadows over the vast valley of wildness. They had a few hurdles to cross, some barriers to dodge and many places to visit before they could repose.
